### FR-NODE-096 — six rules-document statements are corrected to the runtime they describe

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.5.1 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | rules, documentation, fidelity |
| Risk | low |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

Six statements in the bundled rules document that describe behaviour the runtime does not have SHALL be corrected to describe what the runtime does. The status transition table and the `implemented` conditions SHALL be marked as an authoring convention the tool does not gate, naming the status gates the tool does enforce. The governing-rules-version section SHALL state that the tooling reads the `Rules` row version only to keep the row aligned with the installed document and that no runtime behaviour switches on it, because a release bundles exactly one rules version. The atomicity guarantee SHALL be scoped to the three changes inside the requirement's own file and SHALL name the index rollup as a separate follow-up write outside that transaction. The index Required Structure SHALL include the `Rules` row and name the command that writes it for a new project, the command that inserts it into an index that lacks it, and the check that reports its absence. The mutation-kind classification SHALL record that the compatibility-check tools act on a requirement pair and take `aReqId` and `bReqId` rather than `id`. The Completed Work Log section SHALL document that a legacy `docs/spec/05.completed-work.md` takes precedence over the history file when it exists.

#### Rationale

A shipped rules document is a contract that agents and authors follow literally, so a statement it makes that the runtime does not honour is a defect in its own right rather than a documentation nicety. Each of these six was found by a fidelity pass and left open because it was a pre-existing runtime gap; correcting the statement is the honest resolution where implementing the described gate would be a behaviour change with a blast radius the target does not cover.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: The status transition table and the `implemented` conditions are stated as an authoring convention that no tool call enforces, and the section names the status gates the tool does enforce, so a reader cannot conclude that a disallowed transition is refused.
- [x] AC-2: The governing-rules-version section states that the version token is read to keep the `Rules` row aligned with the installed document and that no runtime behaviour selects rules by it.
- [x] AC-3: The tool-compliance section scopes the atomic write to the three changes in the requirement's own file and names the index rollup as a separate follow-up write that is not part of that transaction.
- [x] AC-4: The index Required Structure template contains the `Rules` metadata row, and the section names `speckiwi init`, `speckiwi upgrade` and `speckiwi doctor` for writing, inserting and reporting it.
- [x] AC-5: The mutation-kind classification records that the three compatibility-check tools take a requirement pair as `aReqId` and `bReqId` rather than a single `id`, so the `req-scoped` kind's `id` rule is not read as covering them.
- [x] AC-6: The Completed Work Log section documents that a legacy `docs/spec/05.completed-work.md` receives the appended row in preference to the history file when that file exists.

| VE-2 | test | test/core/mutation/status-transition-gates.fr-node-096.test.ts | AC-1 | 7 cases pinning AC-1 against the runtime rather than the document's own words. Added because the audit found the first correction of 14.2 was itself false - it claimed update_status writes whatever status it is given, and the runtime refuses planned to verified with MUTATION_DENIED. A document-string assertion cannot catch that class. |
| VE-3 | review | docs/next/2026-07-30.numbering-and-2.5.0-residuals.md | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6 | Independent audit 2026-07-30 verified each corrected statement against the code: the 30.5 version gate is genuinely dead code with no production caller, the requirement-file write is a real temp-file-and-rename while the index rollup is a separate second write, all three commands do what 7.1 now claims, the compatibility tools take aReqId and bReqId, and the legacy 05.completed-work.md really takes precedence. One CRITICAL and three MEDIUM findings on 14.2 and 14.3 were fixed and re-checked. |

### FR-NODE-097 — scope allocation skips a number another document already holds

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.5.1 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | numbering, scaffold |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

Scope document allocation SHALL skip a candidate number that any Markdown document directly under `docs/spec` already holds, so a newly scaffolded scope document never lands on a number another document occupies. The candidate SHALL remain one above the highest number among the project's scope SRS documents; only a candidate that collides SHALL advance, and it SHALL advance to the lowest number above it that no document holds. A sidecar SHALL NOT raise the starting candidate, so a project whose only scope document is `01` still receives `02`.

#### Rationale

Allocation and the collision diagnostic have to agree, otherwise the tool's own scaffold command produces a document that its own validation immediately warns about. A project whose highest scope document is `04` beside the legacy `05.completed-work.md` is exactly that case. Skipping only on collision preserves the recorded decision that sidecars do not drive allocation: counting `90.appendix.md` as the highest would push a project's second scope document to `91`, which is the outcome that decision exists to prevent.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A project whose highest scope SRS document is `04` and which also holds `docs/spec/05.completed-work.md` receives `06` for its next scope document, and the created file is at that number.
- [x] AC-2: A project whose scope documents leave the candidate number free receives that candidate unchanged, so allocation is not shifted by the presence of unrelated sidecars.
- [x] AC-3: A project whose only scope document is `01` receives `02` even though `90.appendix.md` and `91.completed-work-log.md` are present, so a sidecar does not raise the starting candidate.
- [x] AC-4: When the candidate and several numbers above it are all occupied, allocation advances to the lowest free number rather than to one above the highest occupied number.
- [x] AC-5: A document scaffolded through allocation produces no `SRS-W070` and no `SRS-W072` finding, so the scaffold and the collision diagnostics agree.
- [x] AC-6: `speckiwi init` allocates its default scope document through the same rule, so a project that carries its own `01` document receives `02` and the index registers it under the number it received; a project with no numbered document of its own still receives `01`.
- [x] AC-7: Every caller that names a scope document goes through the allocator, so no call site carries a fixed number of its own.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/mutation/scope-allocation-collision-skip.fr-node-097.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5 | 6 cases, all green. Audit 2026-07-30 probed each AC - deleting the skip loop, inverting it, letting a sidecar raise the candidate, dropping the second argument at the call site - and every probe failed the cited test. AC-3 was additionally confirmed end to end through the CLI. |
| VE-2 | test | test/core/bootstrap/init-scope-number-collision.fr-node-097.test.ts | AC-6, AC-7 | 6 cases, all green. Added for the HIGH the audit found: init hardcoded the number 1 and was the one caller that never went through the allocator, so a project holding its own 01 document got a colliding 01 scope document that the validator immediately reported as SRS-W072. Red before the fix: 4 of 5 failed. The AC-7 case greps every scopeDocumentName call site for a literal number and caught a second hardcoded site in renderIndexTemplate the audit had not reached. |

### FR-NODE-098 — Target registration accepts every documented target type

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.5.2-phase1-target-lifecycle |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | low |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/kiwi-orchestrator/00.charter.md |

#### Requirement

When a target is registered, SpecKiwi SHALL accept every target type the shipped authoring rules document defines in its Target type values table — version, release, milestone, phase, objective and experiment — and SHALL reject only a value outside that set. The accepted set SHALL be stated once in the implementation so that the Node core, the CLI surface and the MCP tool schema cannot come to disagree.

#### Rationale

The rules document ships inside the npm package and its Target type values table lists six types, while three enforcement points accept only three. Measured by execution: --type phase, --type objective and --type experiment are each refused with "target type must be version, release, or milestone", and only --type milestone succeeds. Nothing in the codebase reads the Type column — no validator, query or diagnostic branches on it — so a hand-written Target Map row carrying phase already validates clean. The tool refuses to write a value it already accepts to read. No test asserts the rejection, so the divergence was never pinned. Every project consuming SpecKiwi reads the six-value table and meets the three-value runtime, which is why this blocks phase-named targets generally rather than only this repository's.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Registering a target with each of the six types the rules document lists succeeds and writes that type into the Target Map row.
- [x] AC-2: Registering a target with a type outside the documented set fails with a USAGE error whose message names the accepted values.
- [x] AC-3: The MCP set_active_target tool schema accepts all six documented types, so a phase-typed registration is not rejected before it reaches the core.
- [x] AC-4: The CLI --type help text and the appendix command signature name the same set as the rules document, verified by comparing them rather than by restating either.
- [x] AC-5: A test derives the expected set from the rules document's Target type values table, so widening or narrowing that table fails the test rather than silently diverging from the runtime.

### FR-NODE-099 — A departing active target keeps an honest status

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.5.2-phase1-target-lifecycle |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | low |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

When the Active Target moves to another target, SpecKiwi SHALL derive the departing target status from that target own requirements rather than fixing it to planned. When the departing target has at least one requirement and every one of them is verified, its Target Map status SHALL become completed. Otherwise it SHALL remain planned, which is the pre-existing behaviour for a target whose work is unfinished.

#### Rationale

The rules document defines completed as the requirements of that target are complete and released as an immutable baseline target that passed release-readiness, and rule 6 says which to use. No code writes either. set-active-target demotes a departing active target to planned, which the same document defines as not started yet. This repository already carries eleven targets in that false state, including v2.4.0 with thirty-one verified requirements and a shipped git tag, and v2.5.1 with seventeen verified requirements, demoted by this very session. The record moves only in the direction that loses information.
- [2026-08-01] Correction, 2026-08-01. Two figures above are wrong. v2.4.0 carries twenty-eight requirements, of which twenty-seven are verified and one is implemented — not thirty-one verified; the figure came from a stale note rather than from the tool. And twelve targets read planned at the time, not eleven. The direction of the argument is unaffected: v2.4.0 is tagged and shipped and the map still called it not started yet. Measured with speckiwi summary against the staged tree.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Moving the Active Target away from a target whose requirements are all verified writes completed into that target Target Map row, not planned.
- [x] AC-2: Moving the Active Target away from a target with at least one unverified requirement leaves it planned.
- [x] AC-3: A departing target that carries no requirements at all is left planned, because nothing supports calling it complete.
- [x] AC-4: The statuses the tool writes are drawn from the status vocabulary the shipped authoring rules document defines, and a test derives that vocabulary from the document rather than restating it.

### FR-NODE-100 — A target status can be set explicitly

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.5.2-phase1-target-lifecycle |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | low |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi SHALL provide a guarded mutation that sets one Target Map row status to a status the shipped authoring rules document defines, so that a finished target can be recorded as completed or released and a row already carrying a wrong status can be repaired. The mutation SHALL refuse a status outside the documented vocabulary, SHALL refuse to create a second active row, SHALL refuse to move the row named by Active Target away from active because it does not update the Active Target metadata row, and SHALL leave every other row and column untouched.

#### Rationale

FR-NODE-099 makes a departing target status honest from now on, but it cannot repair the eleven rows this repository already carries in a false state, and it cannot ever produce released, which records a human release decision the tool has no way to derive. Without an explicit setter the Target Map can only lose information. The mutation is deliberately CLI-only, following the precedent set by upgrade, because a release decision is author-owned rather than agent-owned.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Setting a registered target status to completed or released rewrites only that row status cell and leaves its Target, Type and Description cells byte-identical.
- [x] AC-2: A status outside the vocabulary the rules document defines is refused with a USAGE error naming the accepted values, and nothing is written.
- [x] AC-3: Setting a status to active is refused while another row is already active, because the rules cap the map at one active row.
- [x] AC-4: Setting the row named by Active Target to any non-active status is refused, naming set-active-target in the message, because the rules require that row to be active and this mutation does not update the Active Target metadata row.
- [x] AC-5: An unregistered target is refused with NOT_FOUND and nothing is written.
- [x] AC-6: The mutation takes the SRS mutation lock and honours dry-run, writing nothing and reporting the operations it would perform.

### FR-NODE-101 — Handoff pinning harvest verified without trusted-git under an explicit git cwd

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md), [deferred multiroot audit](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/06.deferred-multiroot-audit.md) |

#### Requirement

The system SHALL verify the H1 pinning harvest — the `readBlob`, topology-hash and `assertPinned*` primitive ported from `wave-agent-instructions.ts` into `freeze.ts` — as a port rather than as ported behaviour: the harvested code SHALL invoke plain `git cat-file` and `git ls-tree` with an explicit `cwd` instead of `runTrustedGit`, SHALL carry neither the `AGENTS.md` discovery half nor any dependency on `trusted-git.ts`, SHALL record a real `git_blob_oid` alongside its sha256 rather than a `null` placeholder, and SHALL be proven by all 15 substitution attack cases re-run against the replaced dependency plus one additional case whose pinned document is the orchestrator's own handoff; each of those tests SHALL be authored test-first, because the port changes the code's dependencies and a ported passing test therefore does not discharge the TDD rule.

#### Rationale

06 §3.1 H1 and 05 §0.4 rule that the pinning half is harvested but that its `runTrustedGit` dependency must be dropped, because 06 §5.1 Defect B reproduces ambient-cwd sensitivity in `trusted-git.ts` outside vitest against compiled `dist` with a clean tree — accepted from a cwd outside any repository, rejected from inside the branch worktree, deterministic across repeats — which is the exact failure class a multi-root orchestrator cannot tolerate. 06 §5.5 item 1 and 05 §0.5 item 1 state the verification, and 05 §10.5 step 4' sequences it as the H1 to `freeze.ts` row under requirement row HV-1. 06:386-388 states that porting a passing test alongside ported code does not satisfy the TDD rule when the port changes the code's dependencies, which H1 does.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: All 15 substitution attack cases from the branch's pinning suite — covering inode, ancestor-directory, symlink, hardlink and junction substitution — run against the harvested primitive in `freeze.ts` and fail the pin re-proof, and none of them reaches `runTrustedGit` or any other `trusted-git.ts` export.
- [x] AC-2: Every `git` invocation on the harvested pinning path passes an explicit `cwd`; a test asserts that no invocation is spawned without a `cwd` option and therefore none inherits the calling process's working directory.
- [x] AC-3: A 16th case whose pinned document is the orchestrator's own authored handoff — not `AGENTS.md` — pins and re-proves successfully, and the harvested module exposes no `AGENTS.md` discovery entry point.
- [x] AC-4: Pinning a handoff records both a sha256 and a real `git_blob_oid` read from the repository; a test asserts the `git_blob_oid` field is never `null` on a successfully pinned document.
- [ ] AC-5: The commit record for this harvest shows each pinning test committed with a recorded failing run before the commit that lands the harvested implementation, so the port is discharged test-first rather than by a ported green test.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/orchestrator/pinning.fr-node-101.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4 | 19 passed / 1 skipped / 0 failed (20 cases), measured 2026-08-01 on this file alone. AC-1: of the 15 substitution attack cases, 14 EXECUTED on this Windows host and 1 did not — case 7, the leaf symlink substitution, calls skip() at :181 with the recorded reason 'file symlink creation is not permitted for this process', because Windows refuses file-symlink creation without SeCreateSymbolicLink. The 14 that ran cover inode (:150), ancestor-directory (:161), hardlink (:187), ancestor junction (:201) and forged-snapshot/HEAD-moved (:281) substitution and each fails the pin re-proof. The no-trusted-git half is asserted at :320: the source contains neither 'trusted-git' nor 'runTrustedGit', and its local import graph is asserted EMPTY, so no trusted-git.ts export is reachable transitively. AC-2 :351 asserts every recorded git invocation (>20) carries a string, non-empty, absolute cwd, and :340 asserts the runner binds to its cwd argument rather than the calling process by reading a HEAD that differs from the SpecKiwi checkout's own. AC-3 :294 pins and re-proves the orchestrator's own authored handoff at .kiwi/orchestrator/handoff/ while an AGENTS.md sits beside it, asserting no pinned document ends in agents.md, and :308 asserts the module's exported key set is exactly [HandoffPinError, PIN_LIMITS, assertPinned, pinHandoff, runGit] with no 'agents.md' string and no /discover/i in the source. AC-4 :101-102 asserts every pinned document's gitBlobOid matches a 40- or 64-hex pattern (which a null cannot satisfy) alongside a 64-hex sha256. |
| VE-2 | inspection | git log --follow test/core/orchestrator/pinning.fr-node-101.test.ts src/core/orchestrator/freeze.ts | AC-5 | MEASURED 2026-08-01 AND DOES NOT HOLD — recorded here so the gap is not silently inherited. AC-5 requires the commit record to show each pinning test committed with a recorded failing run BEFORE the commit that lands the harvested implementation. 'git log --oneline --follow' returns exactly one commit, a8bb354, for the test file and the same single commit a8bb354 for the implementation. Test and implementation therefore landed in one commit, and no earlier commit carries the test with a recorded red run. The port may well have been discharged test-first in the working tree, but the commit record does not evidence it, and this criterion asks for the commit record. AC-5 is left UNTICKED and this requirement is held at implemented rather than verified. Squashing removed the evidence this criterion was written to demand; only re-recording it on a future harvest, or amending the criterion to something the history can show, can close it. |

### FR-NODE-102 — Run-lock harvest verified against a second OS process and two worktrees over one .git

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md), [deferred multiroot audit](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/06.deferred-multiroot-audit.md) |

#### Requirement

The system SHALL verify the H2 run-lock harvest — `lease-lock.ts` ported for the orchestrator run lock — by proving exclusion against a second operating-system process rather than a second promise in one process, and by proving that the lock, re-keyed on the git common dir instead of a per-worktree project root, excludes two worktrees sharing one `.git`; each of those tests SHALL be authored test-first, because the re-keying changes the module's dependencies and a ported passing test therefore does not discharge the TDD rule.

#### Rationale

06 §3.1 H2 harvests `lease-lock.ts` verbatim with one required change: as written every lock path derives from a `ProjectRoot`, so N worktrees over one shared `.git` get N different locks and no mutual exclusion. 06 §5.3 records that `lease-lock.ts`'s kernel fence is a stub on POSIX and that the branch's 64-way race test is 64 promises in one process, so nothing on the branch proves cross-process exclusion. 06 §5.5 item 2 and 05 §0.5 item 2 state the two verifications, and 05 §10.5 step 4' sequences them as the H2 to `run-lock.ts` row under requirement row HV-2. 06:386-388 makes the test-first obligation explicit for H2.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A test spawns a second Node operating-system process — not a second promise inside one process — that attempts to acquire the orchestrator run lock while a first process holds it, and asserts the second acquisition is refused.
- [x] AC-2: A test creates two git worktrees over one `.git`, has each attempt to acquire the orchestrator run lock, and asserts exactly one acquisition succeeds.
- [x] AC-3: A test asserts that two worktrees over one `.git` resolve to one identical lock path, so no lock path in the harvested module derives from a per-worktree project root.
- [ ] AC-4: The commit record for this harvest shows each run-lock test committed with a recorded failing run before the commit that lands the harvested implementation, so the re-keyed port is discharged test-first rather than by a ported green test.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/orchestrator/run-lock.fr-node-102.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3 | 4 passed / 0 failed, measured 2026-08-01 on this file alone. Each case is named for the criterion it discharges. AC-1 is asserted in BOTH directions with a real second operating-system process, not a second promise: :78 spawns a child that acquires first (its pid asserted different from process.pid at :84) and the in-process acquisition then rejects, and :92 has this process hold the lock while the child is refused, the child printing 'REFUSED orchestrator-run-lock-held' (:98) and exiting 3 (:100). AC-2 :106 creates a second linked worktree over one .git via 'git worktree add', races both acquisitions, and asserts exactly one winner and one loser (:120-121) with the loser carrying gate 'orchestrator-run-lock-held' (:122). AC-3 :127 asserts the two worktrees resolve to one identical git common dir (:134) and one identical lock path (:135), and further that the lock path does not contain the second worktree's own path (:140) and is rooted at the common dir (:141), so no lock path derives from a per-worktree project root. |
| VE-2 | inspection | git log --follow test/core/orchestrator/run-lock.fr-node-102.test.ts src/core/orchestrator/run-lock.ts | AC-4 | MEASURED 2026-08-01 AND DOES NOT HOLD — recorded so the gap is not silently inherited. AC-4 requires the commit record to show each run-lock test committed with a recorded failing run BEFORE the commit landing the harvested implementation. 'git log --oneline --follow' returns exactly one commit, a8bb354, for the test file and the same single commit a8bb354 for src/core/orchestrator/run-lock.ts. Test and implementation landed together, so no commit shows the re-keyed port discharged test-first. This is the same shape of gap as FR-NODE-101 AC-5 and has the same cause: the phase-1 work was squashed into one commit. AC-4 is left UNTICKED and the requirement is held at implemented. |

### FR-NODE-103 — Requirement readiness harvest verified against a speckiwi list --json snapshot

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md), [deferred multiroot audit](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/06.deferred-multiroot-audit.md) |

#### Requirement

The system SHALL verify the H3 readiness harvest — `deriveCanonicalRequirementReadiness` and `scopeRequirementDiagnostics` lifted into `readiness.ts` — by re-running all 15 behavioural readiness cases against a snapshot obtained from `speckiwi list --json` rather than from MCP, proving transport independence; the harvested functions SHALL remain pure over `(snapshot, target, requirementIds)` with no MCP client, no git and no filesystem access, and SHALL carry none of the surrounding per-root MCP transport of `wave-spec-client.ts`.

#### Rationale

06 §3.1 H3 lifts the two pure functions and their 15 behavioural tests, and 06 §3.2 rules that the transport around them is not harvested: `createMcpCwdBoundSpecClient` spawns a per-root `speckiwi mcp` process from Node, which a skill cannot call, which charter C1 makes moot, and which 02:402 already records as prohibited at the CLI surface. 06 §5.5 item 3 and 05 §0.5 item 3 state the verification — re-run the 15 cases against a `speckiwi list --json` snapshot to prove transport independence — and 05 §10.5 step 4' sequences it as the H3 to `readiness.ts` row under requirement row HV-3.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: All 15 behavioural readiness cases pass against a snapshot parsed from `speckiwi list --json` output, with no MCP call in the test path.
- [x] AC-2: A test asserts the two harvested functions perform no I/O given `(snapshot, target, requirementIds)` — no MCP client construction, no git invocation and no filesystem read.
- [x] AC-3: The harvested module contains no per-root MCP transport: `createMcpCwdBoundSpecClient` and the surrounding `wave-spec-client.ts` client code are absent.
- [x] AC-4: `hardDependenciesSatisfied`, `evidenceDrift` and `ownershipVerified` are each computed from the snapshot's raw records rather than from a caller-supplied readiness claim, so a caller cannot self-attest readiness; at least one of the 15 cases covers each of the three.
- [x] AC-5: The readiness derivation fails closed on a dependency cycle, on duplicate requirement IDs and on a summary contradiction, each covered by one of the 15 cases.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/orchestrator/readiness.fr-node-103.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5 | 23 passed / 0 failed, measured 2026-08-01 on this file alone. Cases are named for the criterion they discharge. AC-1 :338 asserts the case table names exactly 15 behavioural readiness cases; :401 runs them against a snapshot taken through the real 'speckiwi list --json' CLI with no MCP call on the test path; :453 asserts the same content arriving through the MCP envelope yields an identical snapshot and identical result, so the derivation is transport-independent. AC-2 :380 asserts the module's import graph reaches no node builtin and no MCP client, which is how 'no I/O given (snapshot, target, requirementIds)' is established — statically over the import graph rather than by spying on fs or git. AC-3 :392 asserts no per-root MCP transport survives the harvest, naming wave-spec-client.ts and createMcpCwdBoundSpecClient as absent. AC-4 :356 asserts each of hardDependenciesSatisfied, evidenceDrift and ownershipVerified is covered by at least one of the 15 cases, and :366 asserts readiness is derived from the snapshot's raw records by showing a contradictory summary cannot make a record ready, so a caller cannot self-attest. AC-5 :361 asserts the three fail-closed classes — dependency cycle, duplicate requirement IDs, summary contradiction — are each covered by exactly one of the 15 cases. |

### FR-NODE-104 — Harvest verification under a non-trivial global gitconfig and three working directories

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md), [deferred multiroot audit](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/06.deferred-multiroot-audit.md) |

#### Requirement

Every phase-1 harvest verification test SHALL pass under a non-trivial global gitconfig with filters and `core.autocrlf` set, and from at least three different process working directories, producing the same result in all of them; the phase-1 denominator of this obligation SHALL be exactly the HV-1, HV-2 and HV-3 harvest suites, with HV-4's case joining the denominator in `2.6.0-phase2-parallel-lanes`.

#### Rationale

06 §5.5 item 6 requires every harvested item to run under a non-trivial global gitconfig (filters plus autocrlf) and from at least three different process working directories, and 05 §0.5 item 5 carries it forward naming §5.1 Defect B as the reason: the same guard was reproduced accepting from a cwd outside any repository and rejecting from inside the branch worktree, deterministically, while the fixture tree was clean, because `dynamicCommandOverrides` spawns git with no `cwd` option at all. 06 §5.1 also records that the injected filter-disabling overrides make behaviour depend on the developer's global gitconfig, this machine carrying `filter.lfs.*` and `filter.obfuscate.clean`. 05 §15.1 fixes the phase-1 denominator at HV-1 to HV-3 because HV-4 moves to phase 2 with `integrate()`.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: The HV-1, HV-2 and HV-3 harvest suites run under a global gitconfig that sets at least one `filter.*` entry and `core.autocrlf=true`, and all pass.
- [x] AC-2: The same three suites run from at least three distinct process working directories and produce the same result in every one of them.
- [x] AC-3: The evidence enumerates exactly three covered harvest suites — HV-1, HV-2 and HV-3 — and no criterion of this requirement refers to the HV-4 CAS case, which is phase-2 work.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/orchestrator/harvest.fr-node-104.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3 | 2 passed / 0 failed, measured 2026-08-01 on this file alone. The low case count is structural, not thin coverage: the second case spawns the three harvest suites as child vitest runs three times over. AC-1 writes a gitconfig carrying TWO filter.* entries (filter.speckiwiHarvest with clean/smudge/required, filter.speckiwiHarvestSecond with clean) plus core.autocrlf=true, asserts the file really contains autocrlf, exports it as GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL to each child, and asserts every child run exits 0 with numFailedTests 0 and numPassedTests greater than 0. AC-2 runs from three distinct working directories chosen as the classes that would reproduce the 06 §5.1 cwd-sensitivity defect — the repository under test, a directory outside any repository, and an unrelated repository with a different HEAD — asserts the three cwds are distinct, has each child RECORD the cwd it actually observed per suite and asserts that recorded cwd resolves to the directory the run was launched from, then asserts the totals object is deep-equal across all three runs, which is what makes 'the same result in every one of them' a measurement rather than three independent passes. AC-3 asserts the suite table has length 3, its ids are exactly [HV-1, HV-2, HV-3], it does not contain HV-4, and each named suite file exists on disk; HV-4's CAS case is deliberately absent as phase-2 work and no criterion of this requirement refers to it. |

### FR-NODE-105 — speckiwi skills mirror check and write as the sanctioned .agents/skills writer

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

The system SHALL provide a `speckiwi skills mirror` command with a `--check` mode and a `--write` mode: `--write` SHALL regenerate `.agents/skills/**` from `skills/codex/**` honouring `.agents/skills/.speckiwi-mirror-exclusions.json`, and `--check` SHALL verify the mirror against that same regeneration without writing, reporting divergence; neither mode SHALL invoke `speckiwi init` or `speckiwi skills install`, so that the four-copy parity requirement gains a sanctioned writer without touching the charter's prohibition on running `init`, `skills install` or `upgrade --apply` against this repository as the target root.

#### Rationale

05 §9.5: CP-05 marks `.agents/skills/**` orchestrator-only while §9.4 mirrors `kiwi-orchestrator` normally, §14 registration 5 requires `waves-event.md` v1.4.0 in all four copies edited in one change, and E28 asserts set-equality across all four shipped copies — so four-copy parity was a shipping requirement with no sanctioned writer, the mirror being maintained out of band (`scripts/` holds only `release-check.mjs` and `version-check.mjs`). The named mechanism is a new verb that is neither `init` nor `skills install`, so `00.charter.md:303-304` is untouched; `--check` is the CI/gate form and is what E28 calls. 05 §10.5 step 6" sequences it, red first.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `speckiwi skills mirror --write` regenerates `.agents/skills/**` from `skills/codex/**`, and for every non-excluded skill the regenerated tree matches the codex variant's content.
- [x] AC-2: `speckiwi skills mirror --check` on a divergent mirror reports the divergence and writes no file — a test asserts the tree is byte-unchanged after the check — and on an in-sync mirror it reports success.
- [x] AC-3: Both modes honour `.agents/skills/.speckiwi-mirror-exclusions.json`: every skill named there is neither regenerated nor reported as divergent, and neither the file's entries nor its `reason` field is modified by either mode.
- [x] AC-4: Neither mode reaches the `init` or `skills install` code path; a test asserts the mirror command completes without invoking either.
- [x] AC-5: `--check` detects the four-copy parity case E28 depends on: with `.agents/skills/_shared/kiwi/waves-event.md` diverging from `skills/codex/_shared/kiwi/waves-event.md`, `--check` reports divergence rather than succeeding.

#### Verification Evidence

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/skills/mirror-skills.fr-node-105.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5 | 19 passed / 0 failed, measured 2026-08-01 with 'npx vitest run --no-file-parallelism' on this file alone. Per-AC describe blocks: AC-1 :83-125 regenerated tree matches the codex variant's content, plus prune and idempotence; AC-2 :127-185 divergent mirror reported with the tree asserted byte-unchanged and in-sync reported success; AC-3 :187-235 excluded skills neither regenerated nor reported, and the manifest's entries and reason asserted unmodified in both modes; AC-4 :237-271; AC-5 :273-290 a diverging _shared/kiwi/waves-event.md is reported as divergent, which is the four-copy parity case FR-NODE-129 depends on. AC-4's proof is a static import scan of mirror-skills.ts (regex at :239, asserted :241) plus the absence of the artifacts init and skills install would leave behind after a real --write (:248), NOT a runtime spy on the install code path. src/cli/commands/skills.ts:3 does import installSkill for the sibling 'install' verb, so 'the mirror action never calls it' holds by inspection of the action body at :89-108 rather than by assertion. |
| VE-2 | test | test/cli/skills-mirror.fr-node-105.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2 | 6 passed / 0 failed, measured 2026-08-01 on this file alone. Exercises the same two modes through the real CLI verb: --check exits non-zero on a divergent mirror and the tree is asserted unwritten (:102-114), exits zero on an in-sync mirror (:115), and --write regenerates from skills/codex leaving the exclusions manifest in place (:129-139). This file additionally asserts two failure modes that NO acceptance criterion of this requirement names: --check and --write are mutually exclusive (SKILLS_MIRROR_MODE_CONFLICT, :79-88) and neither mode defaults (SKILLS_MIRROR_MODE_REQUIRED, :65-75), both exiting 2 and writing nothing. Those assertions are recorded here as measured behaviour; they are not counted toward any ticked criterion. |

### FR-NODE-106 — Orchestrator repository registrations for gitignore and coverage include

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

The repository's tracked `.gitignore` SHALL cover `kiwi/waves.jsonl` and `kiwi/orchestrator/`, so that orchestrator machine state is state `git clean -fd` may remove and `git add -A` can never stage, and `vitest.config.ts`'s coverage `include` SHALL cover `src/core/orchestrator/**/*.ts` under the same `parserWorkflowThreshold` the workflow tree already carries.

#### Rationale

05 §14 registration 9 and §10.5 step 10. §4.8 makes the journal and the run directory untracked by policy, and a tracked `.gitignore` is what makes that policy hold on a fresh clone or a CI runner rather than only on a checkout that happens to carry a local exclude. Registration 10 records that without the coverage include the design's 23 new phase-1 orchestrator modules — its largest executable surface — carry no coverage threshold at all, while the two files the work merely modifies sit inside a 90% one.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: The tracked `.gitignore` contains an entry covering `kiwi/waves.jsonl` and an entry covering `kiwi/orchestrator/`.
- [x] AC-2: With both paths present on disk, `git status --porcelain` reports neither as untracked and `git add -A` stages neither.
- [x] AC-3: `vitest.config.ts`'s coverage `include` contains `src/core/orchestrator/**/*.ts`.

### FR-NODE-107 — Terminal lane disposition classifies as lane-quarantined and settles a prior stage

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

The resume reconciliation SHALL classify a lane that carries a terminal `lane_disposition` and is not merged into `frozen.integration_branch` as `lane-quarantined`, an outcome that emits no next action for that lane, where `lane_disposition.kind` is drawn from the closed four-value enum `demoted | quarantined | coupling-reset | refuted` and is read from any result line recorded for that lane's `(wave, stage, lane)` key; and the precondition `P-PRIOR-STAGES-INTEGRATED` SHALL evaluate true for a stage `s` exactly when every lane of every stage `< s` in the wave is settled, where a lane is settled when it carries a merge witness against `frozen.integration_branch` or the journal carries a terminal `lane_disposition` for it.

#### Rationale

05 §4.6 and §4.1. Without the `lane-quarantined` class a lane that left the run without merging — demoted, quarantined by a coupling collapse, or design-refuted — reads as `lane-integrable` on resume and a resumed session integrates work the run had discarded (05 §4.6 finding 5, §20.3 RX-07). Without the disposition disjunct on `P-PRIOR-STAGES-INTEGRATED`, a legally demoted stage-1 lane makes the precondition false forever and the wave becomes unresumable for the rest of its life (05 §4.1). In phase 1 the carrier is the `execute-unit` result line and the one producible kind is `refuted` (05 §22.10 AF-09); without a phase-1 carrier a refuted commitless unit classifies `not-dispatched` and resume re-enters `/kiwi-pm` for the refuted Tasks.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Given a lane whose journal carries a terminal `lane_disposition` for its `(wave, stage, lane)` key and which is not an ancestor of `frozen.integration_branch`, the per-lane classification is `lane-quarantined` and the stage reduction emits no next action for that lane.
- [x] AC-2: `lane_disposition.kind` is validated against the closed four-value enum `demoted | quarantined | coupling-reset | refuted`; a value outside that enum is rejected.
- [x] AC-3: The disposition reader is result-line-agnostic: a `lane_disposition` recorded on a phase-1 `execute-unit` result line is read exactly as one recorded on a phase-2 `verify-lane` or `collect-lane` result line.
- [x] AC-4: A phase-1 unit that produced no commit and has no journal action line, but whose `execute-unit` result line carries `lane_disposition` with `kind: "refuted"`, classifies as `lane-quarantined` and not as `not-dispatched`, so no re-execution of that unit is emitted.
- [x] AC-5: `P-PRIOR-STAGES-INTEGRATED` evaluates true for stage `s` on a fixture where one lane of stage 1 is unmerged and carries a terminal `lane_disposition` and every other lane of stages `< s` carries a merge witness.
- [x] AC-6: `P-PRIOR-STAGES-INTEGRATED` evaluates false when at least one lane of a stage `< s` carries neither a merge witness nor a terminal `lane_disposition`.
- [x] AC-7: In phase 1 the merge witness is the `git-trailer` kind — a commit on `frozen.integration_branch` carrying `Orch-Run`, `Orch-Wave`, `Orch-Stage` and `Orch-Lane` for that unit — and no `git-ancestor` proof between two named refs is required.

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/orchestrator/lane-state.fr-node-107.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6, AC-7 | 25 passed / 0 failed, measured 2026-08-01 on this file alone. Per-AC describe blocks. AC-1 :117-138 an unmerged, dispositioned lane classifies lane-quarantined with no next action, with both negative controls: a merged lane does not (:129) and an undispositioned unmerged lane does not (:134). AC-2 :58-100 the kind enum is asserted to be exactly the four values demoted, quarantined, coupling-reset and refuted (:59), all four read back (:64), an out-of-enum kind is REJECTED rather than read as terminal (:71), and a disposition with no kind at all is rejected (:79). AC-3 :102-116 a disposition on a phase-1 execute-unit result reads exactly as on verify-lane or collect-lane (:103), and one on an intent line is ignored because only a result records a fact (:112). AC-4 :139 a commitless, action-line-free unit whose execute-unit result carries kind refuted classifies lane-quarantined and not not-dispatched. AC-5 :210 P-PRIOR-STAGES-INTEGRATED is true when one stage-1 lane is unmerged but terminally dispositioned and every other prior lane has a merge witness. AC-6 :228 false when a prior-stage lane carries neither, and :234 names every unsettled lane rather than only the first. AC-7 :164-190 the witness requires all four Orch-Run, Orch-Wave, Orch-Stage and Orch-Lane trailers and names exactly those four (:165), refuses a mismatched run (:175) or unit (:179), and :269 asserts the predicate needs no ref pair because its whole git input is a list of trailered commits, so no git-ancestor proof between two named refs is required. The validated reader is wired into the shipped path: src/core/orchestrator/resume.ts:18 imports readLaneDisposition and calls it at :179, so the classification at :188 cannot fire on an unvalidated kind. |

### FR-NODE-108 — Six orchestrator kernels declare fixture-constructible input types

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

Each of the six pure orchestrator kernel functions `evaluateRound`, `computeLanePlan`, `planDuplicationAudit`, `planStageCoupling`, `computeResumeState` and `decideAutoGate` SHALL declare an exported input type for every one of its arguments, and each declared input type SHALL be complete enough that a test fixture can be constructed from the type declarations alone, without reading the function's implementation.

#### Rationale

05 §10.1 (INPUT shapes) and §20.2 RX-20. The work order's first step is 'fixtures plus Layer-1 tests, all red', and a red test cannot be authored first against an undeclared argument type. Revision 2 declared seven output shapes and stopped, leaving six modules with an unauthorable red test — widest exactly where the value is highest, at `evaluateRound` (the ship-first module of §10.5 step 3) and `decideAutoGate` (the entire executable surface of the second phase-1 work item).

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Each of the six functions has a named, exported type for every argument it declares: `evaluateRound(round: Round)`, `computeLanePlan(input: LanePlanInput)`, `planDuplicationAudit(laneDiffs: LaneDiff[], writeSets)`, `planStageCoupling(handoffs: ParsedHandoff[])`, `computeResumeState(view, card, gitFacts, driftInputs)` and `decideAutoGate(input: AutoGateInput)`.
- [x] AC-2: Every field of each declared input type is reachable from an exported declaration — `LanePlanInput` through `TaskCatalogEntry`, `ConvergencePoint`, `Lane` and `Stage`; `computeResumeState`'s fourth argument through `DriftInputs` and `LockDigests` — so no field's shape must be inferred from the implementation.
- [x] AC-3: `planStageCoupling` takes exactly one argument, `ParsedHandoff[]`, and takes no `existing_paths` argument.
- [x] AC-4: `AutoGateInput` declares `gateId`, `critical`, `options[]` of `{id, recommended, defaultIfAuto}`, `mode`, a nullable `votes[]`, `quorum` of `{expected, present}` and `tieRung`, and `decideAutoGate`'s returned `action` is drawn from the closed five-value `AutoGateAction` vocabulary.
- [x] AC-5: `computeResumeState` takes four arguments and performs no git, filesystem or network access: the impure facts arrive injected through `GitFacts` and `DriftInputs`.
- [x] AC-6: A fixture-construction test builds one value of each of the six input types and calls each function, importing only the exported type declarations and no implementation internals.

### FR-NODE-109 — Handoff validation refuses base_sha and resolves against the dispatch base

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

`validateHandoff` SHALL reject a handoff whose front matter carries a top-level `base_sha` field, and its resolvability layer SHALL resolve exactly the declared field set — `plan_path`, `sidecar_path`, every `write_set[]` entry, every `read_set[]` entry, every non-null `acceptance[].test_id`'s file, and every path named in the body's `## Interfaces` and `## Tasks` sections — against HEAD at Phase 3.f plus the set of paths Phase 3.f-prime is about to stage, and SHALL never resolve against a sha that does not yet exist.

#### Rationale

05 §2.1 ordering rule 2 and §6.2 layer 4. A commit's sha is a hash over its own tree, so no value written into a file inside that commit can ever equal it: `base_sha` as a required handoff front-matter field is unsatisfiable, no `--amend` sequence converges, and the natural implementer workaround — writing the last known sha — puts the handoff outside its own `base_sha` and reopens the ENOENT-on-first-read failure. Revision 2's layer 4 said 'every referenced path' with no scope and 'exists at base_sha' with no way to evaluate it, and both halves were unrunnable.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A handoff whose front matter carries a top-level `base_sha` key is rejected by `validateHandoff` regardless of the key's value, including `null`.
- [x] AC-2: A `base_sha` key inside the front matter's `manifest_template` object is not a top-level field and does not trigger that rejection, so a conformant handoff carrying `manifest_template.base_sha: null` validates.
- [x] AC-3: Layer 4 resolves exactly the six declared field sources and no others: a path named only in the body's `## Context` prose is not resolved and does not fail validation.
- [x] AC-4: The four workspace-local or git-ignored fields `manifest_path`, `heartbeat_path`, `deferred_mutations_path` and `decisions_path`, and `regression_baseline_ref`, are excluded from resolution and do not fail validation when they name no existing path.
- [x] AC-5: Within the declared field set, a referenced path resolves when it exists at HEAD at 3.f or is a `write_set` member; a path that is neither raises `handoff-unresolvable-reference`.
- [x] AC-6: A path that does not exist at HEAD at 3.f but is in the set 3.f-prime is about to stage resolves, so validation never waits on a commit that has not been made.
- [x] AC-7: Every non-null `acceptance[].test_id` resolves to a test file that exists at the dispatch base or is in `write_set`, and no two acceptance rows share a `test_id`.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/orchestrator/handoff.fr-node-109.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6, AC-7 | 14 passed / 0 failed, measured 2026-08-01 on this file alone. Per-AC describe blocks. AC-1 :30-36 is parametrized over three values — a real 40-hex sha, null and 0 — and asserts each is rejected with a violation naming base_sha, so the rejection is on the key's presence and not its value. AC-2 :38-43 asserts the manifest template block genuinely contains the nested base_sha null before asserting the conformant handoff validates with an empty violation list, so the case cannot pass by the nested key being absent. AC-3 :45-59 asserts both directions: a path named only in the body's Context prose produces no violation (:46) while the same shape of path named in the Interfaces section raises handoff-unresolvable-reference (:54). The negative half is the discriminator the criterion itself names; the positive enumeration of the remaining declared sources is carried by the AC-5 and AC-6 cases rather than by a single six-way enumeration. AC-4 :61-76 accepts a handoff whose manifest_path, heartbeat_path, deferred_mutations_path, decisions_path and regression_baseline_ref all name nothing that exists. AC-5 :77-95 resolves a read-set path at HEAD (:78) and a write-set path present at neither HEAD nor the staged set (:83), and raises handoff-unresolvable-reference for a read-set path that is neither (:90). AC-6 :96-107 resolves a read-set path absent from HEAD but present in the staged set, so validation never waits on a commit not yet made. AC-7 :108-131 refuses a test id naming a file at neither the dispatch base nor the write set (:109), refuses two acceptance rows sharing one test id (:119), and accepts the four distinct rows of the worked example (:126). |

### FR-NODE-110 — computeRoute is a pure total disqualifier-first route classifier

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [routing design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/09.routing-design.md), [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

`computeRoute(probe, opts)` SHALL be a pure, total, disqualifier-first function over a validated route probe: every predicate SHALL remove rungs and none SHALL select one; each removal SHALL be recorded in `removed[]` as `{rung, by, observed}` with `by` drawn from the closed `DisqualifierId` enum `D1` through `D8` and with a deterministic ordering; the returned rung SHALL be the first survivor of the fixed order `R-PLAN` then `R-STEP` then `R-ORCH` and SHALL be a member of the closed `Rung` enum `R-STEP | R-PLAN | R-ORCH`; no predicate SHALL remove `R-ORCH`; and the returned `RouteDecision` SHALL carry exactly the fields `rung`, `removed`, `alternative`, `decisive`, `recommended` and `withheld_because`.

#### Rationale

09 §3.1, §3.3, §3.4 and §3.6. The classifier is disqualifier-first so that a wrong route can always be traced to one named predicate and one recorded value. Totality is what makes the ladder terminate at exactly one rung with no rung-exhaustion case, which is why `route-probe-unreadable` is a tool-availability gate rather than a 'ran out of routes' gate. D8's `GATED_BY` map must map every probe field id to a list — the empty list, never `undefined` — or the removal loop is undefined for a member.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `computeRoute` is pure: two calls with the same `RouteProbe` value and the same `opts` return deep-equal `RouteDecision` values, and the function performs no filesystem, git, network or clock access.
- [x] AC-2: `computeRoute` is total: a property test over schema-valid probes asserts that exactly one rung is returned on every input and that it is a member of the closed three-value `Rung` enum `R-STEP | R-PLAN | R-ORCH`.
- [x] AC-3: Each of the eight disqualifiers `D1` through `D8` has one fixture in which it fires and one in which it does not; a firing appends exactly one `{rung, by, observed}` entry to `removed[]` whose `by` is that disqualifier's id and whose `observed` records the value it fired on.
- [x] AC-4: `removed[]` ordering is deterministic: a probe firing several disqualifiers produces the same `removed[]` sequence on every call, in the fixed predicate evaluation order D1 through D8.
- [x] AC-5: Every count threshold is exercised at plus and minus one of its boundary: D3 at 1 and 2 scopes; D4 at 1 and 2 `orderedSections`, at 1 and 2 `linkedSubIssues`, and at 0 and 1 `taskListGroups`; D6 at zero and one `open_tasks`; and the 0.2 `anchorCoverage` comparison immediately below and exactly at 0.2.
- [x] AC-6: Each adjacent pair of the selection order is exercised: a probe with no removals selects `R-PLAN`; a probe removing only `R-PLAN` selects `R-STEP`; a probe removing both `R-PLAN` and `R-STEP` selects `R-ORCH`.
- [x] AC-7: No predicate removes `R-ORCH`: a probe in which every disqualifier fires and every probe field id is present in `unreadable[]` still returns `R-ORCH`, and `removed[]` contains no entry whose `rung` is `R-ORCH`.
- [x] AC-8: `alternative` is the second surviving rung in the fixed order, and is `null` when exactly one rung survives.
- [x] AC-9: D8's `GATED_BY` map is total over the twelve `unreadable[]` member ids: one fixture per member asserts that `S3`, `S3c`, `S4`, `S5`, `S7`, `S8` and `S12` each remove `R-STEP`, that `S2`, `S9` and `S10` each remove `R-PLAN`, and that `S1` and `S6` map to the empty list rather than to `undefined`.
- [x] AC-10: `RouteDecision` carries the six declared fields and no others, so it cannot express `proposed_rung`, `overridden_by` or a gate resolution.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/orchestrator/route.fr-node-110.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6, AC-7, AC-8, AC-9, AC-10 | 63 passed / 0 failed, measured 2026-08-01 on this file alone. One describe block per criterion. AC-1 :23-65 purity — repeated calls over one probe return deep-equal decisions and the module reaches no filesystem, git, network or clock. AC-2 :66-123 is a genuine PROPERTY test, not a fixture set: 3000 seeded, stratified iterations over the schema-valid probe domain, asserting on every draw that the rung is a member of the closed three-value RUNGS enum and that the selected rung never appears in removed, and finally that all three rungs were actually visited so the property is not vacuous on one rung. The draw pool deliberately includes two ids OUTSIDE the vocabulary, S11 and S13, which is the totality hole an independent mutation audit found and this test now pins. AC-3 :124-253 gives each of D1 through D8 both a firing and a non-firing fixture, and each firing case asserts the recorded observed value, with D4's three disjuncts covered separately and a case asserting D4 names every disjunct that fired. AC-4 :254-276 ordering determinism over a multi-firing probe. AC-5 :277-310 the boundary pairs. AC-6 :311-327 each adjacent pair of the selection order. AC-7 :328-352 a probe in which every disqualifier fires and every field id is unreadable still returns R-ORCH with no R-ORCH entry in removed. AC-8 :353-366 alternative is the second surviving rung and null when one survives. AC-9 :367-420 drives one fixture per member of the exported PROBE_FIELD_IDS constant, which is exactly the twelve ids S1, S2, S3, S3c, S4, S5, S6, S7, S8, S9, S10 and S12, and additionally covers S11 and S13. AC-10 :421 RouteDecision is asserted a closed six-field record, so it cannot express proposed_rung, overridden_by or a gate resolution. |

### FR-NODE-111 — parseRouteProbe records unreadable probe fields and never defaults them

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [routing design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/09.routing-design.md), [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

`parseRouteProbe(json)` SHALL parse `routing/probe.json` into the `RouteProbe` projection and SHALL record every field it could not read as an explicit entry in `unreadable[]`, and SHALL never substitute a default value for an unreadable field.

#### Rationale

09 §3.2, §3.3 D8 and §10.1. Failing open on the anchored-requirement field yields an empty anchor set, which *enables* the step rung — fail-open in the dangerous direction. D8's fail-closed removal is only correct if the parser distinguishes a field it could not read from a value a producer legitimately returned, so the distinction must be made at parse time and cannot be recovered later.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: For each of the twelve probe field ids `S1`, `S2`, `S3`, `S3c`, `S4`, `S5`, `S6`, `S7`, `S8`, `S9`, `S10` and `S12`, a fixture whose probe cannot supply that field yields a parsed `RouteProbe` whose `unreadable[]` contains that id.
- [x] AC-2: No unreadable field becomes a permissive default: for an unreadable numeric field (`anchorCoverage`, `orderedSections`, `linkedSubIssues`, `taskListGroups`, `planOpenTasks`) the parsed value is not `0`, and for an unreadable array field (`anchoredReqs`, `scopes`, `scopeReqIds`, `externalPaths`, `planReqIds`, `blockedStability`) it is not `[]`.
- [x] AC-3: An unreadable `S3` is recorded in `unreadable[]` rather than parsed as an empty anchor set, so D8 removes `R-STEP` instead of D1 reading 'no anchored requirement' and clearing it.
- [x] AC-4: A value a producer legitimately returned is not an unreadable field: a probe whose input is not a GitHub issue records `task_list_groups` and `linked_sub_issues` as `0` and does not enter `S8` in `unreadable[]`.
- [x] AC-5: A probe whose `S9.activeTarget` is empty records `anchor_coverage` as `0`, `blocked_stability[]` as `[]` and `scope_req_ids[]` as `[]`, and enters none of `S3c`, `S10` or `S4` in `unreadable[]`, because an unregistered target is a value `get_active_target` returned.
- [x] AC-6: A reported scope name that does not resolve against the registered vocabulary is dropped into `S4.unresolved[]` and does not enter `S4` in `unreadable[]`, because an unrecognised free-text label is a naming miss rather than an unreadable field.

#### Verification Evidence

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/orchestrator/route-probe.fr-node-111.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5 | 60 passed / 0 failed, measured 2026-08-01 on this file alone. One describe block per criterion. AC-1 :25-91 drives it.each over the EXPORTED PROBE_FIELD_IDS constant rather than a restated list, giving every one of the twelve ids S1, S2, S3, S3c, S4, S5, S6, S7, S8, S9, S10 and S12 two fixtures — the field omitted (:26) and the field present but null (:32) — plus structurally-wrong and out-of-vocabulary value cases (:38, :43). This block also carries the tests for the totality repair an independent mutation audit prompted: an unreadable id the parser does not recognise is PRESERVED rather than dropped (:57), such a probe can never be read as fully readable and routes to R-ORCH with recommended false (:63), a wrongly-typed member is recorded as the malformed sentinel while the readable declarations survive (:70), and a non-array unreadable field fails closed to the sentinel and R-ORCH (:79). :86 asserts unreadable is ordered by the fixed field order so the projection is deterministic. AC-2 :92-153 asserts no unreadable field becomes a permissive default: unreadable numerics are not 0 and unreadable arrays are not empty. AC-3 :154-172 an unreadable S3 is recorded and removes R-STEP through D8 rather than being read by D1 as an empty anchor set. AC-4 :173-181 a non-GitHub-issue input records task_list_groups and linked_sub_issues as 0 without entering S8 in unreadable. AC-5 :182-207 an empty activeTarget records anchor_coverage 0 with empty blocked_stability and scope_req_ids and enters none of S3c, S10 or S4, with :204 the control showing an absent S3c IS still recorded unreadable when a target is registered. This file does NOT assert AC-6 — in it unresolved appears only as fixture input at :184; AC-6 is evidenced by VE-2. |
| VE-2 | test | test/core/orchestrator/scopes.fr-node-118.test.ts | AC-6 | 17 passed / 0 failed for scopes.fr-node-118.test.ts, measured 2026-08-01 on that file alone. AC-6 of this requirement is asserted here rather than in the route-probe file, because the behaviour spans the scope deriver and the probe parser. The first half — a reported name that resolves against no registered scope is dropped into unresolved rather than counted — is :36-40, which asserts scopes holds only the resolvable name while unresolved holds the free-text label, with :43 the control that an unresolvable name cannot push the scope count to D3's threshold. The second half — that such a name does NOT enter S4 in unreadable — is :84-88, which calls parseRouteProbe on an S4 carrying an unresolved member and asserts unreadable does not contain S4, establishing that an unrecognised free-text label is treated as a naming miss and not as an unreadable field. :78-82 additionally asserts unresolved is absent from the parsed RouteProbe field set entirely. |

### FR-NODE-112 — Anchored-requirement set and Code-typed anchor coverage

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [routing design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/09.routing-design.md), [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

`deriveAnchoredRequirements(records, filePaths)` SHALL compute the anchored-requirement set by normalized exact match between each file path and each requirement's trace-anchor list, and `computeAnchorCoverage(records)` SHALL compute anchor coverage as the fraction of the active target's requirements that carry at least one `traceLinks[]` row whose `type` is `Code`.

#### Rationale

09 §3.2 S3 and S3c. The trace-reference match replaces a `search_requirements` text scan whose union over intent terms saturates on a common token and empties on a novel vocabulary; an exact normalized match answers 'which body requirements already own this file?' and names the requirement that should have blocked a wrong route. Coverage is type-qualified because `traceReferences` is populated from every trace row regardless of type: in a repository whose requirements were never implemented through `kiwi-coder` the anchor set is empty everywhere and would clear the step rung vacuously, and coverage is the guard that stops it.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `deriveAnchoredRequirements` returns a requirement id when a file path matches one of its trace references exactly after normalization, with one fixture each for a backslash-separated path, a `./`-prefixed path, and a reference carrying a `#fragment`, all matching the same requirement.
- [x] AC-2: `deriveAnchoredRequirements` returns no id for a file path that matches no trace reference, and the union over several file paths contains each matching id exactly once.
- [x] AC-3: `computeAnchorCoverage` counts only `traceLinks[]` rows whose `type` is `Code`: a fixture whose requirements carry trace rows of other types exclusively yields coverage `0`.
- [x] AC-4: `computeAnchorCoverage` has one fixture below `0.2`, one exactly at `0.2` and one above `0.2`, so the `>= 0.2` comparison that D1 and D6 apply is pinned at the boundary.
- [x] AC-5: `computeAnchorCoverage` counts a requirement once however many `Code`-typed trace rows it carries, so the result is a fraction of requirements and never exceeds `1`.
- [x] AC-6: `computeAnchorCoverage` reads the record's `traceLinks` field rather than the type-erased compact `traceReferences` field.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/orchestrator/anchors.fr-node-112.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6 | 17 passed / 0 failed, measured 2026-08-01 on this file alone. One describe block per criterion, each named for the criterion it discharges. AC-1 :17-34 a normalized exact match returns the requirement id, with the three normalization fixtures the criterion names — a backslash-separated path, a dot-slash-prefixed path and a reference carrying a fragment — all matching the same requirement. AC-2 :35-67 a path matching no trace reference returns no id, and the union over several file paths contains each matching id exactly once. AC-3 :68-87 computeAnchorCoverage counts only traceLinks rows whose type is Code, with a fixture whose requirements carry trace rows of other types exclusively yielding coverage 0. AC-4 :88-112 pins the 0.2 comparison D1 and D6 apply with one fixture below, one exactly at and one above the threshold. AC-5 :113-122 a requirement is counted once however many Code-typed rows it carries, so the result is a fraction of requirements and never exceeds 1. AC-6 :123 asserts the function reads the record's traceLinks field rather than the type-erased compact traceReferences field, which is what keeps the Code-type filter in AC-3 meaningful. |

### FR-NODE-113 — freezeRoute writes a content-addressed lock the resume path reads instead of recomputing

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [routing design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/09.routing-design.md), [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

`freezeRoute(probe, decision, gate)` SHALL write a content-addressed `route.lock.json` whose digest is recorded in the resume card's `frozen.route` and therefore participates in the run's `invariant_digest`, and a resumed session SHALL read the rung from that lock and never recompute it, raising `run-invariant-drift` when the `probe_digest` recorded in `frozen.route` no longer matches `probe.json` on disk.

#### Rationale

09 §9.2, §9.3 and §9.5; 05 §4.1 property 4. `computeRoute` runs exactly once per run: a resumed session has no conversation and no investigators, and several probe fields are subagent-derived and not reproducible across a compaction, so a recomputation could legally produce a different rung and switch ladders mid-run. Putting `route` inside `frozen` is the same repair applied to `isolation_profile` — a field claimed immutable for the run, sitting outside the digest, with no enforcement anywhere.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `freezeRoute` is deterministic and content-addressed: two calls over the same `probe`, `decision` and gate record produce a byte-identical `route.lock.json`, so a `freeze-route` redo whose digest matches is a no-op.
- [x] AC-2: A gate record carrying `selected: "proposed"` yields a lock whose `rung` and `proposed_rung` both equal `decision.rung` and whose `overridden_by` is `null`.
- [x] AC-3: A gate record carrying `selected: "alternative"` yields a lock whose `rung` equals `decision.alternative`, whose `proposed_rung` equals `decision.rung`, and whose `overridden_by` equals the record's `decided_by` value, one of `user` or `committee`.
- [x] AC-4: The lock's digest is recorded in the resume card's `frozen.route` as `{rung, lock, probe_digest}`, and a byte change to `route.lock.json` after the freeze makes the recomputed `invariant_digest` differ from the card's recorded value.
- [x] AC-5: A resumed session reads `frozen.route.rung` from the lock and does not call `computeRoute`: a resume fixture whose on-disk probe would classify a different rung still resumes on the recorded rung.
- [x] AC-6: A resumed session whose `probe.json` digest differs from `frozen.route.probe_digest` raises `run-invariant-drift` and does not recompute the rung.
- [x] AC-7: A lock written by `freezeRoute` round-trips through the resume-card validator, and a lock whose `probe_digest` was mutated after the freeze is reported by that same validator as drift.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/orchestrator/route-lock.fr-node-113.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6, AC-7 | 23 passed / 0 failed, measured 2026-08-01 on this file alone. One describe block per criterion. AC-1 :36-80 freezeRoute is deterministic and content-addressed — two calls over the same probe, decision and gate record produce a byte-identical route.lock.json, so a freeze-route redo whose digest matches is a no-op. AC-2 :81-92 a gate record carrying selected proposed yields a lock whose rung and proposed_rung both equal the decision rung and whose overridden_by is null. AC-3 :93-154 a gate record carrying selected alternative yields a lock whose rung equals the decision alternative, whose proposed_rung equals the decision rung, and whose overridden_by equals the record's decided_by, covering both permitted deciders. AC-4 :155-187 the digest is recorded in the resume card frozen.route as rung, lock and probe_digest, and a byte change to route.lock.json after the freeze makes the recomputed invariant_digest differ from the card's recorded value. AC-5 :188-204 a resumed session reads frozen.route.rung from the lock and does not call computeRoute, asserted with a resume fixture whose on-disk probe would classify a DIFFERENT rung and which still resumes on the recorded rung — the discriminating case rather than a same-rung one. AC-6 :205-224 a resumed session whose probe.json digest differs from frozen.route.probe_digest raises run-invariant-drift and does not recompute the rung. AC-7 :225-246 a lock written by freezeRoute round-trips through the resume-card validator and a lock whose probe_digest was mutated after the freeze is reported by that same validator as drift. The module-level guarantee behind AC-5 is structural: src/core/orchestrator/resume.ts:20-23 imports the rung reader and the drift check from route-lock and deliberately not computeRoute, so the resume path cannot re-judge the rung even by accident. |
