### FR-NODE-114 — Recommended marker computed as a pure function of the probe

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [routing design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/09.routing-design.md) |

#### Requirement

`computeRoute` SHALL compute `decisive`, `recommended` and `withheld_because[]` as a pure function of the probe, setting `recommended` true if and only if all five clauses hold — `unreadable` is empty; `decisive` is non-null and either carries a margin of at least one in its own unit or is a boolean corroborated by at least one other predicate that also fired; `ambiguities` is zero; when the selected rung is `R-STEP`, the mode source is not `default-wait`; and when the selected rung is `R-STEP` and D1 cleared it by measurement, anchor coverage is at least 0.2 — and SHALL name the failed clause in `withheld_because[]` whenever `recommended` is false.

#### Rationale

09 §8.2. A gate offering a recommended option adopts it immediately with no committee, so the marker is the zero-deliberation path and it is auditable only because it is a pure function of the recorded probe: a reviewer can re-run `computeRoute` over `probe.json` and get the same boolean. A null `decisive` withholds the marker rather than passing vacuously, because a run with nothing removed has no measured discrimination to fast-path on; that polarity is a decision, not an inference.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: One fixture per clause, failing that clause in isolation with the other four holding, asserts `recommended === false` and a `withheld_because[]` entry naming that clause; a fixture with all five clauses holding asserts `recommended === true` and an empty `withheld_because[]`.
- [x] AC-2: `decisive` is the removal of the rung nearest above the selected one in the fixed order `R-PLAN` then `R-STEP` then `R-ORCH`, and when several predicates removed that rung the entry with the lowest D-id is `decisive`.
- [x] AC-3: `decisive` is `null`, and clause 2 therefore fails, whenever no rung above the selected one was removed — including every `R-PLAN` selection and every probe whose `removed[]` is empty — so the marker is withheld on every `R-PLAN` selection, including one where D6 cleared `R-PLAN` on the substitute-link branch.
- [x] AC-4: Margin is computed per predicate in its own unit: D3's margin is the scope count minus 2, and D4's is the maximum of `orderedSections` minus 2, `linkedSubIssues` minus 2 and `taskListGroups` minus 1.
- [x] AC-5: A count predicate observed exactly at its threshold has margin `0` and does not satisfy clause 2's numeric branch: a two-scope D3 observation withholds the marker.
- [x] AC-6: The boolean and set-non-empty predicates D1, D2, D5, D6, D7 and D8 carry no numeric margin and can satisfy clause 2 only through the corroboration branch: a fixture in which the decisive boolean predicate is the only predicate that fired withholds the marker.
- [x] AC-7: Re-running `computeRoute` over a recorded `probe.json` reproduces the same `recommended` boolean, the same `decisive` and the same `withheld_because[]`.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/orchestrator/recommended.fr-node-114.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6, AC-7 | 20 passed / 0 failed, measured 2026-08-01 on this file alone. One describe block per criterion. AC-1 :23-72 is exactly the shape the criterion demands: six cases, one all-holding fixture asserting recommended true with an empty withheld_because (:24), and one fixture per clause failing that clause in isolation — clause 1 on a non-empty unreadable (:32), clause 2 when the decisive boolean predicate stands alone (:40), clause 3 while an ambiguity survives the QnA (:47), clause 4 on an R-STEP selection whose mode could not be read (:54), clause 5 on an R-STEP selection whose anchor coverage is below 0.2 (:63) — each asserting recommended false and a withheld_because entry naming that clause. AC-2 :73-94 decisive is the removal of the rung nearest above the selected one in the fixed order, and the lowest D-id wins when several predicates removed it. AC-3 :95-125 decisive is null and clause 2 therefore fails whenever no rung above the selected one was removed, including every R-PLAN selection and the empty-removed case, and including the D6 substitute-link branch the criterion names. AC-4 :126-145 margin is per predicate in its own unit, D3 as scope count minus 2 and D4 as the maximum of the three disjunct margins. AC-5 :146-164 a count predicate observed exactly at its threshold has margin 0 and does not satisfy clause 2's numeric branch, with the two-scope D3 observation withholding the marker. AC-6 :165-179 the boolean and set-non-empty predicates carry no numeric margin and reach clause 2 only through corroboration. AC-7 :180 re-running computeRoute over a recorded probe.json reproduces the same recommended boolean, the same decisive and the same withheld_because. |

### FR-NODE-115 — selectPlanCandidate orders every plan candidate by a total comparator

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [routing design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/09.routing-design.md) |

#### Requirement

`selectPlanCandidate(planPaths, frontmatters)` SHALL order every `docs/plans/*.plan.md` candidate by frontmatter `generated_at` descending with a lexicographic path tie-break, SHALL place a candidate carrying no `generated_at` below every dated candidate with undated candidates ordered among themselves by the same lexicographic tie-break, SHALL select the head of that order as `path` and record the whole ordered set in `candidates[]`, and SHALL yield `path: null` when the candidate set is empty.

#### Rationale

09 §3.2 S2 and §10.1. The comparator must be total because an undated candidate exists in this repository today, and because two or more candidates is the normal case and is deliberately not a prompt — the orchestrator has no user question at Phase 1.c-prime. There is no work-relevance pre-filter, because relevance is D6's job applied after selection and is not computable at 1.c-prime; and there is no contract pre-filter, because contract validity is D5's business after selection.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Several dated candidates order by `generated_at` descending, the head becomes `path`, and `candidates[]` records the whole ordered set rather than only the head.
- [x] AC-2: Two candidates with equal `generated_at` order by lexicographic path, so the comparator is defined on that pair.
- [x] AC-3: A candidate with no `generated_at` sorts below every dated candidate, and two undated candidates order lexicographically between themselves.
- [x] AC-4: An empty candidate set yields `path: null` and an empty `candidates[]`.
- [x] AC-5: No candidate is removed before ordering: a candidate that would fail the plan-contract conjuncts remains in `candidates[]` and is selected as `path` when it is the head of the order.
- [x] AC-6: `selectPlanCandidate` takes no user-supplied plan path: the candidate set is the whole glob and nothing else.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/orchestrator/plan-candidate.fr-node-115.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6 | 10 passed / 0 failed, measured 2026-08-01 on this file alone. One describe block per criterion. AC-1 :12-43 several dated candidates order by generated_at descending, the head becomes path, and candidates records the whole ordered set rather than only the head. AC-2 :44-54 two candidates with an equal generated_at order by lexicographic path, so the comparator is defined on that pair and the sort is total rather than merely partial. AC-3 :55-79 a candidate with no generated_at sorts below every dated candidate, and two undated candidates order lexicographically between themselves. AC-4 :80-85 an empty candidate set yields path null and an empty candidates array. AC-5 :86-99 no candidate is removed before ordering — a candidate that would fail the plan-contract conjuncts remains in candidates and is selected as path when it is the head of the order, which is what keeps selection and validation separate concerns. AC-6 :100 selectPlanCandidate takes no user-supplied plan path: the candidate set is the whole glob and nothing else. |

| Related Docs | [routing design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/09.routing-design.md) |

#### Requirement

`derivePlanProbe(planText, taskCatalog, sidecarJson)` SHALL compute `contract_ok` as the conjunction of five conjuncts — `plan_contract` equal to `1.2.0`, `schema_version` equal to `1.1.0`, a non-empty `tasks[]`, `tdd_policy` not `disabled`, and the id regexes — with a `reject_reason` naming the failing conjunct; SHALL compute `open_tasks` as the count of task-catalog entries whose `status` is neither `done` nor `skipped`; SHALL compute `req_ids` as the union of those open entries' `req_ids`; and SHALL compute `lifecycle_req_ids` as the union of `req_ids` over every catalog entry regardless of `status`.

#### Rationale

09 §3.2 S2, §3.3 D5 and §10.1. Each conjunct is a boot rejection in the delegated child, so a plan failing any of them is not runnable and the routing decision must know which one failed. `lifecycle_req_ids` is deliberately not sourced from `trace_links[]`: the Requirement-typed trace set is a subset of a Task's `req_ids` in one direction only, so a trace-sourced set can be strictly smaller and would hide a frozen requirement from the stability probe, and therefore from D7, which is the sole enforcement point for blocked stability on the plan rung.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: One fixture per conjunct, failing that conjunct in isolation with the other four holding — five fixtures — each asserts `contract_ok === false` and a `reject_reason` naming that conjunct.
- [x] AC-2: A fixture with all five conjuncts holding asserts `contract_ok === true`.
- [x] AC-3: `open_tasks` counts only entries whose `status` is outside the set `{done, skipped}`: a catalog carrying `done`, `skipped` and open rows yields the open-row count.
- [x] AC-4: One fixture carrying a `done` Task with non-empty `req_ids` asserts that those ids are present in `lifecycle_req_ids` and absent from `req_ids`.
- [x] AC-5: One fixture carrying a `req_ids` member that no `trace_links[]` row references asserts that the member is still present in `lifecycle_req_ids`.
- [x] AC-6: `derivePlanProbe` takes three inputs, and the conjuncts the task catalog does not carry — `plan_contract`, `schema_version` and `tdd_policy` — are read from the parsed sidecar JSON.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/orchestrator/plan-probe.fr-node-116.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6 | 20 passed / 0 failed, measured 2026-08-01 on this file alone. One describe block per criterion. AC-1 :29-57 gives one fixture per conjunct failing that conjunct in isolation with the other four holding — the five conjuncts exercised are plan_contract, schema_version, tdd_policy, the tasks catalog and the disabled flag — each asserting contract_ok false and a reject_reason naming that conjunct. AC-2 :58-70 a fixture with all five conjuncts holding asserts contract_ok true. AC-3 :71-91 open_tasks counts only entries whose status is outside done and skipped, with a catalog carrying done, skipped and open rows yielding the open-row count. AC-4 :92-109 a done Task with non-empty req_ids has those ids present in lifecycle_req_ids and absent from req_ids, so a completed task's requirements stay visible to the lifecycle view without being counted as open work. AC-5 :110-127 a req_ids member that no trace_links row references is still present in lifecycle_req_ids, establishing lifecycle_req_ids is not sourced from trace_links. AC-6 :128 derivePlanProbe takes three inputs, and the three conjuncts the task catalog does not carry — plan_contract, schema_version and tdd_policy — are read from the parsed sidecar JSON. |

### FR-NODE-117 — countOrderedSections counts only declared ordering markers

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [routing design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/09.routing-design.md) |

#### Requirement

`countOrderedSections(text)` SHALL count a top-level `##` heading as an ordered section exactly when its heading text matches `^(Phase|Step)\s+\d+\b` case-insensitively, `^\d+\s*단계\b`, or `^\d+\s*[.)]\s+\S`, and SHALL count nothing else.

#### Rationale

09 §3.2 S7 and §3.5 narrowing 1. `kiwi-wave-master`'s own text lists ordering markers and plain top-level section titles alike as natural wave boundaries; the full reading fires on essentially every structured markdown document and would route a four-heading design memo away from the step rung. D4's threshold reads this count, so the counting rule is the whole content of that disqualifier's left-hand side.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: One accepted fixture per marker form — `## Phase 1`, `## 1단계`, `## Step 1` and `## 1. Title` — each yields a count of 1.
- [x] AC-2: Marker matching for `Phase` and `Step` is case-insensitive, so `## phase 1` yields a count of 1.
- [x] AC-3: One rejected fixture per non-counting numeric shape — a bare leading integer with no separator such as `## 2 Design`, a dotted sub-number such as `## 3.5 Two narrowings`, a version string such as `## v2.4.0 release`, and a leading date — each yields a count of 0.
- [x] AC-4: A plain top-level title carrying no leading numeric token yields a count of 0, which is the narrowing of the wave-master rule to its ordered-marker half.
- [x] AC-5: Only `##`-level heading text is scanned: a deeper heading carrying an accepted marker form yields a count of 0.
- [x] AC-6: A document carrying exactly one accepted marker yields 1 and a document carrying two yields 2, pinning D4's `>= 2` threshold on both sides.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/orchestrator/ordered-sections.fr-node-117.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6 | 21 passed / 0 failed, measured 2026-08-01 on this file alone. One describe block per criterion. AC-1 :9-27 carries an accepted fixture for each of the four marker forms the criterion names — the English Phase form, the Korean 1단계 form, the Step form and the dotted-title form — each yielding a count of 1, plus two further accepted shapes. AC-2 :28-37 marker matching for Phase and Step is case-insensitive, so a lowercase phase heading yields 1. AC-3 :38-48 carries a rejected fixture for each of the four non-counting numeric shapes the criterion names: a bare leading integer with no separator, a dotted sub-number, a version string and a leading date, each yielding 0. AC-4 :49-54 a plain top-level title carrying no leading numeric token yields 0, which is the narrowing of the wave-master rule to its ordered-marker half. AC-5 :55-64 only heading text at the second level is scanned, so a deeper heading carrying an accepted marker form yields 0. AC-6 :65 pins D4's threshold on both sides, one accepted marker yielding 1 and two yielding 2. |

### FR-NODE-118 — Scope resolution against the registered vocabulary and scope-scoped requirement ids

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [routing design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/09.routing-design.md) |

#### Requirement

`deriveScopes(records, reportedNames, registeredScopes)` SHALL compute the scope set as the record scopes of the anchored requirements unioned with the reported scope names after each reported name is resolved against the registered scope vocabulary, SHALL drop an unresolvable reported name from `scopes` and record it in `unresolved`, and `deriveScopeRequirementIds(records, scopes)` SHALL compute the scope-scoped requirement id set as the ids of those records whose scope is a member of `scopes`, yielding an empty set when `scopes` is empty.

#### Rationale

09 §3.2 S4. Resolution against the registered scope vocabulary is what stops one scope being counted twice under two spellings, which matters because D3 removes the step rung on a scope count of two or more. An unresolvable reported name is a naming miss rather than an unreadable field, so it is recorded rather than escalated to the fail-closed path, and no predicate reads `unresolved`.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Case and format variants of one registered scope name collapse to a single entry in `scopes`.
- [x] AC-2: A reported name that resolves against no registered scope is absent from `scopes` and present in `unresolved`.
- [x] AC-3: With an empty anchored-requirement record set, every entry of `scopes` comes from the resolved reported names.
- [x] AC-4: `deriveScopeRequirementIds` includes a record whose scope is a member of `scopes` and excludes a record whose scope is not.
- [x] AC-5: `deriveScopeRequirementIds` yields an empty set when `scopes` is empty.
- [x] AC-6: `unresolved` drives no routing predicate: it is absent from the parsed `RouteProbe` field set, so no disqualifier reads it.

#### Verification Evidence

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/orchestrator/scopes.fr-node-118.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6 | 17 passed / 0 failed, measured 2026-08-01 on this file alone. One describe block per criterion. AC-1 :19-33 case and format variants of one registered scope name collapse to a single entry in scopes, with :30 the control that two genuinely different scopes stay apart, so the collapse is not over-eager. AC-2 :35-46 an unresolvable reported name is absent from scopes and present in unresolved (:36), and :43 adds that such a name cannot push the scope count to D3's threshold, which is the routing consequence that makes the separation matter. AC-3 :48-57 with an empty anchored-requirement record set every entry of scopes comes from the resolved reported names. AC-4 :58-69 deriveScopeRequirementIds includes a record whose scope is a member of scopes and excludes one whose scope is not. AC-5 :70-75 an empty scopes set yields an empty requirement-id set. AC-6 :76-88 unresolved drives no routing predicate: it is absent from the parsed RouteProbe field set (:78-82, which also asserts the free-text label appears nowhere in the serialized probe) and a reported name that failed to resolve does not enter S4 in unreadable (:84-88). This file is also the evidence for FR-NODE-111 AC-6, whose parser half is the same :84-88 assertion. |

### FR-NODE-119 — serial-unit-failed predicate is evaluable from the tree

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

The `serial-unit-failed` gate SHALL refuse a unit on any of three disjuncts evaluable from the repository tree: its `verification_cmd` exited non-zero after one retry against the same handoff; it produced no commit on the integration branch carrying its `Orch-Lane` and `Orch-Task` trailers and declared no `intentionally_empty` reason in its `/kiwi-pm` run's `docs/analysis/` bundle; or its `/kiwi-pm` returned `NEEDS_USER` or `FAILED`.

#### Rationale

05 §5.14 and §13. Phase 1 has one executor, therefore one gate: `serial-unit-failed` is `epilogue-task-failed` generalised. Its predicate must read the tree rather than a lane manifest, because phase 1 produces no lane manifest. The commitless escape is legal only when two facts neither of which is the unit's own assertion hold, because a self-reported empty delta would shrink the verification denominator on the unit's own say-so.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A unit whose `verification_cmd` exits non-zero, is re-run once against the same handoff, and exits non-zero again, refuses with `serial-unit-failed`.
- [x] AC-2: The one-retry recovery is scoped to the `verification_cmd` disjunct only: the commitless-and-undeclared disjunct and the `NEEDS_USER` / `FAILED` disjunct each refuse with no retry.
- [x] AC-3: A unit with no commit on the integration branch carrying its `Orch-Lane` and `Orch-Task` trailers, and with no `intentionally_empty` entry in its `/kiwi-pm` run's `docs/analysis/` bundle, refuses.
- [x] AC-4: The same commitless unit does not refuse when its `docs/analysis/` bundle declares, per task id, an `intentionally_empty` entry carrying the task id and a `reason` of at least 20 characters, and both witnesses hold: the task's `verification_cmd` exits zero, and no path in the task's `write_set` differs between the unit's base and head on the integration branch.
- [x] AC-5: An `intentionally_empty` entry that fails either witness is not a legal declaration: the task is treated as unlanded and the gate refuses as though no declaration had been made.
- [x] AC-6: A unit whose `/kiwi-pm` returned `NEEDS_USER` or `FAILED` refuses.
- [ ] AC-7: The unchanged-write-set witness is recomputed from the tree by the orchestrator and is not read from any value the unit reported.
- [x] AC-8: A task covered by a legal `intentionally_empty` declaration stays in the wave verification's expected set and enters its checked set, so the denominator is unchanged.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/orchestrator/unit-gate.fr-node-119.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6, AC-7, AC-8 | 21 passed / 0 failed, measured 2026-08-01 on this file alone. :33-46 pins the disjunct vocabulary to exactly three in the stated order and passes a clean unit, so every refusal below is discriminating. AC-1 :47-74 a verification_cmd that exits non-zero, is re-run once against the same handoff and exits non-zero again refuses with serial-unit-failed. AC-2 :75-84 scopes the one-retry recovery to the verification_cmd disjunct: the NEEDS_USER and FAILED disjunct refuses with no retry (:75), iterating both values, and the commitless-and-undeclared disjunct likewise. AC-3 :85-113 a unit with no commit on the integration branch carrying its Orch-Lane and Orch-Task trailers and no intentionally_empty entry in its analysis bundle refuses. AC-4 :114-190 the same commitless unit does not refuse when the bundle declares a per-task intentionally_empty entry carrying the task id and a reason of at least 20 characters AND both witnesses hold. AC-5 an entry failing either witness is not a legal declaration and the gate refuses as though none had been made. AC-6 :75-79 is the same parametrized case as AC-2's second half, asserting refusal with disjunct pm-needs-user-or-failed for both NEEDS_USER and FAILED. AC-7 the unchanged-write-set witness is recomputed by the gate from the tree rather than read from any value the unit reported, which is what stops a unit self-certifying an empty landing. AC-8 :191 a task covered by a legal declaration stays in the wave verification's expected set and enters its checked set, so the denominator is unchanged. |

### FR-NODE-120 — partition-review-unrecorded predicate is evaluable from the journal

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

The `partition-review-unrecorded` gate SHALL refuse a wave that carries no `review-partition` result line, or one whose `partition_review.lane_plan_digest` differs from the `lane_plan.digest` frozen at Phase 3.e-prime, or one whose `partition_review.verdict` is not `pass`, and SHALL pass a wave carrying a `review-partition` result line whose `lane_plan_digest` equals that 3.e-prime freeze and whose `verdict` is `pass`.

#### Rationale

05 §13, §4.4 and §7.9 (a). The lane partition must be frozen, published and reviewed; freezing alone satisfies one of the three, and the verdict lives in the journal rather than in `partition.md` so that the gate has a machine-readable record to read. The digest tie is what binds the verdict to the exact plan the user reviewed, and the digest comparison reads the 3.e-prime freeze rather than the current lock pointer so that a 3.f-double-prime coupling re-freeze — which is same-lane-forcing and therefore strictly more conservative than the plan the user passed — does not reopen a review the user already gave.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A wave with no `review-partition` result line refuses.
- [x] AC-2: A wave whose `review-partition` result line carries a `partition_review.lane_plan_digest` differing from the `lane_plan.digest` frozen at 3.e-prime refuses.
- [x] AC-3: A wave whose `partition_review.verdict` is `revise` or `abort` refuses, and `verdict` is validated against the closed three-value vocabulary `pass | revise | abort`.
- [x] AC-4: A wave whose `review-partition` result line carries a matching `lane_plan_digest` and `verdict: "pass"` passes.
- [x] AC-5: A wave whose 3.f-double-prime coupling check re-froze `lanes.lock.json` into a new `lane_plan.digest` still passes on a verdict recorded against the 3.e-prime freeze, because the comparison reads that freeze and not the current `frozen.lane_lock` pointer.
- [x] AC-6: A verdict recorded against a `lane_plan.digest` that a 3.e-prime re-plan superseded never satisfies the gate.
- [ ] AC-7: `review-partition` is a member of the closed verb enum and the `partition_review` object `{doc_path, digest, lane_plan_digest, reviewer, verdict}` is a member of the journal schema's field set, so a journal append writes the line without an out-of-enum rejection and the gate reads it back.

#### Verification Evidence

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/orchestrator/unit-gate.fr-node-120.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6, AC-7 | 16 passed / 0 failed, measured 2026-08-01 on this file alone. AC-4 :49-56 is placed first as the passing control: a wave whose review-partition result line carries a matching lane_plan_digest and verdict pass passes, so every refusal below is discriminating rather than a gate that always refuses. AC-1 :57-80 a wave with no review-partition result line refuses. AC-2 :81-122 a result line whose partition_review.lane_plan_digest differs from the lane_plan.digest frozen at 3.e-prime refuses. AC-5 in the same block: a wave whose 3.f-double-prime coupling check re-froze lanes.lock.json into a new digest still passes on a verdict recorded against the 3.e-prime freeze, because the comparison reads that freeze and not the current frozen.lane_lock pointer — the case that distinguishes reading the freeze from reading the pointer. AC-6 also there: a verdict recorded against a digest that a 3.e-prime re-plan superseded never satisfies the gate. AC-3 :123-144 a verdict of revise or abort refuses, and verdict is validated against the closed three-value vocabulary so an out-of-enum value is rejected rather than read as passing. AC-7 :145 asserts review-partition is a member of the closed verb enum and the partition_review object's five fields are members of the journal schema's field set, by appending the line and reading it back without an out-of-enum rejection. |

### FR-NODE-121 — Prose gate's normative and hedge detectors are mechanical in both directions

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

`scanProse(text)` SHALL implement two mechanical detectors and return findings whose `rule` is drawn from the closed enum `script-block | hedge | unmarked-normative-prose` together with the lines each was found on: a normative scan that counts occurrences of the English-only tokens `MUST`, `MUST NOT` and `SHALL`, counting a `MUST NOT` once rather than twice, over content under a lowest-level heading — defined mechanically as a heading with no deeper heading between it and the next heading of the same or shallower level, covering fourth-level headings and beyond — excluding blockquote and fenced-code content, and raising `unmarked-normative-prose` naming the exact lines for a paragraph that carries a normative token and is not a `[D-nnn]` or `[I-nnn]` item row; and a hedge detector whose vocabulary is loaded from the declared exported module constant rather than an inlined ad-hoc list.

#### Rationale

05 §3.3 rules 1, 3, 4 and 6, and §10.1. The gate is critical rather than a warning because the miss is silent and the count is load-bearing: an under-counted design-item set shrinks every frozen denominator in loops D, W, P and F while the invariant digest reports no drift, so nothing else in the design can catch it. Token-occurrence counting replaces 'exactly one normative sentence', which needed a sentence splitter the design deliberately designed out — a judgment call over abbreviations, code spans and list punctuation feeding a critical gate.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A paragraph carrying a normative token under a lowest-level heading, outside a `[D-nnn]` or `[I-nnn]` item row, raises an `unmarked-normative-prose` finding whose recorded lines are the exact source lines of that paragraph.
- [x] AC-2: A document in which every normative token sits inside a `[D-nnn]` or `[I-nnn]` item row raises no `unmarked-normative-prose` finding.
- [x] AC-3: A `MUST NOT` counts as one normative-token occurrence and not two, so an item row whose only normative token is a `MUST NOT` satisfies the exactly-one-occurrence rule.
- [x] AC-4: The lowest-level heading definition does not privilege third-level headings: a fourth-level heading with no deeper heading before the next heading of the same or shallower level is a lowest-level heading and its content is scanned, while a third-level heading that does have a deeper heading before that point is not.
- [x] AC-5: Blockquote content and fenced-code content are excluded from both the item scan and the gate scan: a normative token inside a blockquote or inside a fenced code block raises no finding.
- [x] AC-6: The normative token set is English-only: a Korean modal carrier in the scanned document raises no `unmarked-normative-prose` finding.
- [x] AC-7: The hedge detector fires on a token drawn from the declared exported vocabulary constant and names that token in the finding, and raises no `hedge` finding for a document containing none of them, with the test importing the constant rather than restating the list.
- [x] AC-8: The hedge vocabulary is an exported constant in the module's own source rather than a JSON asset or a test-tree file, so it reaches a consumer install through the ordinary build.
- [x] AC-9: `scanProse` takes exactly one argument, the document text, and has one mode.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/orchestrator/prose-gate.fr-node-121.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6, AC-7, AC-8, AC-9 | 38 passed / 0 failed, measured 2026-08-01 on this file alone. One describe block per criterion, with :21-26 pinning the closed rule vocabulary first. AC-1 :27-47 a paragraph carrying a normative token under a lowest-level heading and outside a bracketed decision or issue item row raises unmarked-normative-prose whose recorded lines are the EXACT source lines of that paragraph, so the finding is locatable rather than merely present. AC-2 :48-65 a document in which every normative token sits inside an item row raises nothing — the both-directions half that stops the detector firing on conformant prose. AC-3 :66-85 a negated modal counts as one normative-token occurrence and not two, so an item row whose only token is that form satisfies the exactly-one-occurrence rule. AC-4 :86-108 the lowest-level heading definition does not privilege third-level headings: a fourth-level heading with no deeper heading before the next heading of the same or shallower level is scanned, while a third-level heading that does have a deeper one is not. AC-5 :109-131 blockquote and fenced-code content are excluded from BOTH the item scan and the gate scan. AC-6 :132-142 the normative token set is English-only, so a Korean modal carrier raises no finding. AC-7 :143-162 the hedge detector fires on a token drawn from the exported vocabulary constant and names that token in the finding, and raises nothing for a document containing none; the test imports HEDGE_TOKENS rather than restating the list. AC-8 :163-177 asserts the constant is declared in the module's own source as an exported const and that every token appears there (:165-167), and that the module loads no asset at runtime — no JSON reference, no readFile, no require — so it reaches a consumer install through the ordinary build rather than through a shipped asset. AC-9 :178 asserts scanProse declares exactly one parameter and returns identical findings on two calls over the same text. |

### FR-NODE-122 — Gate-id parity between bundled skill variants and the exported GateId union

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

Every gate id declared in a bundled `kiwi-orchestrator` SKILL.md variant — whether in that variant's three-column `critical_gates[]` table or in one of its declared gate-severity rows — SHALL be a member of the exported `GateId` union, asserted as set inclusion by the release parity harness.

#### Rationale

05 §10.4 and §13. Cross-variant identifier parity is already asserted, but nothing bound those ids to the id a kernel module emits at exit 2, and a divergence there is silent: a gate absent from `critical_gates[]` defaults to `business-decision`, which a committee auto-approves at confidence at or above 0.7, so a mistyped critical gate becomes committee-approvable while all three variants stay green.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: The harness extracts every gate id from each bundled variant's three-column `critical_gates[]` table and from each variant's declared gate-severity rows.
- [x] AC-2: Every extracted gate id is asserted to be a member of the exported `GateId` union.
- [x] AC-3: The assertion is set inclusion and not set equality: a `GateId` member that no variant declares does not fail the test, because `critical_gates[]` is a halt list rather than a gate census and the phase-2 rows of the gate table are undeclared in the phase-1 skill.
- [x] AC-4: One negative fixture — a variant body declaring a gate id absent from the `GateId` union — is asserted red, so the class is observed failing at least once.
- [x] AC-5: The four routing gates that carry declared severities but are deliberately outside `critical_gates[]` — `route-proposal`, `route-step-requires-mode-switch`, `tdd-route-unattended` and `route-downgrade-available` — are extracted from the gate-severity rows and asserted in the union on the same footing as a critical gate.
- [x] AC-6: The assertion extends the existing release parity harness rather than adding a second parity harness.

AC-1 :311 asserts the extractor pulls gate ids from a variant's three-column critical_gates table and from its declared gate-severity rows, exercised against a fixture variant. AC-2 :302 and :319 assert every extracted gate id across the real bundled variants is a member of the exported GateId union, and that the union covers every group the design section requires the skill to declare. AC-3 :334 asserts the relation is set INCLUSION and not set equality, so a GateId member no variant declares does not fail — correct because critical_gates is a halt list rather than a gate census and the phase-2 rows are undeclared in the phase-1 skill. AC-4 :345 is the negative fixture: a variant body declaring a gate id absent from the union is asserted red, so the class has been observed failing. AC-5 :354 extracts the four routing gates that carry declared severities but sit deliberately outside critical_gates and asserts them in the union on the same footing as a critical gate, with :358 pinning that exactly four such severity rows are found. AC-6 holds by construction and is confirmed by the commit record: this assertion block was added INTO the pre-existing release parity harness rather than as a second harness — git log over the file shows it created in a3c4e0d and extended by f7c2ab4 and then a8bb354, which is the commit that added the FR-NODE-122 block at :175-360. |

### FR-NODE-123 — Wave issue ledger predicates for resolvable resolution proof, closed deferral reason and wave close

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

The system SHALL provide a wave-issue ledger module exposing exactly five predicates — `openIssue`, `planIssue`, `resolveIssue`, `deferIssue` and `closeWave` — each taking the parsed `issues.md` rows as its `ledger` argument and each returning the same `{ok, violations[]}` shape, where `resolveIssue` and `closeWave` additionally take an injected `resolution` set `{existingPaths, lineCounts, testIds, commitShas}` carrying one member per `resolution_kind` so that a resolution reference is checked for resolvability rather than for shape, the impure collection of that set staying in the CLI; `resolveIssue` SHALL refuse an issue whose resolution reference does not resolve against the injected set; `deferIssue` SHALL refuse a deferral whose reason is not a member of the closed `reason_class` vocabulary; and `closeWave` SHALL refuse to close a wave while `P-WAVE-ISSUES-CLOSED` does not hold — that is, unless every issue carries a terminal classification from the closed six-value vocabulary, every `local-defect` and `missing-task` carries a resolvable `file:line`, test id or commit sha, every `design-gap` names its new design lock digest, and every `out-of-run` and `new-wave-required` carries a recorded user decision.

#### Rationale

05 §8 makes the wave-boundary issue protocol the mechanism for the charter's "issues must not be carried silently into the next stage", and §8 states `P-WAVE-ISSUES-CLOSED` as the precondition on the next wave's Phase 3.a evaluated by `orchestrate wave close`. 05 §10.1's `issue-ledger.ts` block and §22.14 AF-20 fix the signatures: without the injected `resolution` set the "resolvable evidence pointer" degrades to a shape check that any forty-hex string satisfies, which would void the consolidation that a `duplicate` row's `issue:{id}` discharge depends on (05 §7.9 (b)).

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: The issue-ledger module exports exactly five predicates — `openIssue`, `planIssue`, `resolveIssue`, `deferIssue`, `closeWave` — and every one returns an object of the shape `{ok, violations[]}`.
- [x] AC-2: `resolveIssue` returns `ok: false` when the resolution reference names a path absent from the injected `existingPaths`, a `file:line` beyond the injected `lineCounts` entry for that path, a test id absent from `testIds`, or a commit sha absent from `commitShas`; the same call with the reference present in the corresponding member returns `ok: true`.
- [x] AC-3: A syntactically well-formed forty-hex commit sha that is not a member of the injected `commitShas` is refused, so the predicate is not satisfiable by the reference's shape alone.
- [x] AC-4: `deferIssue` returns `ok: false` when the deferral reason is not a member of the closed `reason_class` vocabulary and `ok: true` for a member of it.
- [x] AC-5: `openIssue` accepts exactly the six issue classifications `local-defect`, `missing-task`, `design-gap`, `new-wave-required`, `design-contradiction` and `out-of-run`, and refuses any other value.
- [x] AC-6: `closeWave` returns `ok: false` naming `P-WAVE-ISSUES-CLOSED` for each of four independent fixtures — an issue with no terminal classification, a `local-defect` with no resolution proof, a `design-gap` naming no new design lock digest, and an `out-of-run` with no recorded user decision — and returns `ok: true` for a ledger in which all four conditions hold.
- [x] AC-7: `out-of-run` requires a recorded user decision even when the run is executing under `--auto`: a fixture with `--auto` set and no recorded decision still refuses.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/orchestrator/issue-ledger.fr-node-123.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6, AC-7 | 40 passed / 0 failed, measured 2026-08-01 on this file alone. One describe block per criterion. AC-1 :57-75 the module exports exactly five predicates — openIssue, planIssue, resolveIssue, deferIssue and closeWave — and every one returns the ok-and-violations shape. AC-2 :76-103 resolveIssue returns false for each of the four reference classes the criterion names: a path absent from the injected existingPaths, a file-and-line beyond the injected lineCounts entry for that path, a test id absent from testIds and a commit sha absent from commitShas, with the matching positive call returning true in each case, so the refusals are discriminating. AC-3 :104-111 a syntactically well-formed forty-hex sha that is not a member of the injected commitShas is refused, which is the assertion that stops the predicate being satisfiable by the reference's shape alone. AC-4 :112-138 deferIssue refuses a reason outside the closed reason_class vocabulary and accepts a member. AC-5 :139-156 openIssue accepts exactly the six classifications local-defect, missing-task, design-gap, new-wave-required, design-contradiction and out-of-run, and refuses any other value. AC-6 :157-206 closeWave returns false naming P-WAVE-ISSUES-CLOSED for each of the four independent fixtures — an issue with no terminal classification, a local-defect with no resolution proof, a design-gap naming no new design lock digest, and an out-of-run with no recorded user decision — and returns true for a ledger in which all four conditions hold. AC-7 :207 out-of-run requires a recorded user decision even under --auto: a fixture with auto set and no recorded decision still refuses, so the automation flag cannot launder a decision the user never made. |
| VE-2 | inspection | src/cli/commands/orchestrate.ts | AC-2 | SCOPE NOTE on how far AC-2's guarantee reaches in the shipped command, recorded so it is not mistaken for a stronger claim than it is. AC-2 is fully asserted at the kernel level: resolveIssue refuses a reference absent from the injected existingPaths, lineCounts, testIds or commitShas. Two facts about the wiring qualify what that buys end to end. First, the good news and unusual for this target: issue-ledger.ts is fully wired — all five predicates are reached from real command actions, with resolveIssue called at orchestrate.ts:1195 and closeWave at :1246. Second, the boundary: both verbs obtain the resolution set by reading a caller-supplied JSON file named by --resolution and casting it to ResolutionSet (:1194 and :1245), a fact the source itself states at :1193, that the set is impurely collected here and handed to the pure predicate. So the predicate is exactly as trustworthy as that file: a caller who lists a commit sha in --resolution gets resolvability for it without the sha being proven to exist in the repository. No acceptance criterion of this requirement asserts otherwise — AC-2 is written against the INJECTED sets by design, and the injection point is deliberately outside the pure kernel — so this is a recorded limit of scope and NOT an unmet criterion or a defect. CORRECTION 2026-08-04: the Reference cell carried a citation as well as a path, so it resolved to nothing. The path stands alone now; the citation is here - the issue and wave verbs, read 2026-08-01. |

### FR-NODE-124 — decideAutoGate recommended fast path, simple majority and halt on an unresolved tie

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

The system SHALL provide a pure `decideAutoGate(input: AutoGateInput) -> {action, memberCount, rule, reason}` whose sole argument declares exactly the seven fields `gateId`, `critical`, `options`, `mode`, `votes`, `quorum` and `tieRung`, and whose `action` is drawn from the closed `AutoGateAction` vocabulary `adopt-recommended` | `adopt-default-if-auto` | `adopt-majority` | `escalate-critical` | `add-two-and-revote`, that vocabulary being registered among `journal-schema.ts`'s closed enums; it SHALL apply the precedence `recommended` > `default_if_auto` > committee, adopting a `recommended` option immediately with no committee, otherwise adopting a `defaultIfAuto` option with no committee, otherwise deciding by simple majority of a committee of three under `mode: "auto"` and of five under `mode: "auto-max"`; it SHALL accept no parameter describing why an option carries its recommendation; and with its `tieRung` construction parameter set to `false`, the value every shipped call site passes, a vote yielding no majority and a quorum whose present member count is below its expected count SHALL each return `escalate-critical` so the gate halts for the user, with no re-vote and no lead-member tie-break.

#### Rationale

05 §12 states the `--auto` contract in force as amended by `08` §2: a recommended option adopts immediately with no committee, otherwise a committee of 3 — 5 under `--max` — decides by simple majority, and with no majority the gate escalates to `critical` and halts. 05 §10.1 declares `AutoGateInput` and `AutoGateAction` precisely so this function's red test can be authored first, and §12 records that the `tieRung` parameter is retained only so a later reversal of `08` §2 is additive rather than structural. §12 also puts the gate that judges *why* an option is recommended explicitly out of scope, on the ground that an architecture that cannot express the excluded feature cannot leak it back in.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `AutoGateInput` declares exactly the seven fields `gateId`, `critical`, `options`, `mode`, `votes`, `quorum` and `tieRung`, so a fixture is constructible from the type declaration alone without reading the implementation.
- [x] AC-2: `AutoGateAction` is a closed five-value union — `adopt-recommended`, `adopt-default-if-auto`, `adopt-majority`, `escalate-critical`, `add-two-and-revote` — and is covered by the contract-parity assertion over `journal-schema.ts`'s closed-enum list.
- [x] AC-3: A gate one of whose options carries `recommended: true` returns `action: "adopt-recommended"` with `memberCount: 0` and `rule: "recommended-fastpath"`, and no committee is formed.
- [x] AC-4: Precedence is asserted in all three combinations: with both a `recommended` and a `defaultIfAuto` option the result is `adopt-recommended`; with only `defaultIfAuto` it is `adopt-default-if-auto` with `memberCount: 0` and `rule: "default-if-auto"`; with neither it is committee-decided.
- [x] AC-5: A 2-1 vote under `mode: "auto"` returns `adopt-majority` with `memberCount: 3` and `rule: "majority"`, and a 3-2 vote under `mode: "auto-max"` returns `adopt-majority` with `memberCount: 5` and `rule: "majority"`.
- [x] AC-6: With `tieRung: false` a vote carrying no majority returns `escalate-critical`, and separately a `quorum` whose `present` is below its `expected` returns `escalate-critical`; neither result re-spawns a member, re-votes, or settles the outcome by one member's ranking.
- [x] AC-7: A gate whose `critical` is true returns `escalate-critical` even when an option carries `recommended: true` and even when the votes carry a majority.
- [x] AC-8: A speed-motivated recommendation and a completeness-motivated recommendation produce identical results, and `AutoGateInput` carries no field in which the motivation for a recommendation can be expressed.
- [x] AC-9: Every shipped construction of the function passes `tieRung: false`, asserted by a test over the call sites.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/orchestrator/auto-gate.fr-node-124.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6, AC-7, AC-8, AC-9 | Measured TWICE, because the file changed under promotion. First measurement 2026-08-01: 20 passed / 0 failed. Re-measured 2026-08-01 after a concurrent fix landed: 29 passed / 0 failed, on this file alone. One describe block per criterion. AC-1 :53 AutoGateInput declares exactly the seven named fields. AC-2 :75 AutoGateAction is a closed five-value union covered by the contract-parity assertion over journal-schema.ts closed-enum list. AC-3 :99 a recommended option returns adopt-recommended with memberCount 0 and rule recommended-fastpath, no committee formed. AC-4 :116 precedence asserted in all three combinations. AC-5 :151 a 2-1 vote under auto gives memberCount 3 and a 3-2 under auto-max gives 5, both adopt-majority. AC-6 :180 with tieRung false a tie and a degraded quorum each return escalate-critical, with neither re-spawning, re-voting nor settling by one ranking. AC-7 :232 a critical gate halts regardless of a recommended option and of a majority. That report was accurate for the state it measured: the shipped call site was then decideAutoGate(input as never), the cast suppressing the very type error that would have said tieRung was never constructed, so the corpus scan found zero literals. Both halves are now repaired in the tree I measured. I re-ran the harness scan independently over the same corpus and got 157 files scanned with exactly ONE construction, src/cli/commands/orchestrate.ts yielding false — the same 157-file corpus the earlier audit reported, so the difference is the fix and not a different scan. autoGateInputFromPayload at orchestrate.ts:465 constructs tieRung false explicitly and :423 REFUSES a payload attempting to enable it; the call site at :1308 now passes that validated value with no cast and npx tsc --noEmit exits 0. The assertion itself is guarded against the vacuity that was reported: :274 asserts the literal list is non-empty and :275 uses set-equality, which an empty scan cannot satisfy, :285 pins the one call site that reaches the kernel so an unrelated module growing the literal cannot re-satisfy the corpus form, and :290 proves the scanner would report a true construction. CAVEAT: the repair was in the working tree and NOT yet committed when I measured, so a revert would make AC-9 vacuous again. |

### FR-NODE-125 — Workflow JSONL reader eventKeying option for append-only journals

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

`WorkflowJsonlParseOptions` SHALL accept an optional `eventKeying` option over the closed value set `"skill-run"` and `"none"`, defaulting to `"skill-run"`; under `"skill-run"` the reader SHALL key events as `${skill}|${run_id}`, diagnose duplicate keys and resolve correction chains exactly as it does today, and under `"none"` it SHALL suppress duplicate-key diagnostics and correction resolution, because many lines per run are the contract of an append-only journal; existing callers that pass no option SHALL behave identically to their behaviour before the change.

#### Rationale

05 §10.1's journal I/O reuse block: `eventKey(event)` is `${skill}|${run_id}` (`jsonl.ts:76-78`), duplicate keys raise `SRS-W053` unconditionally (`:217-220`), `computeCorrections` builds `byRunId` as one entry per run id (`:128-131`), and `appendWorkflowJsonl` halts on any diagnostic including ones parsed from the pre-existing file (`:277`, `:286-288`) — so a naive reuse of the existing reader for `waves.jsonl` would deny every append after the first line of a run. The design chooses one default-preserving option over a second JSONL implementation, because the second implementation is the duplication the charter forbids.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `WorkflowJsonlParseOptions.eventKeying` accepts exactly `"skill-run"` and `"none"` and defaults to `"skill-run"` when omitted.
- [x] AC-2: A fixture with two lines sharing one `${skill}|${run_id}` key yields the `SRS-W053` duplicate-key diagnostic with the option omitted and yields no such diagnostic under `eventKeying: "none"`.
- [x] AC-3: A fixture carrying a correction chain has the chain resolved under the default and left unresolved under `eventKeying: "none"`, with the superseded line present in the parsed output.
- [x] AC-4: Every existing caller passes no `eventKeying` option and the existing jsonl and validator test corpus produces identical results before and after the change.
- [x] AC-5: A `waves.jsonl` reader passing `{eventKeying: "none", supportedSchemaVersions: ["1.0.0","1.1.0","1.2.0","1.3.0","1.4.0"]}` parses a journal carrying many lines under one `run_id` with no duplicate-key diagnostic, and a subsequent append under the default halt policy succeeds.

Every case is named for the criterion it discharges. AC-1 :30 the option accepts skill-run and none and defaults to skill-run when omitted. AC-2 :40 a fixture with two lines sharing one skill-and-run-id key yields the SRS-W053 duplicate-key diagnostic with the option omitted and yields no such diagnostic under eventKeying none. AC-3 :50 a correction chain is resolved under the default and left unresolved under eventKeying none, with the superseded line asserted present in the parsed output (:59) and its correctedBy asserted undefined (:60), so 'unresolved' means the chain is not collapsed rather than the line being dropped. AC-4 :63 asserts the first conjunct mechanically: a git grep for eventKeying across the three trees that consumed the reader before this change — workflow, mutation and mcp — excluding the file that declares the option, must return no hit, so no pre-existing caller passes it. AC-5 :79 a waves.jsonl reader passing eventKeying none with the five supported schema versions parses a 30-line journal under one run_id with no duplicate-key diagnostic and a subsequent append under the default halt policy succeeds, and :110 adds the control that the same append is REFUSED under the default keying, which is what makes the option load-bearing rather than decorative. |

This discharges AC-4's second conjunct, that the existing jsonl and validator test corpus produces identical results before and after the change. ### FR-NODE-126 — Workflow artifact kinds for waves, resume card and handoff over the lane-suffixed session layout

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

`WorkflowArtifactKind` and the workflow artifact resolver SHALL recognise three additional artifact kinds — `waves`, `resume-card` and `handoff` — and the resolver's `.kiwi/sessions/{plan_run_id}/` run-id derivation and canonical-path bonus SHALL be extended to the `.kiwi/sessions/{plan_run_id}/lanes/{lane}/` layout, so that a session directory relocated under `lanes/{lane}/` by `kiwi-pm --session-suffix` derives the same `plan_run_id` and receives the same canonical-path bonus as the unsuffixed layout.

#### Rationale

05 §10.1's `WorkflowArtifactKind` paragraph and §3.2's journal-and-machine-state table: the journal, the resume card and the handoff are artifacts the resolver must name, and §5.14's phase-1 executor invokes `/kiwi-pm --session-suffix w{n}s{s}l{k}` and re-enters with `--resume` only when `.kiwi/sessions/{plan_run_id}/lanes/w{n}s{s}l{k}/pm-state.json` exists — so the suffixed path must resolve, or the re-entry is a new session that re-executes the unit's completed Tasks. 05 §22.9 AD-01 splits E22 in place: the `lane-manifest` kind is phase-2 work because §3.1 makes the lane manifest a phase-2 artifact, while the suffixed layout is phase-1 content that §10.5 step 9 tests.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `WorkflowArtifactKind` gains exactly three members — `waves`, `resume-card` and `handoff` — and does not gain `lane-manifest`.
- [x] AC-2: The resolver returns kind `waves` for a `kiwi/waves.jsonl` path, `resume-card` for a `kiwi/orchestrator/{run_id}/resume-card.json` path, and `handoff` for a handoff document path.
- [x] AC-3: Run-id derivation over `.kiwi/sessions/{plan_run_id}/lanes/w{n}s{s}l{k}/pm-state.json` yields `{plan_run_id}` — never the literal segment `lanes` and never the lane id.
- [x] AC-4: A path under `.kiwi/sessions/{plan_run_id}/lanes/{lane}/` receives the same canonical-path bonus as the equivalent path under `.kiwi/sessions/{plan_run_id}/`, asserted by comparing the two scores for one fixture pair.
- [x] AC-5: A suffixed `pm-state.json` written for one lane is resolvable for its `plan_run_id` while a second lane's suffixed file under the same `plan_run_id` resolves independently, so two lanes' session state is not conflated.

#### Verification Evidence

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/workflow/artifacts-orchestrator-kinds.fr-node-126.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5 | 5 passed / 0 failed, measured 2026-08-01 on this file alone. One describe block per criterion. AC-1 :51-63 the kind vocabulary gains exactly the three members waves, resume-card and handoff, and the assertion additionally pins that it does NOT gain lane-manifest, which is the phase-2 member the criterion explicitly excludes. AC-2 :64-76 the resolver returns waves for a kiwi/waves.jsonl path, resume-card for a run-scoped resume-card.json path, and handoff for a handoff document path. AC-3 :77-90 run-id derivation over the lane-suffixed session layout yields the plan_run_id and is asserted never to yield the literal lanes segment and never the lane id, which are the two wrong answers the path shape invites. AC-4 :91-106 compares the two scores for one fixture pair and asserts a path under the lanes subtree earns the SAME canonical-path bonus as the equivalent path directly under the plan run id, so the lane layout is not penalised. AC-5 :107 two lanes' suffixed pm-state.json files under one plan_run_id resolve independently while both carry that plan_run_id, so two lanes' session state is not conflated. |

### FR-NODE-127 — Journal validation runs unconditionally on every journal append and every resume

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

`orchestrate journal append` SHALL run `validateWavesJournal` over the resulting journal view on every write and SHALL refuse, without appending, any write that would leave the journal invalid, reporting the diagnostic instead; `orchestrate resume` SHALL run the same validation and SHALL refuse to proceed on an invalid journal; neither SHALL make the validation conditional on a flag, an option or a mode; and `workflow_doctor` SHALL continue to offer the same check on demand as a convenience surface only, the pre-commit hook being no host for it.

#### Rationale

05 §10.1's wiring analysis: an agent instructed to call a detector is the same agent that skipped the gate, so the wiring matters more than the validator, and the first revision's two named hosts are both unreachable — `kiwi/waves.jsonl` is untracked by policy so the pre-commit branch never executes, `installGitPreCommitHook` is reached only from `speckiwi init` which the charter forbids against this repository, it refuses to overwrite a pre-existing non-speckiwi hook, and it skips entirely when `.git` is a file; and `workflow_doctor` is an on-demand tool, so calling it is a choice. `orchestrate journal append` is the only always-run host that exists, and `orchestrate resume` covers the second-most-common entry point (§11.1 step 3).

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: An append payload that would make the resulting journal invalid is refused: the command reports the diagnostic and the journal file is byte-identical before and after the attempt.
- [x] AC-2: An append payload that leaves the journal valid is written, and the validation ran — the command reports an empty diagnostic set rather than skipping the check.
- [x] AC-3: `orchestrate resume` over an invalid journal refuses and computes no resume state.
- [x] AC-4: No invocation form of `orchestrate journal append` or `orchestrate resume` skips validation: there is no flag, option or mode that disables it, asserted over the walked command tree.
- [x] AC-5: `workflow_doctor` reports the same diagnostics on demand for a historical journal, and no pre-commit hook code path invokes `validateWavesJournal`.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/cli/orchestrate-journal-validation.fr-node-127.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5 | 7 passed / 0 failed, measured 2026-08-01 on this file alone. One describe block per criterion. AC-1 :75-90 an append payload that would make the resulting journal invalid is refused, the command reports the diagnostic, and the journal file is asserted BYTE-IDENTICAL before and after the attempt, so the refusal leaves no partial write. AC-2 :91-110 a valid append is written and the command reports an EMPTY diagnostic set rather than omitting the field, which is what distinguishes 'validated and clean' from 'skipped', with :106 asserting the file holds exactly one line so the write happened once. AC-3 :111-125 orchestrate resume over an invalid journal returns the refusal without a resume payload, so no resume state is computed. AC-4 :126-142 walks the registered command tree for journal append and resume and asserts neither registers any flag, option or mode that disables the check — an assertion over the walked tree rather than over a hand-listed set of invocations, so a newly added bypass flag would fail it. AC-5 :143-171 workflow_doctor reports the same diagnostics on demand for a historical journal (:144), leaves the doctor untouched when no run is named (:153), and :162 asserts validateWavesJournal is invoked from no pre-commit hook code path, keeping the check on the command and out of the hook. |

### FR-NODE-128 — Skill command-citation parity against the real command tree with a measured invocation floor

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

The release parity harness SHALL extract every `speckiwi orchestrate` invocation from each of the three bundled SKILL.md variants, normalise documented placeholders against the declared thirteen-form placeholder table, and resolve each cited verb and flag against the command tree built by `buildCommand` and walked with `outputHelp()`, never by calling `program.parse()`; a citation carrying a placeholder token absent from the table SHALL fail the test rather than be silently normalised; and the extracted invocation count SHALL be asserted per variant against that variant's own count of `speckiwi ` occurrences measured in the same body at test time, never against a hard-coded number, so that zero extracted invocations against a non-empty skill body fails.

#### Rationale

05 §10.4's command-citation class: `test/release/tool-signature-parity.test.ts` already reads the doc and skill roots, builds the real program with `buildCommand` and walks the tree with `outputHelp()`, so this is an extension rather than a new harness. It resolves rather than parses because commander has no parse-only mode and `program.parse()` would execute action handlers, which in this repository would trip the hermeticity guards. The measured floor and the negative fixture exist because without both, the test is green at every point of the work order — before step 8 the skill does not exist, and step 8 authors it so that every citation already resolves.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Extraction runs over all three bundled variants — `skills/claude`, `skills/codex`, `skills/etc` — and each variant's extracted invocation count is compared against that variant's own count of `speckiwi ` occurrences measured from the same body at test time, with no numeric literal in the assertion.
- [x] AC-2: A non-empty skill body from which zero invocations are extracted fails the test.
- [x] AC-3: Every extracted verb and every extracted flag resolves against the walked command tree; a negative fixture whose body cites a verb absent from the tree fails, and that failure is observed before the real SKILL.md body is authored.
- [x] AC-4: The placeholder table declares thirteen forms — `<path>`, `<path to routing/route-gate.json>`, `<sha>`, `<id>`, `<payload>`, `<manifest…>`, `<v>`, `<t>`, `N`, `S`, `L`, `{run_id}` and the literal alternation form `a|b|c` — and a citation carrying a token outside that set fails rather than being normalised.
- [x] AC-5: The harness calls `buildCommand` and `outputHelp()` and does not call `program.parse()`; no command action handler executes during the test and the repository's hermeticity guards stay green.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/release/skill-citation-parity.fr-node-128.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5 | 6 passed / 0 failed, measured 2026-08-01 on this file alone. AC-1 :198-213 runs extraction over all three bundled variants and compares each variant's extracted invocation count against that variant's OWN count of speckiwi occurrences measured from the same body at test time; the assertion carries no numeric literal, so the floor moves with the body rather than needing maintenance. AC-2 :214 a non-empty skill body from which zero invocations are extracted fails the test, which is the guard that stops AC-1 passing vacuously on a broken extractor. AC-3 :223-254 rejects a fabricated verb and a fabricated flag while accepting a real citation (:224), then resolves every citation of every bundled variant against the walked command tree (:242). The criterion's further requirement that the negative fixture be observed failing BEFORE the real SKILL.md body was authored is borne out by the commit record: this test file entered in a8bb354, while the three kiwi-orchestrator SKILL.md bodies first appear in cdc25dc, a later commit — the suite was red for the missing body in between. AC-4 :255-267 declares the thirteen placeholder forms and asserts a citation carrying a token outside that set FAILS rather than being normalised. AC-5 :268 resolves every citation through buildCommand and outputHelp() while parse and parseAsync are booby-trapped, so the test fails if any code path parses; no action handler executes and the repository hermeticity guards stay green. |

### FR-NODE-129 — waves-event.md field tables and bullet invariants set-equal to the journal schema across four copies

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

The parity harness SHALL parse both the field tables and the bullet-form invariants of every one of the four shipped `waves-event.md` copies — the three skill variants plus the `.agents` mirror — and assert set-equality of the parsed field set against `journal-schema.ts`'s per-event-kind required and optional field sets and of the parsed enum values against its twelve closed enums, enumerating the bullet set by measurement from the document at test time rather than against a hard-coded count.

#### Rationale

05 §10.4's contract-parity class: the mechanical invariants `verification-gate.ts` implements are stated in `waves-event.md` as bullet list items rather than table rows, so a table-only parity check would miss the one drift class it most needs to catch — the drift that produced `IR-CLI-080`. §10.4 states that the first revision's "the nine invariants at `waves-event.md:89-99`" is wrong on the count, the range and the unit, and that a parity test pinned to it would have asserted the wrong denominator; §14 registration 5 requires all four copies to be edited in one change because the test asserts across all of them.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Field-table parity is asserted as set-equality in both directions: a field present in a `waves-event.md` copy and absent from `journal-schema.ts` fails, and a schema field absent from a copy fails.
- [x] AC-2: The bullet-form invariants are parsed as a set and their count is derived from the document at run time; no bullet count literal appears in the assertion.
- [x] AC-3: All four copies are asserted — the three skill variants and the `.agents` mirror — and a fixture in which a field or a bullet lands in three copies but not the fourth fails.
- [ ] AC-4: Enum parity covers the twelve closed enums `status`, `phase`, `verb`, `recovery_class`, `proof_kind`, `conflict_reason`, `recipe_kind`, `exclusion_class`, `reason_class`, `issue_class`, `manifest_status` and `AutoGateAction`.
- [x] AC-5: `GateId` is not asserted by this set-equality check, because §4.2's v1.4.0 field table carries no `gate` field; gate-id parity is a separate set-inclusion assertion.
- [x] AC-6: A negative fixture adding one bullet to a single copy fails, so the bullet half of the check is observed red.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/orchestrator/waves-event-parity.fr-node-129.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-5, AC-6 | 41 passed / 0 failed, measured 2026-08-01 on this file alone. AC-1 :165-196 asserts field-table parity as set-equality in BOTH directions, per copy, against the required and optional field sets, with two negative fixtures: a field present in the document and absent from the schema fails (:182), and a schema field removed from the document fails (:188). AC-2 :197-227 measures the bullet set from each document at run time and asserts it non-empty, resolves every rule anchor to exactly one bullet, and asserts no bullet is left unclaimed by a rule or by the declared non-sources; no bullet count literal appears in the assertion. AC-3 :158-164 asserts exactly four copies are read and every one is non-empty, and :246 is the negative fixture in which a bullet landing in three copies but not the fourth fails; :296 makes the same four-copy demand of the enum declarations. AC-5 :318-322 asserts GateId is absent from the twelve-enum census and that its value set is not among the censused values, keeping gate-id parity a separate set-inclusion assertion. AC-6 :228 adds one bullet to a single copy and asserts the harness goes red, so the bullet half has been observed failing. The four copies read are the three skill variants under skills/claude, skills/codex and skills/etc plus the .agents mirror. |
| VE-2 | inspection | skills/{claude,codex,etc}/_shared/kiwi/waves-event.md and .agents/skills/_shared/kiwi/waves-event.md, measured with the harness's own parseEnumValues | AC-4 | AC-4 IS ONLY PARTIALLY COVERED and is therefore left UNTICKED. The criterion asks for enum parity across twelve named closed enums. The harness names all twelve at :127-140, and :257-275 asserts the census is exactly twelve with each entry distinct and bound to a live journal-schema.ts constant rather than a restated literal — but note precisely what that assertion checks: it checks the TypeScript array literal in the test, NOT any document. The per-copy comparison at :281 filters the census to those enums the document actually declares values for. I measured that set on 2026-08-01 by running the harness own longestValueRun and parseEnumValues over all four copies: exactly 5 of 12 are declared, identically in every copy — status, phase, proof_kind, exclusion_class and reason_class. The other seven are compared against the schema in NO copy. They fail to be declared for two different reasons, which matters for how each is closed: verb does appear in the document but defers its values to the skill body rather than listing them, so it parses to an empty value run; recovery_class, conflict_reason, recipe_kind, issue_class, manifest_status and AutoGateAction have zero occurrences in the document at all. What IS asserted for all seven is only that the set of declared enums is identical across the four copies (:296), so a value landing in three copies but not the fourth fails. Closing AC-4 requires declaring the remaining seven in waves-event.md, and for verb specifically it requires inlining the values rather than deferring them. The harness needs no change: the census is measurement-driven and will pick them up automatically. |

### FR-NODE-130 — package-doctor requires the orchestrator skill in all three variants

| Field | Value |

#### Requirement

`src/doctor/package-doctor.ts`'s `EXPECTED_KIWI_SKILLS` SHALL include `kiwi-orchestrator`, which mechanically extends `EXPECTED_SKILL_ENTRYPOINTS` so that `package-doctor` requires the skill's entrypoint under each of the three bundled variants and reports a missing variant as a doctor failure naming that variant.

#### Rationale

05 §14 contract registration 4, restated in §10.5 step 8: the skill is added to `EXPECTED_KIWI_SKILLS` (measured at `src/doctor/package-doctor.ts:58-75`), which mechanically extends `EXPECTED_SKILL_ENTRYPOINTS` (`:77`) to require all three variants, and that test goes red first. §14 exists as the "easy to forget, break silently" list precisely because a skill absent from the doctor's expectation set ships broken without any check firing.

- [x] AC-1: `EXPECTED_KIWI_SKILLS` contains `kiwi-orchestrator`.

- [x] AC-3: Removing any single variant's `kiwi-orchestrator` entrypoint makes `package-doctor` report a failure that names the missing variant.
- [x] AC-4: The doctor check is red before the SKILL.md bodies are authored and green after all three exist.
