### FR-NODE-131 — Orchestrator run lock keyed on the git common dir

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

The orchestrator run lock SHALL derive its lock path from the git common dir rather than from a project root, so that every worktree sharing one `.git` contends for one lock and one orchestrator run exists per repository rather than per worktree; it SHALL expose `acquire`, `renew` and `release`, guard acquisition with an `O_EXCL` sentinel, reclaim a sentinel whose recorded process is no longer live while refusing one whose process is live, fence concurrent acquisition on Windows through a named pipe, and refuse a second acquisition while the lock is held with the gate `orchestrator-run-lock-held`; the lock SHALL be acquired at Preflight P.5 and released at run end or on `abort-run`.

#### Rationale

05 §5.2: nothing else in the design protects against two orchestrator runs over one repository, a different hazard from two lanes. `06:161` records that as written every lock path derives from a `ProjectRoot`, so N worktrees over one shared `.git` get N locks and no mutual exclusion at all; §0.4 and §10.1 therefore harvest `lease-lock.ts` (H2) re-keyed on the git common dir. §13 lists `orchestrator-run-lock-held` as a critical gate raised at Preflight P.5.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Two linked worktrees over one `.git` resolve to the same lock path; acquisition from the first succeeds and acquisition from the second is refused with `orchestrator-run-lock-held`.
- [x] AC-2: Two repositories with distinct common dirs each acquire successfully and concurrently, so the lock is repository-scoped rather than machine-global.
- [x] AC-3: The module exports `acquire`, `renew` and `release`, and acquisition uses an `O_EXCL` sentinel so a second acquire against an existing sentinel fails rather than overwriting it.
- [x] AC-4: A sentinel whose recorded pid is no longer live is reclaimable; one whose recorded pid is live is not.
- [x] AC-5: Release followed by acquire succeeds, and `orchestrate run abort` releases the lock so a subsequent acquire succeeds.
- [x] AC-6: `orchestrate run status` reports the holder of a held lock and reports no holder after release.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/orchestrator/run-lock.fr-node-131.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5 | 6/6 pass. AC-1 adds a linked worktree over one .git, asserts resolveGitCommonDir and runLockPath agree across both, and that the second acquire rejects with RunLockHeldError carrying gate orchestrator-run-lock-held and owner run-a. AC-2 asserts two repositories yield distinct lock paths, both acquire, and readHolder reports each owner. AC-3 asserts acquire/renew/release are exported functions and that acquiring against a pre-written sentinel rejects while the sentinel bytes are unchanged (run-lock.ts writes with flag wx, the O_EXCL form). AC-4 reclaims a sentinel holding a dead pid, refuses one holding a live pid, and refuses a foreign-host sentinel whose liveness cannot be proven locally. AC-5 first half: release then acquire succeeds with a new token and the stale holder's renew rejects. |
| VE-2 | test | test/cli/orchestrate-deferred-halves.test.ts | AC-5, AC-6 | 7/7 pass. The command half: over a throwaway git repository, orchestrate run lock acquires, run status reports holder.owner fr-node-131-test, run abort --reason exits 0 and the following run status reports holder null, then a fresh run lock succeeds - AC-5's abort clause and AC-6's held-then-released report. |

### FR-NODE-132 — Requirement readiness derived from a snapshot, transport-independently

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

Requirement readiness SHALL be derived rather than trusted, by the pure functions `deriveCanonicalRequirementReadiness(snapshot, target, requirementIds)` and `scopeRequirementDiagnostics`, which compute `hardDependenciesSatisfied`, `evidenceDrift` and `ownershipVerified` from raw requirement records and fail closed on dependency cycles, duplicate requirement ids and a summary that contradicts the records; the functions SHALL take no transport client, so that the same snapshot content obtained through MCP and through `speckiwi list --json` yields identical results; a caller SHALL NOT be able to assert its own readiness; and a not-ready result SHALL raise `requirement-not-ready` at Phase 3.c′.

#### Rationale

05 §5.11 carries `06:233`'s ruling — wherever the design reads requirement readiness before dispatch, it derives rather than trusts — and states the reason the audit gives for harvesting these two functions rather than writing an equivalent: they are pure over `(snapshot, target, requirementIds)`, so a caller cannot self-attest readiness (`06:162`). They work over a snapshot from any transport, and the 780 lines of MCP client around them are deliberately not taken (`06:166-195`).

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Both functions are pure over `(snapshot, target, requirementIds)`: neither signature nor the module's import graph reaches the network, the filesystem, the clock or an MCP client.
- [x] AC-2: The derivation computes `hardDependenciesSatisfied`, `evidenceDrift` and `ownershipVerified` from the raw records rather than from any summary field.
- [x] AC-3: Each of three fail-closed fixtures — a dependency cycle, a duplicate requirement id, and a summary contradicting the records — yields not-ready rather than ready.
- [x] AC-4: One snapshot's content, presented once as an MCP payload and once as `speckiwi list --json` output, yields identical readiness results for the same `(target, requirementIds)`.
- [x] AC-5: A record that asserts its own readiness while the derived computation refutes it is reported not-ready, so no input path lets a caller self-attest.
- [x] AC-6: A not-ready result at Phase 3.c′ raises `requirement-not-ready` and refuses the dispatch.

#### Verification Evidence

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/orchestrator/readiness.fr-node-132.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6 | 6/6 pass. AC-1 asserts readiness.ts source carries no node: import, no Date.now, no new Date, no Math.random and no process reference, that deriveCanonicalRequirementReadiness has arity 3 over (snapshot, target, requirementIds), and that two calls agree while the snapshot is unmutated. AC-2 partitions the module into top-level function bodies and asserts summaryContradictsRecords is the only reader of a summary field, then flips evidenceDrift, hardDependenciesSatisfied and ownershipVerified one at a time by changing only a record. AC-3 asserts the cycle, duplicate-id and summary-contradiction fixtures each yield not-ready. AC-4 parses one content through cliPayload and mcpPayload and asserts identical snapshots and identical readiness. AC-5 feeds a record carrying its own hardDependenciesSatisfied/evidenceDrift/ownershipVerified/ready fields and asserts the parsed record key set drops them and the derivation reports evidenceDrift true. AC-6 asserts assertRequirementsReady throws RequirementNotReadyError with gate requirement-not-ready naming FR-B-013, passes on a ready snapshot, and fails closed on an id the snapshot does not carry. |
| VE-2 | inspection | src/core/orchestrator/readiness.ts | AC-1 | Import-graph half of AC-1, measured 2026-08-01: readiness.ts declares exactly one import, { REQUIREMENT_STATUSES, STABILITY_LEVELS, DiagnosticSeverity, RequirementStatus, Stability } from ../types.js, and src/core/types.ts declares no import at all. The closure of the module's import graph is therefore those two files, neither reaching node:, a network client, the clock or an MCP client. |
| VE-3 | test | test/cli/orchestrate-deferred-halves.test.ts | AC-6 | 7/7 pass. Command half of the Phase 3.c-prime refusal: orchestrate readiness check over a snapshot missing the requested id exits 2 with gate requirement-not-ready and no readiness payload, and over a ready snapshot exits 0 with empty violations. |

### FR-NODE-133 — Wave allocation check over sidecar req_ids and the design-item map

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

Phase 3.c′ SHALL evaluate an allocation check over a fresh snapshot and SHALL refuse with `unallocated-req-id` unless all four conjuncts hold: the union of `sidecar.tasks[].req_ids` is a subset of the 3.b allocation set recorded as `allocation.requirement_ids[]` on the `register-wave-srs` result line; no sidecar task carries an empty `req_ids`; every allocated `req_id` carries at least one design item in the wave's `design_item_map`; and every `design_items[]` entry of the wave appears against at least one `req_id`. The allocation set SHALL be derived mechanically as the sorted set difference between the `list_requirements` snapshots for the wave target captured before and after the `/kiwi-srs` hop, with the pre-hop snapshot's sha256 recorded as `allocation.pre_snapshot_digest` on the `register-wave-srs` intent line, and a resumed session whose recomputed pre-snapshot digest no longer matches that recorded value SHALL refuse rather than re-derive the set.

#### Rationale

05 §5.11 check 1: handoff resolvability catches an unresolvable requirement id but not a task whose `req_ids` is empty, and neither catches an id allocated somewhere other than 3.b — so charter C2 becomes a plan property rather than a runtime hope. §5.11 adds the two design-item-map conjuncts as what makes `computeLanePlan`'s design-item-map input a checked operand rather than an assumption, and states the derivation of the allocation set and the map because revision 2 named neither producer, leaving E34 testing a comparison against a value nothing produced.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A sidecar carrying a `req_id` outside `allocation.requirement_ids[]` is refused with `unallocated-req-id` naming the offending id.
- [x] AC-2: A sidecar carrying a task with an empty `req_ids` array is refused with `unallocated-req-id` naming that task.
- [x] AC-3: An allocated `req_id` with no design item in the wave's `design_item_map` is refused.
- [x] AC-4: A wave `design_items[]` entry that appears against no `req_id` is refused, so the map is checked total in both directions.
- [x] AC-5: A sidecar for which all four conjuncts hold passes the check.
- [x] AC-6: `allocation.requirement_ids[]` equals the sorted set difference of the pre-hop and post-hop `list_requirements` snapshots for the wave target, and a resume whose recomputed pre-snapshot digest differs from `allocation.pre_snapshot_digest` refuses rather than re-deriving the set.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/orchestrator/allocation.fr-node-133.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6 | 18/18 pass. AC-1 asserts a task carrying FR-NODE-999 outside allocation.requirementIds refuses with code unallocated-req-id, conjunct req-id-outside-allocation, and a detail naming both the id and the task, and that every offending id is named. AC-2 asserts a task with an empty reqIds array refuses with conjunct empty-req-ids naming that task. AC-3 asserts an allocated id absent from designItemMap refuses with allocated-req-id-without-design-item, and so does an entry present but empty. AC-4 asserts a wave design item D-004 claimed by no allocated id refuses with design-item-against-no-req-id, including when it is mapped from an id outside the allocation set, and a composite fixture reports all four conjuncts at once. AC-5 asserts the all-holding input returns {ok:true} and that ALLOCATION_CONJUNCTS is exactly those four. AC-6 asserts deriveAllocationSet is the sorted, deduplicated set difference of the pre-hop and post-hop snapshots including the empty and removed cases, that resolveAllocationOnResume returns the recorded set on a matching digest, and refuses with allocation-pre-snapshot-drift naming both digests when they differ. |

### FR-NODE-134 — Handoff pinning records a real blob oid and resists path substitution

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

Handoff pinning SHALL record, for each pinned document, both a sha256 over its content and a real `git_blob_oid` — never a null placeholder — obtained through plain `git cat-file` and `git ls-tree` invoked with an explicit working directory rather than through the harvested `runTrustedGit` wrapper, and SHALL refuse a document exceeding its declared size cap; and re-proving a pin SHALL fail when the pinned path has been substituted by inode replacement, ancestor-directory replacement, symlink, hardlink or Windows junction.

#### Rationale

05 §0.4's A1 ruling, restated at §10.1's `freeze.ts` entry: the harvest takes the pinning half only — `readBlob`, the topology hash and the `assertPinned*` family — drops the AGENTS.md discovery half, drops the `runTrustedGit` dependency in favour of plain git with an explicit `cwd` (`06:160`, `06:330`), and deletes the earlier "records the blob-OID field as null" sentence, because a null oid makes the pin unprovable. The pin is what makes drift digest 4 meaningful: a hand-edited handoff after verification silently changes what a lane is told to do (§4.7).

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A freeze over a pinned handoff records a non-null `git_blob_oid` equal to the object id git computes for that content, and a `sha256` over the same content; neither field is null nor a placeholder.
- [x] AC-2: `assertPinned` succeeds against an unchanged pinned document and fails when the document's content has changed by a single byte.
- [ ] AC-3: Re-proving a pin fails under each of five substitution classes asserted independently: inode replacement, ancestor-directory replacement, symlink substitution, hardlink substitution and Windows junction substitution.
- [x] AC-4: The pinning code path invokes plain `git` with an explicit working directory and contains no call to `runTrustedGit`.
- [x] AC-5: A document exceeding the declared size cap is refused rather than pinned.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/orchestrator/pinning.fr-node-134.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-4, AC-5 | 9 passed, 1 skipped. AC-1 asserts document.gitBlobOid equals both git hash-object and rev-parse HEAD:path, matches a 40- or 64-hex oid, and that sha256 equals a fresh createHash over the file bytes with byteLength recorded. AC-2 asserts assertPinned resolves on the unchanged document and rejects with HandoffPinError after a single byte is flipped at equal length. AC-4 injects a git runner, asserts every call carries an absolute cwd and that rev-parse, ls-tree and cat-file are among the verbs, and asserts pinning.ts contains execFile and does not contain runTrustedGit. AC-5 asserts a document one byte over PIN_LIMITS.maxDocumentBytes is refused with HandoffPinError while one exactly at the cap pins. |
| VE-2 | test | test/core/orchestrator/pinning.fr-node-134.test.ts | AC-3 | PARTIAL - AC-3 left unticked. The SUBSTITUTION_CLASSES table is asserted to hold exactly the five named classes, and the suite drives each independently, but on this Windows host only 4 of 5 executed: inode replacement, ancestor-directory replacement, hardlink substitution and windows junction substitution each rejected assertPinned with HandoffPinError, while symlink substitution was skipped with the recorded reason 'this substitution class needs a filesystem privilege this process lacks: EPERM' - Windows refuses file-symlink creation without SeCreateSymbolicLink. The fifth class is declared and implemented but was not evaluated in this run. |

| Type | Reference | Relation | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |

#### Research / Analysis

- -

#### Implementation Notes

- -
- [2026-08-01] AC-3 unticked, measured 2026-08-01. Five substitution classes are declared and implemented; four were evaluated on this Windows 11 host and the fifth was not. Skipped: symlink substitution, which calls fs.symlink(target, link, 'file') and fails EPERM - Windows refuses file-symlink creation to a process lacking SeCreateSymbolicLinkPrivilege. The runner records the reason verbatim: 'this substitution class needs a filesystem privilege this process lacks: EPERM'.
- [2026-08-01] The four classes that did execute each rejected assertPinned with HandoffPinError as AC-3 requires: inode replacement, ancestor-directory replacement, hardlink substitution and windows junction substitution. Measured file count: 9 passed, 1 skipped. The skipped case is 'not evaluated on this host', not 'does not work' - no evidence either way exists for file-symlink substitution on a host holding the privilege.
- [2026-08-01] Row deliberately left at implemented rather than verified. The criterion was not amended to match what ran: amending an AC so that work already done satisfies it is the same shape as weakening a test to reach green, and the author of the failure should not also be its editor. Closing it is someone else's decision - run the suite on a host holding SeCreateSymbolicLinkPrivilege, or have a human rule that AC-3 should have admitted a host-refused class.

### FR-NODE-135 — Duplication auditor emits candidate rows only, never a classification

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

`planDuplicationAudit(laneDiffs, writeSets) -> AuditRow[]` SHALL be a pure candidate constructor over the union of a wave's diffs, emitting exactly one row when two lanes each contribute an added block with the same `normalizedHash`, exactly one row when two lanes each contribute an added top-level declaration sharing a normalized `declName`, and no row for anything else; each row SHALL carry `{symbolOrBlock, lanes[], paths[], verdict, resolutionTaskId}` with `verdict` drawn from the closed enum `duplicate` | `parallel-evolution` | `acceptable`; the function SHALL NOT classify, the verdict and `resolutionTaskId` being a recorded subagent judgment; and the gate `cross-lane-duplication-unresolved` SHALL refuse when any candidate carries no recorded verdict from that enum, and refuse a `duplicate` row whose `resolutionTaskId` is neither the id of an epilogue task that has already run nor an `issue:{id}` reference to a `local-defect` row opened in this wave's issue ledger.

#### Rationale

05 §7.9 (b) promotes the charter's no-duplication requirement out of the self-criticism section into a named step at 3.k with an artifact and a gate, and §1.1's boundary rule splits it: the tool constructs candidates mechanically and the subagent judges them, because near-duplicate classification is judgment. §7.9 (b) also fixes what a resolution is for both phases — a note is not a resolution, and because 3.k executes the epilogue task set before the audit runs, a `duplicate` row cannot name a member of this wave's frozen epilogue set that has yet to run.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Two lanes contributing an added block with the same `normalizedHash` produce exactly one row naming both lane ids and both paths.
- [x] AC-2: Two lanes contributing an added top-level declaration with the same normalized `declName` produce exactly one row.
- [x] AC-3: Two fixtures produce zero rows: one where a single lane duplicates a block against itself, and one where two lanes' added blocks differ in `normalizedHash` and in `declName`.
- [x] AC-4: The function is pure and deterministic: two calls over the same `laneDiffs` and `writeSets` return byte-identical `AuditRow[]`.
- [x] AC-5: No input to `planDuplicationAudit` expresses a classification and the module contains no branch selecting among `duplicate`, `parallel-evolution` and `acceptable`.
- [x] AC-6: The gate refuses when any candidate row carries no verdict from the closed enum, and accepts when every candidate carries one.
- [x] AC-7: The gate refuses a `duplicate` row whose `resolutionTaskId` is null, and refuses one naming a member of this wave's frozen `serial_epilogue`, `unassigned` or `demoted` set that has not run; it accepts an already-run epilogue task id and accepts an `issue:{id}` reference.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/orchestrator/duplication-audit.fr-node-135.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6, AC-7 | 17/17 pass. AC-1 asserts two lanes sharing normalizedHash h1 yield exactly one row naming both lane ids and both paths with a null verdict. AC-2 asserts declName normalisePath against NormalisePath yields one row keyed on the normalised symbol. AC-3 asserts zero rows for a lane duplicating a block against itself, for two lanes differing in hash and name, and for a block outside the contributing lane's write set. AC-4 asserts two JSON.stringify results over the same laneDiffs are identical and non-empty. AC-5 asserts LaneDiff carries no verdict field, that the sliced planDuplicationAudit body contains none of AUDIT_VERDICTS, and that every constructed row's verdict is null. AC-6 asserts the gate refuses a null verdict and an off-enum verdict with gate cross-lane-duplication-unresolved and accepts a ledger where every row carries an enum member. AC-7 asserts a duplicate row with a null resolutionTaskId is refused, one naming frozen-but-not-run T-EP-02 is refused, one naming already-run T-EP-01 is accepted, issue:I-004 is accepted and free prose is refused; the requirement's serial_epilogue, unassigned and demoted sets are modelled as the single precomputed union DuplicationGateContext.frozenEpilogueTaskIds. |

### FR-NODE-136 — planStageCoupling over authored handoffs, with no hoist predicate and no existing-paths input

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

`planStageCoupling(handoffs: ParsedHandoff[]) -> {couplings: Array<{path, fromLane, toLane}>}` SHALL be a pure function over one stage's authored handoffs, emitting one coupling record for each path that appears in one lane's `write_set` and another lane's `read_set` within that stage and carrying exactly the three fields `ParsedHandoff[]` determines; it SHALL take no `existing_paths` argument and SHALL emit no `hoist` output, the shared-substrate hoist predicate being deliberately absent; it SHALL run at Phase 3.f″ after handoffs exist, backing `orchestrate coupling check` and the gate `stage-coupling-unresolved`; and `shared-substrate` SHALL NOT be a member of the `conflict_reason` enum.

#### Rationale

05 §7.9 (a): revision 1's `shared-substrate` conflict edge could not be evaluated where it was placed — it was circular, `read_set` is a handoff field authored two phases later, and no input on any surface carries symbols — and revision 2's path-level replacement is wholly subsumed, since §6.2 layer 4 rejects such a handoff one step earlier and `write-set-overlap` has already forced the lanes same-lane. What survives is the cross-lane coupling check, which uses only fields that exist at 3.f″ and is covered by no conflict edge. §22.11 AC-08 narrows the return to `{path, fromLane, toLane}` and makes the caller, not the pure function, resolve the postmortem row's task ids.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A path in lane A's `write_set` and lane B's `read_set` within one stage yields exactly one record `{path, fromLane: A, toLane: B}`.
- [x] AC-2: A path in two lanes' `write_set`s and in no lane's `read_set` yields no coupling record.
- [x] AC-3: Each returned record carries exactly three keys — `path`, `fromLane`, `toLane` — and the return object carries no `hoist` key, no task id and no symbol.
- [x] AC-4: The declared parameter list is `handoffs` alone; there is no `existingPaths` or `existing_paths` parameter.
- [x] AC-5: `shared-substrate` is not a member of the `conflict_reason` closed enum, and `planStageCoupling` returns no conflict edge.
- [x] AC-6: Two calls over the same `ParsedHandoff[]` return byte-identical output.
- [x] AC-7: `orchestrate coupling check --wave N --stage S` reports the same coupling set the function returns, and a stage with a second coupling hit after one re-partition pass raises `stage-coupling-unresolved`.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/orchestrator/substrate.fr-node-136.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6 | 10/10 pass. AC-1 asserts a path in lane l1's write_set and lane l2's read_set yields exactly [{path, fromLane: l1, toLane: l2}], one record per reading lane, and no self-coupling when one lane both writes and reads. AC-2 asserts a path in two lanes' write_sets and no read_set yields []. AC-3 asserts Object.keys(result) equals [couplings] and every record's sorted keys are exactly [fromLane, path, toLane], so there is no hoist key, task id or symbol. AC-4 asserts planStageCoupling.length is 1, so there is no existingPaths parameter. AC-5 asserts CONFLICT_REASONS is exactly the eleven members and excludes shared-substrate, and that no returned record carries a reason property. AC-6 asserts two JSON.stringify results over the same handoffs are identical with the expected four-record ordering, that shuffling the input order gives the same output, and that an empty stage yields no couplings. |
| VE-2 | test | test/core/orchestrator/handoff-reader.fr-node-136.test.ts | AC-1, AC-6 | 6/6 pass. The producer half: readHandoff emits exactly the seven declared ParsedHandoff fields, reads kind, lane, wave, stage, write_set, read_set and the ten headings in source order, returns null for a document with no closed front matter, and planStageCoupling consumes two readHandoff outputs unmodified to find the lane-3 to lane-4 coupling, so there is no second front-matter parser. |

### FR-NODE-137 — Uniform orchestrate exit codes for gate refusal, operational error and success

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

Every `orchestrate` gate verb SHALL exit 2 on refusal with the payload `{ok: false, gate, violations[]}` where `gate` is a member of the exported `GateId` union, exit 1 on an operational error with a payload carrying `{ok: false, error}` and no `gate`, and exit 0 on success with `{ok: true, violations: []}`; a refused mutation verb SHALL exit 2 with an envelope whose `applied` is false and whose `gate` names the refusal, and exit 1 on an operational error; and `--dry-run` SHALL produce the same exit codes as the normal mode, so a dry run of a call that would be refused also exits 2.

#### Rationale

05 §10.6's exit-code table, stated once for the whole namespace because revision 1 said what mutation verbs return and nothing about what any verb exits, while §5.9's own pseudo-code branches on failure and every skill sentence that branches on a gate needs the same answer. Exit 2 is the gate-refusal code and is deliberately distinct from 1, so a skill can branch on refusal without parsing the payload and a CI wrapper cannot mistake a gate for a crash. "Inspectable before it is consequential" means the same thing in both modes, which is why a dry run of a refused call still exits 2.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Each phase-1 gate verb — `wave close`, `handoff validate`, `validate`, `resume` — exits 2 on refusal with `{ok: false, gate, violations[]}` and a `gate` value that is a member of the exported `GateId` union.
- [x] AC-2: Each of those verbs exits 0 on success with `{ok: true, violations: []}`.
- [x] AC-3: Each exits 1 on an operational error such as an unreadable file or malformed JSON, with a payload carrying `{ok: false, error}` and no `gate` field.
- [x] AC-4: Exit 2 and exit 1 are never returned for the same class of outcome, so refusal is distinguishable from a crash without parsing the payload.
- [x] AC-5: A refused mutation verb exits 2 with an envelope whose `applied` is false and whose `gate` names the refusal.
- [x] AC-6: `--dry-run` on a mutation verb that would be refused exits 2 and writes nothing; `--dry-run` on one that would succeed exits 0 and writes nothing.

### FR-NODE-138 — Six freeze lock kinds validated against their schemas and byte-deterministic over injected inputs

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

`freezeLock(kind, body, inputs) -> Lock` SHALL support exactly the six lock kinds `design`, `waves`, `lanes`, `handoff`, `issues` and `postmortem`, validate each `body` against that kind's schema under the common envelope `{schema_version, run_id, kind, inputs_digest, git_blob_oid, sha256, written_at}` — where `sha256` covers every envelope field above it and `inputs_digest` covers the exact declared inputs the lock was computed from — and produce byte-identical output for the same `(kind, body, inputs)` triple, its `inputs` arriving injected so the determinism holds without reading the filesystem; `lanes.lock.json` SHALL record the five input digests `sidecar_digest`, `registry_digest`, `existing_paths_digest`, `design_item_map_digest` and `prior_postmortem_digests[]` plus the literal `lane_cap`, `code_roots` and `test_roots`, and those eight recorded fields SHALL pin the nine declared `computeLanePlan` inputs that drift digest 3 recomputes against.

#### Rationale

05 §3.3a fixes the freeze target set at six after revision 2 stated three different sets of five, and gives each lock a common envelope plus a per-kind body, because leaving them unstated left five JSON contracts to be invented by whoever implemented the gates that read them. §4.7 digest 3 is the consumer: the recomputation uses the inputs the lock itself records rather than the inputs available today, which is what makes the check meaningful rather than noisy. §10.1 and §10.5 step 1′ scope `freeze` here to its lock-generation half so §10.3's byte-determinism property is a Layer-1 unit test rather than a disk-reading one.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `freezeLock` accepts exactly the six kinds `design`, `waves`, `lanes`, `handoff`, `issues` and `postmortem` and rejects any seventh kind value.
- [x] AC-2: Each of the six kinds has one rejected-body fixture: a body missing a field its schema requires is refused rather than frozen, including a `lanes` body missing `design_item_map_digest`.
- [x] AC-3: Two calls with the same `(kind, body, inputs)` produce byte-identical `Lock` output including `inputs_digest` and `sha256`; changing one member of `inputs` changes `inputs_digest`.
- [x] AC-4: `sha256` covers every envelope field above it: altering `written_at` alone changes `sha256`.
- [x] AC-5: A frozen `lanes.lock.json` carries exactly the five digest fields `sidecar_digest`, `registry_digest`, `existing_paths_digest`, `design_item_map_digest` and `prior_postmortem_digests[]`, plus `lane_cap`, `code_roots` and `test_roots` recorded as literal values with no digest of their own — eight recorded fields.
- [x] AC-6: Drift digest 3 reconstructs all nine declared `computeLanePlan` inputs from those eight recorded fields, `sidecar_digest` covering both `catalog` and `existing_modules`.
- [x] AC-7: `inputs` is a parameter rather than a value the function reads: the byte-determinism test runs with no filesystem access.

#### Verification Evidence

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/orchestrator/freeze.fr-node-138.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6, AC-7 | 29/29 pass. AC-1 asserts FREEZE_LOCK_KINDS is exactly design, waves, lanes, handoff, issues, postmortem, freezes each against its own valid body, and rejects a seventh kind with code unknown-lock-kind naming it. AC-2 iterates the six kinds with one required field deleted each and asserts code lock-body-invalid whose detail names the field, including a lanes body missing design_item_map_digest asserted separately. AC-3 asserts byte-identical JSON for the same triple across all six kinds, stability under body key reordering, and that changing or adding a declaredInputs member changes inputs_digest while written_at does not. AC-4 asserts sha256 changes when written_at alone changes, and also on run_id, git_blob_oid, inputs_digest, kind and body changes, over the eight-key envelope. AC-5 asserts LANES_LOCK_DIGEST_FIELDS is the five named digests and LANES_LOCK_RECORDED_INPUT_FIELDS is those plus lane_cap, code_roots and test_roots for eight, that the three literals carry no digest field of their own, and that omitting any one of the eight is refused naming it. AC-6 asserts reconstructLanePlanInputPins yields exactly nine input pins over eight distinct recorded fields, that sidecar_digest covers exactly catalog and existingModules, and that each pin resolves to the recorded value rather than a current one. AC-7 asserts run id, blob oid and written_at all arrive injected so the module holds no clock or filesystem read. |

### FR-NODE-139 — Journal validation ignores an unmatched intent and evaluates lane terminality only at completion

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

`validateWavesJournal` SHALL never report an unmatched `intent` line as a diagnostic, an intent with no matching result being the normal transient state of the write-ahead discipline and its interpretation belonging to `computeResumeState`; and it SHALL evaluate the v1.4.0 lane-terminality rule — every lane in `lane_plan` has a terminal state, and an integrated lane has a `merge_sha` — only when the line under validation is a wave `complete` or a `final-verify` line, never per line.

#### Rationale

05 §10.1's wiring section states both exclusions as load-bearing consequences of validating on every append: §4.3's discipline is write-ahead intent, act, write-behind result, so an unmatched intent is the normal state after every intent append — if validation reported it, the validate-on-every-append host would refuse the very first write of every verb and the design could not execute a single step. For the same reason the lane-terminality rule is a run-completion rule rather than a per-line rule; applied per line it would refuse every append made while a unit is running.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A journal whose last line is an `intent` with no matching `result` validates with zero diagnostics naming the unmatched intent, and the next `orchestrate journal append` on that journal succeeds.
- [x] AC-2: `computeResumeState` classifies the same journal as an interrupted verb, so the unmatched intent is still observable where its interpretation belongs.
- [x] AC-3: A `complete` line whose `lane_plan` carries a lane with no terminal state is refused; the identical journal state evaluated on a non-`complete`, non-`final-verify` line produces no terminality diagnostic.
- [x] AC-4: A `complete` line naming an integrated lane that carries no `merge_sha` is refused.
- [x] AC-5: An append made while a unit is still running succeeds, so the terminality rule fires on no intermediate append.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/orchestrator/waves-validate.fr-node-139.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5 | 8/8 pass. AC-1 asserts a journal ending in an unmatched execute-unit intent validates to zero diagnostics, and walks every prefix of a four-line write-ahead sequence asserting each validates clean - the append verb validates the candidate journal, so a clean prefix at length n+1 is the next append succeeding. AC-2 asserts computeResumeState over that same journal reports nextAction.interrupted true, verb execute-unit and args.lane lane-2. AC-3 asserts a complete line whose lane_plan declares two lanes with one unsettled yields exactly [lane-not-terminal], the identical state on a non-complete non-final-verify line yields [], and a final-verify line also carries the rule. AC-4 asserts a complete naming an integrate-lane result with no merge_sha yields integrated-lane-without-merge-sha. AC-5 asserts an append made while lane-2 is still running validates clean, and a terminal lane_disposition of kind refuted counts as settled. |

### FR-NODE-140 — Executable waves.jsonl parser with run scoping, engine filtering and 1.0.0-1.4.0 schema support

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [kiwi-orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md), [waves-event journal contract](../../skills/claude/_shared/kiwi/waves-event.md) |

#### Requirement

The system SHALL provide an executable `waves.jsonl` parser `parseWavesJournal(root, {runId, engine})` that returns a journal view carrying `runId`, `engine`, the parsed `lines`, a `byVerb` index keyed on the tuple `verb|wave|stage|lane`, a `latestPerWave` map, the observed `schemaVersions` and parse `diagnostics`. The parser SHALL restrict the returned view to events of the requested `run_id`, SHALL filter events by producing engine treating a line that carries no `engine` field as `kiwi-wave-master`, and SHALL accept every schema version in the closed set 1.0.0, 1.1.0, 1.2.0, 1.3.0 and 1.4.0 while reporting a version outside that set as a diagnostic. It SHALL obtain its line parsing from the existing workflow JSONL reader invoked with append-only event keying, so that many lines per run produce no duplicate-key diagnostic and no correction resolution, rather than forking a second JSONL implementation.

#### Rationale

05 section 3.2 and section 10.1 (`waves-journal.ts`). Nothing in `src/` reads `waves.jsonl` today, so every invariant of the journal is applied by an agent rather than by code. The engine filter closes a live defect named in 05 section 4.2: with no producer discriminator, `kiwi-wave-master --resume` selects an orchestrator run as its own most recent incomplete run. The module is written fresh against the mainline `waves-event.md` contract, carrying only the transition table, per the 06 audit ruling A2 recorded at 05 section 0.4 - `event-schema.ts` and `waves.ts` are not ported.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `parseWavesJournal` called with a `runId` over a journal holding two interleaved runs returns a view whose `lines` contain only the requested run's events, and whose `latestPerWave` is computed from that run alone.
- [x] AC-2: `parseWavesJournal` called with `engine: "kiwi-orchestrator"` excludes every line whose `engine` is `kiwi-wave-master` and every line carrying no `engine` field; called with `engine: "kiwi-wave-master"` it includes the lines carrying no `engine` field.
- [x] AC-3: A journal whose lines carry schema versions 1.0.0, 1.1.0, 1.2.0, 1.3.0 and 1.4.0 parses with no unsupported-version diagnostic on any of the five, and a line carrying a version outside that set is reported as a diagnostic rather than silently accepted.
- [x] AC-4: The returned view exposes `runId`, `engine`, `lines`, `byVerb`, `latestPerWave`, `schemaVersions` and `diagnostics`, and `byVerb` is keyed such that two lines differing only in `lane` occupy different keys and two lines differing only in `stage` occupy different keys.
- [x] AC-5: Parsing a run whose journal holds many lines produces no duplicate-key diagnostic and resolves no correction chain, because the parser passes append-only event keying to the shared reader.

#### Verification Evidence

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/orchestrator/waves-journal.fr-node-140.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5 | 8/8 pass. AC-1 asserts a journal interleaving run-a and run-b returns only run-a's two lines and a latestPerWave built from run-a alone. AC-2 asserts engine kiwi-orchestrator yields only the orchestrator line while kiwi-wave-master yields both the explicit wave-master line and the line carrying no engine field. AC-3 asserts WAVES_SCHEMA_VERSIONS is exactly 1.0.0 through 1.4.0, that a journal carrying all five parses with no SRS-W055 diagnostic and reports all five in schemaVersions, and that a 2.0.0 line produces exactly one SRS-W055 naming line 1. AC-4 asserts the view's key set is exactly byVerb, diagnostics, engine, latestPerWave, lines, runId and schemaVersions, and that three execute-unit results differing only in lane or stage occupy three distinct byVerb keys. AC-5 asserts a 41-line run yields no SRS-W053 duplicate-key and no SRS-W054 correction-chain diagnostic and that a status CORRECTION line stays an ordinary line rather than superseding anything. |

### FR-NODE-141 — Journal validator enforces every mechanical round invariant as a named violation code

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [kiwi-orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md), [waves-event journal contract](../../skills/claude/_shared/kiwi/waves-event.md) |

#### Requirement

The system SHALL provide a journal validator that enforces every mechanical round invariant of the `waves-event.md` round-record section as a named violation code drawn from a closed set, and that set SHALL contain the eleven codes `unmapped-design-item`, `constraint-violation`, `unapproved-damage`, `new-regression`, `no-baseline-nonzero-exit`, `denominator-mismatch`, `truncated-residual`, `complete-without-verification`, `verification-without-status`, `fix-in-clean-round` and `cap-exhausted`. `complete-without-verification` SHALL report a `complete` event that carries no `verification` object as unverified rather than as clean, and SHALL be reported rather than treated as a hard failure. `verification-without-status` SHALL refuse a reading that infers completion from a `verification` object alone, `status` being the only positive completion signal and `verification` being evidence rather than authority.

#### Rationale

05 section 10.1's violation-code table, sourced from the round-record bullets of `waves-event.md` for nine codes and from `kiwi-wave-master` for `fix-in-clean-round` and `cap-exhausted`. The two completion-reading codes were absent from the earlier validator table; omitting them would have set the wrong denominator for the cross-variant parity check that is meant to catch exactly this drift. 05 section 10.3 requires each rule asserted in both directions plus a composite fixture, with the rule set enumerated by measurement rather than a hard-coded count.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Each of the eleven violation codes is asserted in both directions by a named fixture: a payload violating exactly that rule is refused with that code, and the minimal legal payload for the same rule is accepted.
- [x] AC-2: A composite fixture that satisfies every other rule in the validator's two rule tables and violates exactly one is refused, and the composite fixtures are enumerated over those tables by measurement rather than against a fixed count.
- [x] AC-3: A `complete` event carrying no `verification` object yields `complete-without-verification` and is reported as unverified, and validation does not fail hard on it.
- [x] AC-4: A reading that would infer completion from a `verification` object while `status` is not a completion signal is refused with `verification-without-status`.
- [x] AC-5: The test that pairs violation codes with their `waves-event.md` source bullets derives the bullet set by measuring the cited ranges rather than comparing against a hard-coded count, so a bullet added to `waves-event.md` with no matching violation code fails the test.

#### Verification Evidence

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/orchestrator/waves-validate.fr-node-141.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5 | 7/7 pass. AC-1 asserts VIOLATION_CODES is exactly the eleven named codes in order and equals VIOLATION_RULES' code list, then for every rule asserts the violating fixture's code list contains that code and the legal baseline yields [], plus a separate isolation test asserting each journal composite's code list equals exactly [code] so no fixture tests two rules at once. AC-2 asserts the CASES key set equals the measured code set of VIOLATION_RULES concatenated with JOURNAL_RULES, so a row added to either table with no fixture fails rather than being counted against a literal. AC-3 asserts a complete with verification undefined yields exactly one complete-without-verification at severity warning and no error-severity diagnostic. AC-4 asserts a verification-bearing waveVerify with status undefined yields verification-without-status. AC-5 measures the bullets in waves-event.md section 2.3, asserts the denominator is non-empty, asserts every rule sourceBullets anchor and every declared non-source anchor resolves to exactly one bullet, and asserts no bullet is left unclaimed. |

### FR-NODE-142 — Journal validator enforces the wave completion gate with run-scoped downgrade closure

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [kiwi-orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md), [waves-event journal contract](../../skills/claude/_shared/kiwi/waves-event.md) |

#### Requirement

The journal validator SHALL enforce the wave completion gate: a `complete` event is valid only when the same run's same wave carries a latest wave-verify record whose verification verdict is `pass`. The gate SHALL consult the latest such record only, so that a non-terminal in-progress round does not satisfy it. The schema version 1.1.0 applicability exemption SHALL be evaluated per run rather than per line, so that the presence of one line at version 1.1.0 or higher anywhere in a run subjects every `complete` in that run to the gate regardless of the event's own recorded `schema_version`. The gate SHALL NOT apply to `wave="all"` run-scope events, and `wave="all"` events SHALL be excluded from per-wave status computation.

#### Rationale

05 section 3.2 and section 10.1's journal-rule table, over the `waves-event.md` verification gate. Without the gate a journal that skipped verification entirely is byte-identical to one that did not, so the omission is undetectable after the fact. The exemption is run-scoped because the schema version is written by the producer being policed, which makes a per-line exemption a downgrade bypass.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A `complete` for wave n in a run that carries no wave-verify record for that run and wave is refused.
- [x] AC-2: A run whose wave n carries wave-verify records in the order pass then in-progress, followed by `complete`, is refused, because the gate reads only the latest record.
- [x] AC-3: A run containing at least one line at schema version 1.1.0 or higher subjects every `complete` in that run to the gate, including a `complete` whose own `schema_version` is 1.0.0.
- [x] AC-4: A run recorded entirely below schema version 1.1.0 has its existing `complete` events respected and reported as unverified rather than refused.
- [x] AC-5: A `complete` carrying `wave="all"` is exempt from the gate, and `wave="all"` events are excluded when the latest per-wave status is computed, so no phantom wave is reported incomplete.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/orchestrator/waves-validate.fr-node-142.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5 | 8/8 pass. AC-1 asserts a complete with no wave-verify record for its run and wave yields complete-without-latest-pass and that adding the pass record clears it; two further tests assert a later wave's verify record and a verify record appended after the complete both fail to satisfy it. AC-2 asserts pass then in-progress then complete is refused while in-progress then pass then complete is accepted, so only the latest record counts. AC-3 asserts a complete whose own schema_version is 1.0.0 is still gated when the run carries a 1.1.0 line. AC-4 asserts a run recorded entirely at 1.0.0 yields only complete-without-verification at severity warning and that computeRunProgress still reports wave 1 complete. AC-5 asserts the wave=all final-verify line - status complete - validates clean without a wave-verify record of its own and that waveStatuses holds only key 1, so no phantom wave is reported. |

### FR-NODE-143 — Journal validator enforces run scoping without version exemption and the run-completion predicate

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [kiwi-orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md), [waves-event journal contract](../../skills/claude/_shared/kiwi/waves-event.md) |

#### Requirement

The journal validator SHALL scope every completion judgment to the current `run_id` with no schema-version exemption of any kind, so that another run's `complete` never satisfies this run's wave, and this scoping SHALL apply retroactively to journals recorded at any version. The validator SHALL evaluate the run-completion predicate as: every wave of the run is `complete` and the latest `final-verify` event's verification verdict is `pass`. A `final-verify` that did not pass SHALL be recorded with `status=failed` and never as `complete`. The `final-verify` conjunct of the predicate SHALL be subject to a run-scoped schema version 1.2.0 exemption: a run recorded entirely below 1.2.0 is complete on all waves being `complete` alone, while one line at 1.2.0 or higher anywhere in the run subjects it to the conjunct regardless of the individual event's version.

#### Rationale

05 section 10.1's journal-rule table over the `waves-event.md` resume contract. Run scoping alone carries no version exemption because an exemption there preserves the path by which another run's `complete` is read as this run's, whose consequence is reporting a whole run complete with zero implementation; the exemption protects nothing and loses everything, so it is applied retroactively. The `final-verify` conjunct keeps its 1.2.0 exemption, run-scoped for the same downgrade-bypass reason as the wave gate.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A journal in which run A carries `complete` for wave 1 and run B does not reports wave 1 of run B as incomplete.
- [x] AC-2: A `complete` recorded at schema version 1.0.0 in another run is still refused as evidence for this run, so run scoping carries no version exemption.
- [x] AC-3: A run whose waves are all `complete` but whose latest `final-verify` verdict is not `pass` is not reported complete, and resume targets the final verification rather than reporting the run done.
- [x] AC-4: A run whose waves are all `complete` and whose latest `final-verify` verdict is `pass` is reported complete.
- [x] AC-5: A `final-verify` event that did not pass and is recorded with `status=complete` is refused; the same event recorded with `status=failed` is accepted.
- [x] AC-6: A run recorded entirely below schema version 1.2.0 is reported complete on all waves being `complete` alone, while a run containing at least one line at 1.2.0 or higher is subject to the `final-verify` conjunct regardless of the individual event's recorded version.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

### FR-NODE-144 — evaluateRound is a pure verdict predicate given the loop mode explicitly

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [kiwi-orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md), [waves-event journal contract](../../skills/claude/_shared/kiwi/waves-event.md) |

#### Requirement

The system SHALL provide `evaluateRound(round)` as a pure predicate over a single round argument returning verdict, violations, streak, remaining cap and an unreachable-PASS flag. The verdict SHALL be drawn from the closed set `pass`, `pass-with-residual`, `fail-residual`, `fail-cap` and `invalid`. The round's mode SHALL be supplied by the caller as one of `normal`, `max`, `mini` and `explicit` and SHALL never be inferred by the function from the round's other fields. A round whose enumerated row count differs from the round's frozen denominator SHALL be `invalid`, consuming the cap and resetting the streak to zero, which is distinct from failing the round. When the rounds remaining under the cap are fewer than the mode's required consecutive clean streak, the function SHALL report PASS as unreachable and return `fail-cap` rather than recommending a further round. A round in which a fix was applied SHALL NOT be the passing round.

#### Rationale

05 section 7.1 and section 10.1. The verification engine's mechanical arithmetic - caps, streaks, unreachable-PASS and the invalid-round rule - exists today only as skill prose applied by an agent. The mode is a parameter rather than an inference because the recorded round object carries neither mode nor variant and a cap value alone is ambiguous between `--max` and an explicit `--loops`; the design keeps this rule out of the journal validator for exactly that reason and places it here, where the caller supplies the mode.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `evaluateRound` returns all five of verdict, violations, streak, remaining cap and the unreachable flag, and its verdict is one of the five closed values `pass`, `pass-with-residual`, `fail-residual`, `fail-cap` and `invalid`.
- [x] AC-2: A round whose enumerated row count differs from its frozen denominator returns verdict `invalid` with the cap decremented and the streak reset to 0, and the result is distinguishable from a `fail-residual` round.
- [x] AC-3: A round in which a fix was applied and which carries no violations does not return `pass`.
- [x] AC-4: A round whose remaining rounds under the cap are fewer than the mode's required consecutive clean streak returns the unreachable flag true and verdict `fail-cap` with no further round recommended; the same round with a larger cap does not.
- [x] AC-5: Two calls identical except for mode can return different verdicts, and the function derives no mode from the round's other fields.
- [x] AC-6: `evaluateRound` performs no file, process or network access and returns identical output for two calls with the same round value.

#### Verification Evidence

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/orchestrator/verification-gate.fr-node-144.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6 | 12/12 pass. AC-1 asserts the outcome key set is exactly capRemaining, streak, unreachable, verdict, violations, that ROUND_VERDICTS is exactly pass, pass-with-residual, fail-residual, fail-cap, invalid, and that the verdict is a member. AC-2 asserts a round whose three rows differ from frozenDenominator 4 returns invalid with violation denominator-mismatch, capRemaining decremented to 3 and streak reset to 0, and that a fail-residual round is a distinguishable verdict. AC-3 asserts a clean round with fixAppliedThisRound true returns fail-residual with violations exactly [fix-in-clean-round] and streak 0, not pass. AC-4 asserts a dirty round at index 8 of cap 8 under max returns unreachable true, verdict fail-cap and capRemaining 0, while the same round at cap 12 returns unreachable false and fail-residual. AC-5 asserts the same round returns pass under normal and fail-residual under max, that mode explicit with cap 8 still passes where max with cap 8 does not so the mode is never inferred from cap, and that REQUIRED_CLEAN_STREAK declares a value for all four LOOP_MODES. AC-6 asserts two calls over one round value are equal, the argument is unmutated, and evaluateRound.length is 1. |
| VE-2 | inspection | src/core/orchestrator/verification-gate.ts | AC-6 | No-access half of AC-6, measured 2026-08-01: verification-gate.ts declares exactly one import, { REQUIRED_CLEAN_STREAK, LoopMode, ReasonClass, RoundVerdict, ViolationCode } from ./journal-schema.js, and journal-schema.ts declares no import at all. The module's import closure is those two files, so there is no file, process or network access to reach. |

### FR-NODE-145 — computeLanePlan is pure and byte-deterministic over its nine declared inputs

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [kiwi-orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

The system SHALL provide `computeLanePlan(input)` as a pure, deterministic function over its nine declared inputs - catalog, registry, existing modules, existing paths, prior postmortems, design item map, lane cap, code roots and test roots - such that two calls with equal inputs produce byte-identical output. Path existence SHALL arrive injected as the existing-paths input and SHALL never be read from the filesystem. Each lane's design items SHALL be derived as the sorted, deduplicated union of the design item map over that lane's requirement ids. A path absent from the existing-paths input SHALL suppress the prefix-directory clause of the write-set-overlap rule, because a directory that does not yet exist cannot be a shared parent. A prior-postmortem row whose resolution is `merge-into-one-lane` SHALL emit a `learned-coupling` same-lane edge between its originating and target tasks, or, when either task id is absent from this wave's catalogue, between the tasks whose declared files contain the row's path; a row carrying any other resolution SHALL emit no edge. After topological layering, a stage holding more lanes than the lane cap SHALL be split into consecutive stages deterministically, longest-first.

#### Rationale

05 section 5.3, with the per-input effect table. Determinism is load-bearing rather than stylistic: the resume-time lane-plan drift digest recomputes the plan from the inputs the lock itself records and compares byte-for-byte, so an impure planner makes that check fire on any unrelated file creation. The design refused an impure path-existence check for this reason and moved it to the command (design row E8a).

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Two calls to `computeLanePlan` with the same nine inputs produce byte-identical serialised output, asserted over repeated calls on the same fixture.
- [x] AC-2: The lane-plan module reads no path from the filesystem: a call whose injected existing-paths array deliberately disagrees with the real repository returns the plan the injected array implies.
- [x] AC-3: Each lane's design items equal the sorted, deduplicated union of the design item map over that lane's requirement ids, and a fixture whose map sends two of a lane's requirements to an overlapping item set yields each item once.
- [x] AC-4: A fixture in which two tasks declare paths sharing a parent directory absent from the existing-paths input does not force them into one lane; the same fixture with that directory present in the existing-paths input does.
- [x] AC-5: A prior-postmortem row resolved `merge-into-one-lane` places its two tasks in one lane under a `learned-coupling` edge; a row resolved `re-planned` or `accepted` produces no edge; and a row whose task ids are absent from this wave's catalogue couples the tasks whose declared files contain the row's path.
- [x] AC-6: A fixture whose layering places more lanes in one stage than the lane cap is split into consecutive stages longest-first, and two calls with the same inputs produce the same split; a call differing only in the lane cap produces a different split.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/orchestrator/lane-plan.fr-node-145.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6 | 16/16 pass. AC-1 asserts the input object's key set is exactly catalog, codeRoots, designItemMap, existingModules, existingPaths, laneCap, priorPostmortems, registry, testRoots - nine keys, asserted by length - and that two JSON.stringify results agree; task declaration order within a component does not change the output. AC-2 asserts a catalogue naming src/core/orchestrator, which exists in this checkout, splits across lanes when existingPaths is empty, while a catalogue naming src/no-such-directory, which does not exist, is folded into one lane when the injected array asserts it - both directions the disk would refuse. AC-3 asserts a lane's designItems equal the sorted deduplicated union over its reqIds, that an overlapping map yields each item once, and that an unmapped req id yields []. AC-4 asserts two tasks sharing parent src/core/orchestrator land in different lanes when that directory is absent from existingPaths and in one lane when present. AC-5 asserts a merge-into-one-lane row emits {a,b,reason: learned-coupling} and co-locates its tasks, that re-planned and accepted emit no such edge and leave the tasks apart, and that a row whose task ids are gone falls back to the tasks whose declared files contain its path. AC-6 asserts six components under laneCap 2 split into three stages sized [6,5],[4,3],[2,2] longest-first, that two calls agree while laneCap 3 gives a different split with stageCount 2, and that a stage inside the cap is untouched. The suite also asserts byte-determinism over all five pinned real sidecars. |

### FR-NODE-146 — Lane plan output is a checkable partition of the sidecar task catalogue

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [kiwi-orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

The lane plan SHALL be a partition of the sidecar task catalogue: every task id present in the input catalogue SHALL appear exactly once across the union of the lanes' task ids, the serial epilogue and the unassigned set. The function SHALL assert this completeness invariant over its own output and SHALL report a violation as an internal error rather than as a warning. The unassigned field SHALL be present in the output shape even when empty, so that the invariant remains a checkable partition and the phase-2 executor consumes an identical shape.

#### Rationale

05 section 5.3. This is the partition-layer analogue of the decomposition coverage gate: a silently dropped task is the execution-model equivalent of an unassigned design item, and nothing else in the pipeline would notice it, because every downstream consumer reads the plan rather than the catalogue.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: For every lane-plan fixture, the union of the lanes' task ids, the serial epilogue and the unassigned set equals the input catalogue's task-id set exactly, with no task appearing twice and none missing.
- [x] AC-2: The unassigned field is present in the returned plan even when empty, so the partition is checkable without inferring the empty case.
- [x] AC-3: The completeness assertion is evaluated over the characterization fixtures built from the pinned real sidecars as well as over the synthetic fixtures, so a degenerate grouping a small fixture cannot express is still covered.
- [x] AC-4: A completeness violation is surfaced as an internal error that fails the call rather than as a warning attached to a returned plan.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/orchestrator/lane-plan.fr-node-146.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4 | 10/10 pass. AC-1 asserts over seven synthetic fixtures - empty catalogue, a single lane-eligible task, everything routed to the epilogue, one component holding every task, all-singletons, a mixed barrier/epilogue/lane wave, and a cap-split stage - that the concatenation of lane task ids, serialEpilogue and unassigned has the same length as the declared catalogue, sorts equal to it, and holds no duplicate. AC-2 asserts unassigned is a present key equal to [] both on a one-task plan and after a JSON round trip, and that an empty catalogue returns every collection empty. AC-3 runs the same partition assertion over all five pinned real sidecars, and again over them under a three-point registry with laneCap 2 and narrow roots; it additionally asserts the 206-task fixture forms lanes holding more than 100 tasks beside a non-empty epilogue, so the partition cannot be satisfied by routing everything to the epilogue. AC-4 asserts assertLanePlanPartition throws lane-plan-incomplete on a dropped task and on a doubly-placed task, names the offending id, accepts an exact cover, and that the returned plan's key set carries no warnings or diagnostics channel a violation could be downgraded into. |

### FR-NODE-147 — Lane plan conflict model is the closed conflict_reason enum with every member reachable

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [kiwi-orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

The system SHALL express the lane-plan conflict model as the closed `conflict_reason` enum whose members are `task-dependency`, `phase-dependency`, `write-set-overlap`, `tdd-pair`, `req-shared`, `convergence-point`, `module-barrier`, `unknown-write-set`, `srs-write`, `non-code-write-set` and `learned-coupling`, computed by `analyzeConflicts` over five arguments: the catalog, the convergence registry, the existing modules, the prior postmortems and the constraints carrying code roots and test roots. Write-set overlap, TDD pairs, shared requirement ids and learned couplings SHALL force their tasks into one lane; unknown write sets and module barriers SHALL raise a stage barrier; SRS writes and non-code write sets SHALL route their task to the serial epilogue. A convergence-point match SHALL consult the matched point's recipe kind under the most-restrictive-wins precedence order orchestrator-only, then replay, then regenerate, then exclusive-lane, with exclusive-lane producing a lane-eligible assignment under whole-wave uniqueness and the other three producing serial-epilogue assignment. The module-barrier predicate SHALL fire when the task's case-folded action string contains an existing-module entry, by full repo-relative path or by basename, together with a member of the closed structural-change marker vocabulary; the SRS-write predicate SHALL fire when the case-folded action string contains a SpecKiwi mutation verb outside the four calls the coder skill already makes inline. Neither predicate SHALL claim completeness: an action that omits the marker SHALL produce no edge, and that omission SHALL be recorded as declared behaviour rather than denied.

#### Rationale

05 section 5.2 with the recipe-kind lane-eligibility rule, the precedence order and the two lexical predicates, plus section 5.3 for the learned-coupling member. Reachability rather than mere presence is the bar because the earlier partitioner sent any registry match to the serial epilogue, leaving the exclusive-lane recipe dead while a presence-only test still passed. The non-code-write-set member is charter clause C1's first sentence, which nothing previously enforced: a task typed infra with a config file in its write set grounded cleanly, raised no barrier and became lane-eligible.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `analyzeConflicts` takes exactly the five declared arguments, and the `learned-coupling` and `non-code-write-set` edges are each derivable only from the fourth and fifth of them respectively.
- [x] AC-2: Every one of the eleven `conflict_reason` values is produced by at least one fixture input that the declared input types admit, so no enum member is dead.
- [x] AC-3: Every one of the four recipe kinds is reached: exclusive-lane yields a lane-eligible assignment in which at most one lane in the wave owns the unit and every task touching it is placed in that lane, while orchestrator-only, regenerate and replay each yield serial-epilogue assignment.
- [x] AC-4: A path matching two registry points with different recipe kinds resolves at plan time to the most restrictive under the order orchestrator-only, replay, regenerate, exclusive-lane.
- [x] AC-5: The module-barrier predicate fires for a fixture whose action names an existing-module entry by full repo-relative path together with a marker from the closed vocabulary, and again for one naming it by basename; it does not fire when the marker is absent, and that non-firing is asserted as declared behaviour.
- [x] AC-6: The SRS-write predicate fires for a fixture whose action names a SpecKiwi mutation verb outside the four inline coder calls, and does not fire for an action naming only those four.
- [x] AC-7: A task whose type is outside code and perf_test, or any of whose declared files or test files falls outside the union of code roots and test roots, is assigned to the serial epilogue under `non-code-write-set`; a task satisfying both constraints is lane-eligible.
- [x] AC-8: A task whose declared files are empty, or any of whose entries carries an inferred-write-set label, raises `unknown-write-set` as a stage barrier.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/orchestrator/conflict.fr-node-147.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6, AC-7, AC-8 | 27/27 pass. AC-1 asserts analyzeConflicts.length is 5, that learned-coupling appears only when the fourth argument carries the postmortem row, and that non-code-write-set appears only under the fifth argument's roots. AC-2 asserts CONFLICT_REASONS has eleven members and that one composite fixture reaches all eleven, with task-dependency asserted over the transitive closure and phase-dependency lifted across both phases' tasks. AC-3 asserts RECIPE_KINDS is exactly orchestrator-only, replay, regenerate, exclusive-lane, that a registry match produces the binary exclusive-lane edge and three unary edges, and in the assignment half that every task touching an exclusive-lane unit lands in one lane no other lane owns while orchestrator-only, regenerate and replay all land in the serial epilogue. AC-4 asserts resolveRecipeKind over five pairings and a two-point registry match resolving docs/spec/00.index.md to orchestrator-only. AC-5 asserts module-barrier fires for an action naming the module by full path and by basename, and does not fire when the marker is absent or when a marker appears with no module mention. AC-6 asserts srs-write fires for each of eleven mutation verbs and does not fire for an action naming only add_trace_link, add_verification_evidence, update_status and add_completed_work. AC-7 asserts each of six non-code types and a test file outside the roots yields non-code-write-set while code and perf_test inside the roots yield no edge. AC-8 asserts an empty files list and an INFERRED-labelled entry each yield unknown-write-set. NOTE on the requirement body: the fifth argument is described as constraints carrying code roots and test roots; the implemented ConflictConstraints carries existingPaths as well, because write-set-overlap's prefix-directory clause has no other input (NOTES.mechanical-findings.md M-03). The arity of five that AC-1 asserts is unaffected. |

### FR-NODE-148 — Lane plan serialized ordering matches selectNextTask over the same catalogue

| Field | Value |

| Status | verified |

#### Requirement

The lane plan's serialized output SHALL be the exact task sequence the existing serial task selector produces over the same catalogue, so that degrading a run to serial execution is behaviour-preserving rather than merely argued to be.

#### Rationale

05 section 5.3. Serial execution is phase 1's whole executor and the documented fallback in later phases, so the claim that it preserves behaviour is load-bearing. The design requires it asserted as a differential oracle against the existing implementation rather than against a reimplementation of the ordering.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A differential test drives the existing serial task selector over a catalogue to exhaustion, marking each returned task done, and asserts the resulting sequence is element-wise identical to the lane plan's serialized output for the same catalogue.
- [x] AC-2: The differential test runs over the pinned real-sidecar characterization fixtures as well as the synthetic fixtures.
- [x] AC-3: The oracle in the test is the existing selector implementation, not a reimplementation of its ordering inside the test.

AC-2 runs the same differential over all five pinned real sidecars, asserting each oracle sequence is non-empty first so no comparison is vacuous. AC-3 is structural: the helper driveSerialSelector imports selectNextTask from src/core/workflow/validate.js and only turns the crank - it contains no ordering logic of its own. |
