### FR-NODE-149 — Sidecar task catalogue additionally carries files, test files, covered ACs, type and action

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [kiwi-orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

The sidecar task catalogue and its normalisation SHALL additionally carry each task's declared files with their optional line ranges, its declared test files, its covered acceptance-criterion ids, its type and its action string, parsed additively from the sidecar. The extension SHALL NOT change the fields or the behaviour any existing consumer of the catalogue reads today.

#### Rationale

05 section 5.1 and section 10.1. The existing catalogue entry and sidecar task types carry none of the fields the conflict classifier needs, so every conflict edge is unreachable until the parse is extended. The design declares the regression surface rather than hiding it: this modifies a module already covered by the workflow validator suite.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A catalogue entry parsed from a sidecar exposes the declared files as path-and-optional-line-range entries, the declared test files, the covered acceptance-criterion ids, the type and the action string, each carrying the value the sidecar declared.
- [x] AC-2: A sidecar that omits the new fields parses without error and yields the catalogue an existing consumer sees today, so the extension is parse-additive.
- [x] AC-3: The existing workflow validator suite, including serial task selection, cycle detection, missing-dependency detection and blocked-by reporting, passes unchanged against the extended entry type.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/orchestrator/task-catalog.fr-node-149.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2 | 10/10 pass. AC-1 asserts a parsed entry exposes type code, the verbatim action string, files as path-and-optional-lineRange entries, testFiles likewise, coversAc [AC-1, AC-4], and additionally the declared tdd phase; separate tests assert an [INFERRED:level] label is stripped and recorded as inferred true and that paths are normalised to repo-relative POSIX. AC-2 asserts a sidecar omitting every new field yields exactly the seven pre-existing fields an existing consumer reads - id, phase_id, title, depends_on_task, req_ids, legacyReqIds, status - with no diagnostics, and that every new field defaults conservatively. DISCREPANCY, not silently absorbed: the requirement names five additional fields; the shipped entry carries eight - the five named plus tdd, phaseDependsOn and a per-file inferred flag. Each of the three was added because a closed-enum member of FR-NODE-147 was otherwise unreachable: tdd for tdd-pair, phaseDependsOn for phase-dependency, inferred for unknown-write-set. Recorded in NOTES.mechanical-findings.md M-02, M-03 and M-04. AC-1 as worded is satisfied because it requires those five to be exposed, not that they are the only ones. |

Serial task selection is additionally driven to exhaustion against the extended catalogue by test/core/orchestrator/lane-plan.fr-node-148.test.ts, 9/9 pass. |

### FR-NODE-150 — computeResumeState derives the next verb, recovery class, reconciliation outcome and drift from injected facts

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [kiwi-orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

The system SHALL provide `computeResumeState(view, card, gitFacts, driftInputs)` that derives, with no conversation input, the next verb from the closed verb enum, that verb's recovery class from the closed set `pure-reauthor`, `idempotent-by-key` and `externally-visible`, one of the four reconciliation outcomes `consistent`, `card-stale`, `interrupted-external-action` and `ledger-reconciliation-divergent`, and a drift report carrying exactly one entry per drift digest, indexed 1 to 4, each with an outcome from `match`, `stale-not-wrong` and `drift` and a blocking gate that is `run-invariant-drift`, `lane-plan-drift` or null. Git facts SHALL arrive injected, and the operands of drift digests 2, 3 and 4 SHALL arrive injected as drift inputs carrying both the lane-plan inputs the lock recorded and those same inputs re-digested now. The function SHALL shell out to nothing and its signature SHALL be structurally incapable of accepting conversation state.

#### Rationale

05 section 4.6 and section 4.7, with the input and output shapes in section 10.1. A resumed session has no conversation, so the next action must be derived from artifacts alone; the design records five reachable failures where an earlier per-set reconciliation classified a correctly proceeding run as divergent. The drift operands are injected because digests 2, 3 and 4 read values that no journal view, card or git fact carries, and injecting them keeps the function pure and the impure collection in the command.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: The function's signature accepts exactly the journal view, the card, injected git facts and injected drift inputs, and structurally cannot accept conversation state; the module invokes no git subprocess and reads no path.
- [x] AC-2: A journal carrying an intent line with no matching result for a verb yields the interrupted classification together with that verb's declared recovery class, and each of the three recovery-class values is produced by at least one fixture.
- [x] AC-3: Each of the four reconciliation outcomes is produced by at least one fixture over injected git facts, and the ordinary mid-execution state - a unit started with no result line yet - yields `consistent` rather than `divergent`.
- [x] AC-4: The returned drift report carries exactly four entries, indexed 1 to 4, one per drift digest, each with an outcome from the three declared values and a gate that is `run-invariant-drift`, `lane-plan-drift` or null.
- [x] AC-5: A digest-3 fixture whose recorded and recomputed existing-paths digest or prior-postmortem digests differ yields `stale-not-wrong` with a null gate and no mid-wave recomputation of the lock, while one whose sidecar digest differs yields `drift` with gate `lane-plan-drift`.
- [x] AC-6: The returned state's key set equals the four declared fields: classification, next action, drift and blocking.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/orchestrator/resume.fr-node-150.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6 | 12/12 pass. AC-1 asserts computeResumeState.length is 4 - journal view, card, git facts, drift inputs - and that resume.ts contains none of child_process, node:fs, execSync, spawnSync, simple-git or process.cwd. AC-2 asserts an unmatched intent yields interrupted true with the verb's recovery class for decompose-waves, freeze-lane-plan and execute-unit, that the produced set equals all three RECOVERY_CLASSES, and that a landed result line clears interrupted. AC-3 asserts all four RECONCILIATION_OUTCOMES are produced from injected git facts and that the ordinary mid-execution state - a unit started with no result line - is consistent with blocking null, while a locked worktree gives interrupted-external-action, a lane already an ancestor of integration gives ledger-reconciliation-divergent, and a card naming an unknown lane gives card-stale with blocking null. AC-4 asserts drift.digests has exactly four entries indexed 1 to 4, each with an outcome from DRIFT_OUTCOMES and a gate in {null, run-invariant-drift, lane-plan-drift}. AC-5 asserts a changed existing-paths digest and changed prior-postmortem digests each give digest 3 stale-not-wrong with a null gate and no blocking, while a changed sidecar digest gives drift with gate lane-plan-drift and blocking lane-plan-drift. AC-6 asserts the state's key set is exactly blocking, classification, drift, nextAction. The suite also pins that lane-quarantined is read through lane-state.ts's readLaneDisposition: a disposition kind outside the closed enum classifies not-dispatched with nextVerb execute-unit rather than settling the lane. |

### FR-NODE-151 — Resume card is validated on write against its cap, enums, proofs, digest and rollup rule

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [kiwi-orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

The resume card SHALL be validated on write by a validator that takes the journal view as a parameter, and a card failing validation SHALL NOT be written. Validation SHALL enforce a hard 8 KB cap measured on the serialised card; a next-action verb drawn from the closed verb enum and preconditions drawn from the closed five-value precondition vocabulary; a proof kind from the closed seven-value set on every completed-work entry; a witness whose kind is not `journal` on every completed-work entry whose proof kind is `journal`; an invariant digest recomputable over the frozen block and covering the isolation profile, the integration branch, the base branch, the engine, the work root and the run root; immutability of the frozen isolation profile against the profile the journal's isolation-probe result records; and the rollup rule bounding the completed-work list, the open list and the frozen lane-lock map together.

#### Rationale

05 section 4.1 and section 10.1. The card is the only mandatory read on resume, so an uncapped or unvalidated card degenerates into a second journal and a drifting one silently changes what a resumed session believes. The journal view is a parameter because a function cannot check an input it is not given: the earlier design typed the module as read, validate and write of the card alone while requiring it to enforce a rule against the journal.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A card whose serialised size exceeds 8 KB is refused and not written.
- [x] AC-2: A card whose next-action verb falls outside the closed verb enum is refused, as is one whose preconditions carry a value outside the five `P-DESIGN-FROZEN`, `P-LANE-PLAN-FROZEN`, `P-HANDOFF-VERIFIED`, `P-WAVE-ISSUES-CLOSED` and `P-PRIOR-STAGES-INTEGRATED`.
- [x] AC-3: A completed-work entry whose proof kind falls outside the seven kinds `git-ancestor`, `git-ref`, `git-trailer`, `digest`, `mcp-state`, `fs-exists` and `journal` is refused.
- [x] AC-4: A completed-work entry whose proof kind is `journal` and which carries no witness, or whose witness kind is also `journal`, is refused; the same entry carrying a `git-trailer` witness is accepted.
- [x] AC-5: A card whose invariant digest does not equal the recomputation over the frozen block is refused, and changing any one of the six covered fields - isolation profile, integration branch, base branch, engine, work root, run root - changes the recomputed digest.
- [x] AC-6: A card whose frozen isolation profile differs from the profile recorded on the journal's isolation-probe result is refused, with the journal view supplied to the validator as a parameter rather than read inside it.
- [x] AC-7: A card at the design's maximum - 22 completed-work entries, 8 open entries and a frozen lane-lock map holding only the current wave's entry - validates inside the 8 KB cap, while a card retaining a completed wave's lane-lock entry, or lane-level completed-work entries for a wave that has completed, is refused by the rollup rule.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/orchestrator/resume-card.fr-node-151.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6, AC-7 | 11/11 pass. AC-1 asserts RESUME_CARD_MAX_BYTES is 8192, that a 9000-byte card yields resume-card-too-large, and that writeCard returns ok false carrying no text so nothing is written. AC-2 asserts CARD_PRECONDITIONS is exactly the five named values and that an out-of-enum verb yields unknown-verb and an out-of-enum precondition yields unknown-precondition. AC-3 asserts PROOF_KINDS is exactly the seven named kinds and that an eighth yields unknown-proof-kind. AC-4 asserts a journal-kind proof with no witness and one whose witness is also journal each yield journal-proof-without-witness, while the same entry with a git-trailer witness validates clean. AC-5 asserts a wrong invariant_digest yields invariant-digest-mismatch and that changing each of the six covered fields - isolation profile, integration branch, base branch, engine, work root, run root - changes computeInvariantDigest. AC-6 asserts a card whose frozen isolation profile differs from the journal's probe-isolation result yields isolation-profile-changed while the aligned card validates clean, and that validateCard.length is 2 so the journal view is a parameter. AC-7 builds the design's maximum - 22 done entries, 8 open entries and a lane_lock holding only wave-9 - and asserts it validates clean under 8192 bytes, while a retained wave-0 lane lock yields lane-lock-not-rolled-up and a lane-level entry beside its wave's own entry yields lane-entry-not-rolled-up. |

### FR-NODE-152 — A verdict-bearing journal line never passes validation on a journal proof alone

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [kiwi-orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md), [waves-event journal contract](../../skills/claude/_shared/kiwi/waves-event.md) |

#### Requirement

Journal validation SHALL refuse any verdict-bearing line whose only proof kind is `journal`. A line recording a verification verdict, a lane's integration or a wave's completion SHALL additionally carry at least one externally recomputable proof kind drawn from `git-ancestor`, `git-ref`, `git-trailer`, `digest` and `mcp-state`. A line that records no verdict SHALL NOT be subject to this rule.

#### Rationale

05 section 4.5. The journal kind is the only proof with no external witness, which is why it is the artifact most likely to be truncated and the one whose claims are least recoverable. The writer stamp authenticates the path a line was written by, not the payload it carries, so a journal-only verdict is a claim with no witness at all.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A wave `complete` line whose only proof is of kind `journal` is refused.
- [x] AC-2: The same line carrying one additional proof is accepted, asserted once for each of `git-ancestor`, `git-ref`, `git-trailer`, `digest` and `mcp-state`.
- [x] AC-3: A line that records no verdict is accepted with a `journal` proof alone, so the rule's subject is the verdict-bearing set and not every line.
- [x] AC-4: A verification-verdict line and a wave-completion line are each covered by the rule, asserted separately, so the verdict-bearing set is not narrowed to one event kind.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/orchestrator/waves-validate.fr-node-152.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4 | 6/6 pass. AC-1 asserts a wave complete carrying a single journal-kind proof yields journal-only-verdict. AC-2 asserts EXTERNAL_PROOF_KINDS is exactly git-ancestor, git-ref, git-trailer, digest, mcp-state and that adding each one in turn beside the journal proof validates clean, while a second journal proof does not. AC-3 asserts a non-verdict-bearing plan-wave result carrying a journal proof alone validates clean. AC-4 asserts the rule separately on a verdict-bearing wave-verify round record, on a wave complete and on the run-scope final-verify, each yielding exactly [journal-only-verdict]; a further test covers an integrate-lane result. STATED BOUNDARY, recorded rather than implied: the rule fires on a verdict-bearing line that CARRIES proofs which are all journal. A line carrying no proof field at all is not refused, because refusing absence would refuse every shipped 1.0.0 to 1.3.0 complete. No AC on this requirement asserts the absence case, so the boundary narrows no criterion. |

### FR-NODE-153 — Run-root preflight comparison is a pure function over four normalisation rules taking both roots

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [kiwi-orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

The system SHALL expose the run-root preflight comparison as a function taking both roots and an injected realpath probe, returning whether they match, both normalised forms, and the rule that decided the comparison, where the rule is drawn from the closed four-value set `separators`, `trailing-separator`, `windows-case` and `realpath`. The function SHALL perform no filesystem access of its own, the realpath answer arriving through the injected probe. The preflight command SHALL accept the MCP workspace root and the git toplevel as explicit arguments supplied by the caller, SHALL NOT derive either root from its own working directory or its own server root, and SHALL raise `run-root-preflight-mismatch` when the two roots do not match after normalisation.

#### Rationale

05 section 10.1 and section 10.6. The check cannot be performed inside a tool on either surface: the CLI resolves its root from the caller's working directory, which makes the comparison vacuous, and the MCP server fixes its workspace root to its own process directory, so a comparison issued inside it always matches. The agent therefore obtains both values and the tool performs the judgment-free normalisation and comparison - the boundary rule applied to the one gate that cannot be fully automated.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Two roots differing only in path separators match under rule `separators`; two differing only in a trailing separator match under `trailing-separator`; two differing only in letter case including the drive letter match under `windows-case`; and two resolving through the injected probe to one real path match under `realpath`.
- [x] AC-2: Each of the four rules also has a mismatch fixture in which the two roots genuinely differ and the result reports no match.
- [x] AC-3: The returned rule is never a value outside the closed four-value set.
- [x] AC-4: The function performs no filesystem access: a fixture supplying a probe that maps two distinct inputs to one real path reports a match without touching disk, and the module resolves no path itself.
- [x] AC-5: The preflight command takes both roots as arguments and raises `run-root-preflight-mismatch` when they do not match after normalisation; it derives neither root from its own working directory nor from its own server root, so the comparison can never be a root compared against itself.

#### Verification Evidence

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/orchestrator/preflight.fr-node-153.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4 | 12/12 pass. AC-1 asserts one match fixture per rule, each naming the rule that decided it: backslash vs forward slash gives separators, a trailing slash gives trailing-separator, mixed case including the drive letter gives windows-case, and two spellings the injected probe maps to one path give realpath. AC-2 asserts a genuine-difference mismatch fixture for each of the same four subjects. AC-3 asserts ROOT_NORMALISATION_RULES is exactly [separators, trailing-separator, windows-case, realpath] and that every one of eight fixtures, including the empty string and bare roots, returns a rule inside that set. AC-4 asserts a probe returning a path that exists on no disk still produces a match under rule realpath with both inputs consulted in order, and that an earlier rule deciding the comparison leaves the probe entirely unconsulted. |
| VE-2 | inspection | src/core/orchestrator/preflight.ts | AC-4 | Resolves-no-path half of AC-4, measured 2026-08-01: preflight.ts declares no import at all, so the module has no filesystem or path-resolution facility to reach; realpath arrives only as the injected RealpathProbe parameter. |
| VE-3 | test | test/cli/orchestrate-surface.ir-cli-082.test.ts | AC-5 | 14/14 pass. Command half: orchestrate preflight declares --mcp-root and --git-root as mandatory options; run from this repository, which is neither root, two distinct temp roots exit 2 with gate run-root-preflight-mismatch while the same temp root passed twice exits 0 with ok true - a root defaulted from the working directory or the server root would have made that second case a mismatch. |

### FR-NODE-154 — Handoff validation enforces the thirteen task fields, set equality, ten ordered headings and the front-matter schema

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [kiwi-orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

Handoff validation SHALL count thirteen fields per task - id, type, requirement ids, files, test files, action, acceptance tests, verification command, definition of done, rollback, covered acceptance criteria, task dependencies and TDD phase - against an expected count of thirteen times the number of declared task ids, and a checked count differing from the expected count SHALL invalidate the round. It SHALL require equality, not subset, of the declared task ids against the assignment for the handoff's kind and of the declared write set against the lane's computed write set. It SHALL require the ten body headings Setup, Objective, Context, Interfaces, Tasks, Acceptance, Constraints, Out of scope, Manifest and Escalation, all present and in that order. It SHALL require an ordered bootstrap of entries each carrying a kind and both a POSIX and a Windows command, in which an install entry, when present, precedes its assert entries, and in which a heartbeat-writing entry, when present, is typed `record` rather than `assert`. It SHALL require the manifest template to be the manifest object with every value null except a literal schema version, an intentionally-empty flag set to false, and empty arrays for the six array fields written paths, commits, commands run, acceptance results, red evidence and external side effects; and it SHALL require the heartbeat path and the decisions path to be present. It SHALL refuse a handoff carrying a base-sha front-matter field outright. It SHALL select its layers from the handoff kind: all five layers for a lane handoff and for an epilogue handoff, and layers 2 through 5 for a remediation handoff, whose layer 1 is skipped.

#### Rationale

05 sections 6.1 and 6.2. The handoff is the executing agent's complete input, read by a cold agent with no conversation, so every mechanical property of it must be checkable rather than reviewed. The counts are load-bearing and were previously inconsistent across the document: the validator is authored from the normative sentence, so a heading count that disagreed with the enumerated list produced a validator that rejects the design's own example. The base-sha refusal enforces the ordering rule that a handoff committed into a dispatch-base commit cannot name that commit's sha.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A handoff whose per-task field count differs from thirteen times the number of declared task ids invalidates the round, and the reported expected value is computed as thirteen times that number rather than fixed.
- [x] AC-2: Each of the thirteen task fields is individually detected: thirteen fixtures each omit exactly one field and each is refused.
- [x] AC-3: A handoff whose declared task ids are a strict subset of the assignment for its kind is refused, as is one that is a strict superset; the same two cases are refused for the declared write set against the lane's computed write set.
- [x] AC-4: A handoff missing any one of the ten body headings is refused, a handoff carrying all ten in a different order is refused, and a handoff carrying all ten in the declared order is accepted.
- [x] AC-5: A bootstrap whose install entry follows an assert entry is refused; a bootstrap consisting of the assert entries alone, with no install and no record entry, is accepted; and a heartbeat-writing entry typed assert rather than record is refused.
- [x] AC-6: A bootstrap entry carrying only one of its POSIX and Windows commands is refused.
- [x] AC-7: A manifest template in which any of the six array fields is null rather than an empty array, or whose schema version is null, or whose intentionally-empty flag is not false, is refused; the object carrying those three exceptions with every other value null is accepted.
- [x] AC-8: A handoff omitting the heartbeat path or the decisions path is refused.
- [x] AC-9: A handoff carrying a base-sha front-matter field is refused regardless of that field's value.
- [x] AC-10: A remediation handoff skips layer 1 and its lock records a task field count of zero, while a lane handoff and an epilogue handoff carrying the same layer-1 defect are both refused.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/orchestrator/handoff.fr-node-154.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6, AC-7, AC-8, AC-9, AC-10 | 59/59 pass, over a worked-example fixture the file first asserts is accepted with zero violations. AC-1 asserts a two-task handoff missing one field reports expected 26 actual 25, and a three-task handoff reports expected 39 actual 38, so expected is computed as thirteen times the declared task count. AC-2 iterates HANDOFF_TASK_FIELDS - asserted to have length 13 - deleting each in turn and asserting handoff-task-field-count. AC-3 asserts a strict subset and a strict superset of the task assignment, and of the lane's computed write set, each yield handoff-set-inequality. AC-4 asserts HANDOFF_BODY_HEADINGS has ten members equal to the fixture's, iterates removing each one for handoff-schema-invalid, refuses a transposed order, and accepts the declared order. AC-5 asserts an install after an assert is refused, asserts alone are accepted, a heartbeat-writing entry typed assert is refused and the same entry typed record is accepted. AC-6 asserts an entry carrying only posix and one carrying only windows are each refused. AC-7 asserts MANIFEST_TEMPLATE_ARRAY_FIELDS has six members, iterates nulling each for a refusal, refuses a null schema version and a non-false intentionally-empty flag, and accepts the object carrying those three exceptions with every other value null. AC-8 asserts omitting the heartbeat path and omitting the decisions path are each refused. AC-9 asserts a base_sha field is refused for a real sha, for null and for the empty string. AC-10 asserts a remediation handoff skips layer 1 with taskFieldCount 0 and ok true over a catalog carrying the defect, while a lane handoff and an epilogue handoff with the same defect are both refused. |

### FR-NODE-155 — Handoff acceptance is non-vacuous under a capped, attributed and resolvable untested-AC rule

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [kiwi-orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

Handoff validation SHALL enforce non-vacuous acceptance. The acceptance list SHALL be non-empty. The number of acceptance rows carrying a null test id SHALL NOT exceed the greater of the explicitly allowed untested-row count and the ceiling of one quarter of the acceptance-row count, where the allowance is an absolute row count rather than a ratio and is journalled when used. On a lane handoff or a remediation handoff at most one null row per requirement id SHALL be permitted, in addition to the count cap. Every null row SHALL carry an untested reason of at least twenty characters and an untested owner naming a later lane, a later wave, or deferral to the user. Every non-null test id SHALL resolve to a test file that exists at the dispatch base or is a member of the declared write set, and no two acceptance rows SHALL share a test id. A violation SHALL be reported as `handoff-untested-ac-over-cap`.

#### Rationale

05 section 6.5. This is the one critical defect that a serial scope does not remove: nothing bounded the number of null test ids, and the party choosing null was the handoff author, so a unit could ship with every acceptance criterion untested plus one trivial test, pass every mechanical gate, and promote a requirement with zero executable evidence - against the charter's rule that no requirement is marked verified without evidence. The rounding convention is ceiling because flooring forbids any untested criterion on a handoff with fewer than four rows, which is most handoffs, and declaring the legitimate case is the whole purpose of the attribution fields.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A handoff whose acceptance list is empty is refused.
- [x] AC-2: A handoff with three acceptance rows and one null test id is accepted, because the cap is the ceiling of three divided by four, which is one; the same handoff with two null rows is refused, so the rounding convention is ceiling and not floor.
- [x] AC-3: Raising the allowance to an absolute count N accepts a handoff at exactly N null rows and refuses one at N plus one, and the allowance value used is recorded in the run journal.
- [x] AC-4: A lane handoff carrying two null rows for the same requirement id is refused even when the count cap would permit both; an epilogue handoff with the same shape is accepted under the count cap alone.
- [x] AC-5: A null row missing its untested reason, carrying an untested reason shorter than twenty characters, or missing its untested owner is refused.
- [x] AC-6: A non-null test id naming a file that neither exists at the dispatch base nor appears in the declared write set is refused; the same test id naming a write-set member is accepted.
- [x] AC-7: Two acceptance rows sharing one test id are refused.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/orchestrator/handoff.fr-node-155.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6, AC-7 | 17/17 pass. AC-1 asserts an empty acceptance list yields handoff-untested-ac-over-cap with ok false. AC-2 asserts three rows with one null validate clean reporting untestedCap 1, and the same three rows with two nulls are refused, so the convention is ceiling rather than floor. AC-3 asserts allowUntestedAc 2 accepts exactly two null rows out of four reporting untestedAllowance 2, refuses three, and that the default handoff reports allowance 0 so the value in force is always recorded. AC-4 asserts a lane handoff with two null rows for one requirement is refused even though untestedCap is 2 and untestedRowCount is 2, while an epilogue handoff of the same shape validates clean. AC-5 asserts a null row missing its reason, one whose reason is 10 characters, and one missing its owner are each refused, and that a reason of exactly 20 characters is accepted. AC-6 asserts a test id naming a file neither at the dispatch base nor in the write set yields handoff-unresolvable-reference while a write-set member is accepted. AC-7 asserts two rows sharing one test id are refused and two distinct ids in one file are accepted. |
| VE-2 | test | test/cli/orchestrate-deferred-halves.test.ts | AC-3 | 7/7 pass. Journal half of AC-3: orchestrate handoff validate over a base declaring allowUntestedAc 2 and naming --run-id writes exactly one verify-handoff line to kiwi/waves.jsonl carrying untested_allowance 2; the same call with no run named writes no line, so the allowance is recorded only where a run exists to record it against. |

### FR-NODE-156 — Handoff English-only scan over a named denominator with three excluded constructs

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [kiwi-orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

Handoff validation SHALL scan the handoff body, the escalation front-matter field and every acceptance row's untested reason for Hangul, CJK ideographs, Kana and Cyrillic, and SHALL raise `handoff-not-english` on any hit. Fenced code blocks, inline code spans and blockquote content SHALL be excluded from the scan. Every other front-matter field - identifiers, paths, enumerated values, commands, the bootstrap entries and the forbidden list - SHALL be outside the scan's denominator.

#### Rationale

05 section 6.2 layer 3. The charter makes English a hard requirement for handoffs, and a script regex over a named denominator is the only form of that requirement that survives; the two front-matter fields are inside the denominator because they are authored natural-language prose the executing agent reads exactly as the body is, while the remaining fields are identifiers and quoted closed lists rather than free authoring. The design states the limitation explicitly: this is a script check and not a language check.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Hangul, a CJK ideograph, a Kana character and a Cyrillic character, each placed in the handoff body in its own fixture, raise `handoff-not-english`.
- [x] AC-2: The same characters placed inside a fenced code block, inside an inline code span and inside blockquote content are accepted, asserted once for each of the three exclusions.
- [x] AC-3: Hangul in the escalation field and Hangul in an acceptance row's untested reason each raise `handoff-not-english`.
- [x] AC-4: Hangul in any other front-matter field - an identifier, a path, an enumerated value, a command, a bootstrap entry or a forbidden-list entry - does not raise the violation, because those fields are outside the denominator.
- [x] AC-5: A handoff body written in fluent Spanish is accepted, pinning the declared limitation that the scan is a script check rather than a language check.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/orchestrator/handoff.fr-node-156.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5 | 27/27 pass. AC-1 places Hangul, a CJK ideograph, Kana and Cyrillic in the body one fixture each and asserts handoff-not-english, after asserting the unvaried body is clean so no fixture is vacuous. AC-2 asserts the same four samples inside a fenced code block, inside an inline code span and inside blockquote content are each accepted - twelve assertions, one per script per exclusion. AC-3 asserts Hangul in the escalation field and Hangul in an acceptance row's untested_reason each raise the violation. AC-4 asserts Hangul in a lane identifier, in a read-set path, in an enumerated commit_policy value, in a verification command, in a bootstrap entry, in a forbidden-list entry and in the heartbeat path each raise nothing, because those fields are outside the denominator. AC-5 asserts a body whose Objective, Context and Constraints are fluent Spanish is accepted, pinning that the scan is a script check rather than a language check. |

### FR-NODE-157 — Handoff resolvability over a named field set against the dispatch base

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [kiwi-orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

Handoff validation SHALL resolve, against the dispatch base - the repository head at handoff authoring plus the set of paths the dispatch-base commit is about to stage - exactly the following scoped field set: the plan path, the sidecar path, every declared write-set entry, every declared read-set entry, every non-null acceptance test-id file, and every path named in the body's Interfaces and Tasks sections. Within that scope every referenced path SHALL exist at the dispatch base or be a declared write-set member; every task id shaped as a sidecar task identifier SHALL exist in the named sidecar; every requirement id SHALL resolve through requirement lookup; every acceptance-row criterion id SHALL exist on its named requirement; and the handoff-level verification command SHALL name a real script whose arguments cover every per-task verification command's test files and every non-null acceptance test-id file. The manifest path, the heartbeat path, the deferred-mutations path, the decisions path, the regression-baseline reference and prose in the Context section SHALL be outside the scope and SHALL NOT be resolved. An unresolvable reference SHALL be reported as `handoff-unresolvable-reference`.

#### Rationale

05 section 6.2 layer 4. The earlier form said every referenced path with no scope and required existence at a sha that does not yet exist when the layer runs, which was unrunnable on both axes: five mandatory front-matter fields are workspace-local or git-ignored and exist at no tree, and the layer runs one step before the commit whose sha would be named. A handoff is consumed by a cold agent with no conversation, so an unresolvable reference is a defect rather than a nit; the out-of-scope set is named explicitly so that no implementer invents the boundary.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A plan path, a sidecar path, a read-set entry, and a path named in the Interfaces or Tasks section that neither exists at the dispatch base nor is a declared write-set member are each refused with `handoff-unresolvable-reference`, one fixture per field.
- [x] AC-2: A path that does not exist at the repository head but is among the paths the dispatch-base commit stages resolves, so the validator evaluates against the dispatch base rather than against the head alone and never against a commit sha that does not yet exist.
- [x] AC-3: A sidecar-shaped task id named in the body but absent from the named sidecar is refused; the same id present in the sidecar resolves.
- [x] AC-4: A requirement id that does not resolve is refused, and an acceptance-row criterion id that does not exist on its named requirement is refused.
- [x] AC-5: A handoff-level verification command that omits a per-task verification command's test file, or omits a non-null acceptance test-id file, is refused; a command naming both sets is accepted.
- [x] AC-6: A fixture in which the manifest path, the heartbeat path, the deferred-mutations path, the decisions path and the regression-baseline reference all name non-existent locations is accepted, and a path named only in Context prose is likewise not resolved.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/orchestrator/handoff.fr-node-157.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6 | 16/16 pass, over a fixture the file separately asserts resolves clean with its plan and sidecar paths present at the base. AC-1 asserts an absent plan path, an absent sidecar path, an absent read-set entry, an absent path named in Interfaces and an absent path named in Tasks each yield handoff-unresolvable-reference, one fixture per source. AC-2 asserts a path absent from headPaths is refused and the same path resolves once it appears in stagedPaths, so the evaluation is against the dispatch base rather than the head. AC-3 asserts a sidecar-shaped id T-PH009-99 named in the body but absent from sidecarTaskIds is refused and resolves once the sidecar carries it. AC-4 asserts req id FR-FLOW-999 and acceptance criterion AC-9 on FR-FLOW-071 each fail to resolve. AC-5 asserts a handoff-level command omitting a per-task verification command's test file is refused, one omitting a non-null acceptance test-id file is refused, and one naming both sets is accepted. AC-6 asserts a fixture whose manifest path, heartbeat path, deferred-mutations path, decisions path and regression-baseline reference all name non-existent locations validates clean, and that a path named only in Context prose is not resolved. |

### FR-NODE-158 — Atomic-write scratch files are ignored by filename pattern rather than by one directory

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The tracked .gitignore SHALL ignore the atomic-write scratch files that SRS patch application and merge-journal writes leave behind, matched by filename pattern rather than by a single directory path, so that a scratch file written beside a step SRS document under docs/spec/steps/, beside any other patched file, or at the repository root is never reported by git status and never staged by git add -A; and the widened pattern SHALL NOT match any tracked repository path.

#### Rationale

apply-patch.ts:58 writes .speckiwi-<uuid>.tmp beside the file it patches, and merge-journal.ts:73 and :143 write two further scratch shapes. The entry added on 2026-08-01 covers only docs/spec/.speckiwi-*.tmp, so a scratch file left beside a step SRS document under docs/spec/steps/<task>/ is still untracked and still staged by git add -A. Measured with git check-ignore: docs/spec/.speckiwi-abc.tmp is ignored while .speckiwi-abc.tmp, src/.speckiwi-abc.tmp and docs/spec/steps/.speckiwi-abc.tmp are not. Two such files were committed by git add -A during the 2026-08-01 session, which is how the leak was found. FR-NODE-106 sets the precedent that this repository states its .gitignore registrations as requirements.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: git check-ignore matches a .speckiwi-<token>.tmp scratch file in every directory a patch can write one: the repository root, docs/spec/, and a nested step directory under docs/spec/steps/.
- [x] AC-2: The merge-journal scratch shape .speckiwi-merge-journal.json.<token>.tmp at the repository root is ignored.
- [x] AC-3: With scratch files of both shapes present on disk, git status --porcelain reports none of them and git add -A stages none of them.
- [x] AC-4: The widened pattern matches no tracked path: git ls-files returns nothing that the pattern would ignore, so widening the rule cannot hide a file the repository tracks.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/orchestrator/scratch-file-ignore.fr-node-158.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4 | 8/8 pass (npx vitest run --no-file-parallelism, 2026-08-02). AC-1 asserts git check-ignore matches a .speckiwi-<token>.tmp at the repository root, under docs/spec/, under a nested docs/spec/steps/<task>/ and under src/core/orchestrator/. AC-2 asserts the merge-journal shape at the root. AC-3 writes four real scratch files, asserts git status --porcelain reports none and git add -A --dry-run stages none, and guards its own vacuity by asserting the status output is non-empty; it creates and removes docs/spec/steps/ because this repository has no steps yet, and cleans up in a finally block. AC-4 runs one git check-ignore --stdin over git ls-files and asserts no tracked path is shadowed, with a companion case asserting the tracked list exceeds 100 entries so the check is not vacuous; the obvious per-file loop was tried first and failed by timeout after spawning about 1,500 git processes, which is not evidence either way. Mutation probe: narrowing the two patterns back to the single docs/spec/.speckiwi-*.tmp entry turns 5 of the 8 cases red including AC-3; bytes restored and re-run green. A first attempt at that probe replaced zero occurrences and left the file unchanged, so the run was discarded rather than read as a pass. |

### FR-NODE-159 — A program-counter journal line never alters the run's computed wave status

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

Appending a program-counter line — an intent or result line recording that a verb ran — SHALL leave the run's computed wave status exactly as it was: the wave's status, the run's first incomplete wave, and whether a final verification is still owed SHALL all be unchanged by the append. This SHALL hold both for a wave still in progress, which such a line SHALL NOT remove from the computation, and for a wave already complete, which such a line SHALL NOT reopen.

#### Rationale

Measured against dist/ on 2026-08-02, both failure directions are live. Today the CLI writes a verb line with no status: appending one for wave-2 to a journal whose waveStatuses were [[1,complete],[2,in_progress]] yields [[1,complete]] — wave-2 disappears, firstIncompleteWave goes 2 to null, and needsFinalVerify flips false to true, so a resumed run advances to final verification with wave-2 never done. The obvious repair, stamping status in_progress on the verb line, fails the other way: against a journal whose wave-1 was complete it yields [[1,in_progress]] with firstIncompleteWave 1, reopening a finished wave. A verb line carrying wave 'all' leaves the computation untouched, which is the behaviour this requirement fixes as the contract. The cause is that latestPerWave (waves-journal.ts:76) is set by every line carrying a wave-{n} value, and a program-counter line is not a wave-status assertion. The requirement states the invariant and not the mechanism, because the repair may belong on the reader, on the writer, or on both, and that choice is under review.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Appending a line that asserts no wave status for a wave that is in progress leaves that wave in the computed wave-status map with its status unchanged, and leaves the run's first incomplete wave unchanged.
- [x] AC-2: Appending a line that asserts no wave status for a wave that is already complete leaves that wave complete and does not make it the run's first incomplete wave. A line that does assert a status is honoured, because a compliant wave completion may itself be a verb result line and excluding those would skip the completion.
- [x] AC-3: Appending a line that asserts no wave status does not change whether the run still owes a final verification.
- [x] AC-4: A line that does assert a wave status still changes the computation, so the rule narrows the statusless case only and does not silence the journal.
- [x] AC-5: The three assertions above are made against a journal whose waves are not all complete, so a computation that returned an empty map for every input would fail them rather than pass vacuously.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/orchestrator/wave-status-invariant.fr-node-159.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5 | 6/6 pass (npx vitest run --no-file-parallelism, 2026-08-02). The fixture is a two-wave journal, wave-1 verified and complete and wave-2 in progress, so AC-5's non-vacuity case asserts the baseline is [[1,complete],[2,in_progress]] with firstIncompleteWave 2 and needsFinalVerify false before any program-counter line is added. AC-1 appends the line orchestrate journal append writes today for wave-2 and asserts the whole map and the first incomplete wave are unchanged. AC-2 does the same on the completed wave-1, and a second case repeats it with the line carrying status in_progress, which is the repair that fails the other way. AC-3 sweeps four program-counter lines and asserts needsFinalVerify is unchanged by each. AC-4 appends a real wave-progress line and asserts the computation does move, so the rule narrows the program-counter case rather than silencing the journal. Mutation probe: removing the isProgramCounter conjunct from waves-journal.ts turns exactly the four behavioural cases red and leaves AC-4 and AC-5 green; bytes restored and re-run green. Regression: test/core/orchestrator, test/cli and test/mcp together, 144 files / 1441 pass / 0 fail. |
| VE-2 | test | test/core/orchestrator/wave-status-invariant.fr-node-159.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5 | 7/7 pass (npx vitest run --no-file-parallelism, 2026-08-02), re-measured after the discriminator was corrected. The first repair keyed on the presence of an event field and was wrong: FR-NODE-152 requires a verdict-bearing line to carry an external proof and the write discipline writes a result line per verb, so a compliant wave completion can itself be a verb result line - measured, that shape was skipped and the wave fell back from complete to in_progress. The discriminator is now a valid status, which waves-event.md 2.3 names as the only positive completion signal. AC-5 pins the baseline at [[1,complete],[2,in_progress]] with firstIncompleteWave 2. AC-1 appends the statusless line orchestrate journal append writes today and asserts the map and the first incomplete wave are unchanged. AC-2 has two cases in opposite directions: a statusless line on the completed wave leaves it complete, and a line that DOES assert in_progress is honoured, taken at its word. AC-4 appends a wave completion written as a verb result with status complete and a git-trailer proof and asserts the wave completes, which is the case the first discriminator broke. AC-3 sweeps the statusless lines. Mutation probe: removing the assertsStatus conjunct turns AC-1, AC-2 and AC-3 red and leaves AC-4 and AC-5 green; bytes restored, rebuilt, re-run 7/7. Regression: test/core/orchestrator plus test/cli plus test/mcp, 1458 pass 0 fail. |

That consequence needs a caller and there is none: computeRunProgress has zero call sites anywhere under src/, measured with grep -a including inside its own module, and orchestrate run status reports only the run lock path and its holder. The defect the criteria assert is real and the fix is correct — the computation returned the wrong map, and the mutation probe turns exactly the four behavioural cases red — but it is a defect in a function no shipped code path reaches today, not a live corruption of a run. Recorded because the overstatement is exactly the pattern this session has been cataloguing, made an hour after writing the lesson down. A committee member produced the counterexample: a compliant wave completion may itself be a verb result line, because FR-NODE-152 requires a verdict-bearing line to carry an external proof and the write discipline writes a result line per verb. Measured against dist/ - that shape was skipped and the wave fell back from complete to in_progress, so the repair introduced a second failure mode while closing the first. The discriminator is now a valid status. AC-2 and AC-3 asserted that a status-bearing line must not move the wave; that is exactly the case the counterexample shows is compliant, so both were narrowed to the statusless line and AC-2 gained the opposite assertion for the status-bearing one. The row was demoted before the criteria were touched and re-promoted on re-measured evidence. |

### FR-NODE-160 — A resumed session recognises a unit that already landed on the integration branch

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | critical |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

The resume state computation SHALL decide whether a unit has landed from the merge witness the run actually leaves — the Orch-Run, Orch-Wave, Orch-Stage and Orch-Lane trailers on commits reachable from the integration branch — and the injected git facts SHALL carry those trailered commits so the witness can reach the computation. A resumed session SHALL NOT direct re-execution of a unit whose commits carry that witness, and SHALL NOT report such a run as reconciled and unblocked while doing so.

#### Rationale

Measured end-to-end. computeResumeState decides landing from branch.ancestorOfIntegration (resume.ts:199), a lane-branch ancestry proof. Phase 1 creates no lane branch — the unit commits onto the integration branch, which the module's own comment at :207 states — so that value is structurally always false and a landed unit falls through to not-dispatched with nextVerb execute-unit, reconciliation consistent and blocking null. The tool does not merely fail to notice: it affirmatively reports the state is fine while directing a duplicate implementation. The predicate that gets it right already exists and is tested — hasMergeWitness (lane-state.ts:125) returns true over the same commits — but GitFacts declares only branches, worktrees, heartbeats, integrationHead and hostStatusPaths, so no field can carry a commit trailer and the landed and unlanded cases are indistinguishable by construction. The only barrier today is prose in the skill body telling the agent to check the trailers by hand, which is the split-responsibility failure with the wrong copy shipped in the tool.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Given a journal whose unit has an execute-unit intent and result, and injected git facts carrying a commit with the four Orch trailers for that run, wave, stage and lane, the resumed state does not classify the lane as not-dispatched and does not return execute-unit as the next verb.
- [x] AC-2: The injected git-facts bundle declares a field for the trailered commits on the integration branch, so the witness is supplied by the caller rather than invented by the tool.
- [x] AC-3: The landing decision is made by the shared merge-witness predicate rather than by a second inline copy, so the two cannot disagree.
- [x] AC-4: The same journal with no trailered commit still classifies as not-dispatched with execute-unit as the next verb, so the rule recognises a landing rather than suppressing the class.
- [x] AC-5: A run whose unit has landed is not reported as reconciled-and-unblocked while a re-execution is being directed: the two cannot both hold.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/orchestrator/resume-merge-witness.fr-node-160.test.ts | AC-1, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5 | 4/4 pass (npx vitest run --no-file-parallelism, 2026-08-02). The fixture is a journal whose unit has an execute-unit intent and result, plus the repository's own resume fixtures for the card and drift inputs. AC-3 asserts hasMergeWitness returns true over the trailered commit, so the predicate that was unreachable is shown to have the right answer. AC-4 runs the same journal with an empty commit list and asserts the lane is still not-dispatched with execute-unit next, so the rule recognises a landing rather than suppressing the class. AC-1 supplies the trailered commit and asserts the lane is not not-dispatched and the next verb is not execute-unit. AC-5 asserts the conjunction directs-re-execution AND blocking-is-null is false, which is the shape of the original defect: the tool affirmed the state was unblocked while telling the agent to re-run landed work. Mutation probe: restoring the branch-ancestry-only computation turns AC-1 and AC-5 red and leaves AC-3 and AC-4 green; bytes restored, rebuilt, re-run 4/4. Regression: test/core/orchestrator plus test/cli, 1327 pass 0 fail. Found by an independent committee member who reproduced the classification end-to-end against dist/ before I re-derived it here. |
| VE-2 | test | test/cli/orchestrate-resume-witness.fr-node-160.test.ts | AC-2 | 2/2 pass (npx vitest run --no-file-parallelism, 2026-08-02). Drives main() rather than the kernel, because a field the kernel accepts proves nothing about a run — this session's record is full of correct predicates nothing reached. Baseline: a --facts bundle with an empty integrationCommits list resumes exit 0 with nextAction.verb execute-unit and reconciliation consistent. With the trailered commit in the same bundle the run halts exit 2 on gate ledger-reconciliation-divergent and dispatches nothing, because the witness reaches the classifier, merged becomes true, and with no integrate-lane result the lane reads journal-behind-git. The refusal is the fix: the run stops for reconciliation instead of being told to redo work that already committed. Mutation probe: replacing the hasMergeWitness call with false turns the second case red and leaves the baseline green; bytes restored, rebuilt, re-run 2/2. The CLI needed no change — orchestrate.ts:695 passes the parsed gitFacts object through whole and selects no fields, so the new bundle member reaches the kernel unaltered. |

### FR-NODE-161 — A program-counter line is not the run's final verification record

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

The run-completion computation SHALL take its final-verification record only from a line that asserts a run status, and SHALL NOT take it from a program-counter line — an intent or result line recording that a verb ran — even when that line carries the final-verify phase and a passing verdict. A run whose only final-verify-phased line is a program-counter line SHALL still be reported as owing a final verification.

#### Rationale

FR-NODE-159 closed one reader and left its sibling open. Measured against dist/: latestFinalVerify (waves-validate.ts:494-497) scans view.lines and filters on phase alone — no event filter, no wave filter — so a loop-F round record carrying phase final-verify and verdict pass becomes the run's final-verification record. On a journal whose waves are complete and whose run-scope final verification was never written, appending that one program-counter line flips needsFinalVerify from true to false and runComplete from false to true, indistinguishable from the real run-scope line. The verb that would write it, orchestrate round record, hard-codes verify-design today, so the line is reachable through orchestrate journal append rather than through the round verb. The discriminator is the one FR-NODE-159 already established and already argued for under the contract's SemVer rule: kiwi-wave-master writes no event field, so no line either producer has recorded reads differently. Found by an independent committee member attacking a proposed answer rather than the code.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A run whose waves are all complete and whose only final-verify-phased line is a program-counter line is reported as still owing a final verification, and not as complete.
- [x] AC-2: The same run with a genuine run-scope final-verification line — one asserting a status and carrying no program-counter fields — is reported as not owing one, so the rule distinguishes the two rather than refusing both.
- [x] AC-3: A run with no final-verify-phased line at all is still reported as owing a final verification, so the rule adds a distinction rather than changing the empty case.
- [x] AC-4: The distinction is drawn by the same discriminator FR-NODE-159 uses, so the two readers cannot disagree about what a program-counter line is.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/orchestrator/final-verify-witness.fr-node-161.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4 | 4/4 pass (npx vitest run --no-file-parallelism, 2026-08-02). AC-3 asserts the empty case is unchanged: waves complete with no final-verify line at all still owes one. AC-1 appends a loop-F round record — wave all, verb final-verify, event result, phase final-verify, verdict pass — and asserts the run still owes a final verification and is not complete. AC-2 appends the genuine run-scope line instead, one asserting a status and carrying no program-counter fields, and asserts the run is discharged, so the rule distinguishes rather than refusing both. AC-4 asserts the same program-counter line leaves the wave-status map untouched, so FR-NODE-159's reader and this one agree on what such a line is. Mutation probe: removing the isProgramCounter conjunct turns AC-1 and AC-4 red and leaves AC-2 and AC-3 green; bytes restored, rebuilt, re-run 4/4. Regression: test/core/orchestrator plus test/cli, 1333 pass 0 fail. Found by an independent committee member who attacked a proposed answer to a different question and measured that FR-NODE-159 had closed only one of the two readers. |

### FR-NODE-162 — A resumed session validates the card it reads, not only the card it wrote

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | critical |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

A resumed session SHALL validate the resume card it reads against the run journal as that journal stands now, and SHALL refuse with the resume-card-missing-or-invalid gate when any declared card violation holds. Validation performed only when the card was written SHALL NOT be treated as sufficient, because three of the declared violations compare the card against journal content that accrues after the card is written.

#### Rationale

Measured: validateCard has zero callers under src/ outside its own module — its only call is inside writeCard. orchestrate resume reads the card through readCard, which performs JSON.parse and an object-shape check and returns ok for any object, so nine of the ten declared violations are accepted on the resume path. The tenth, invariant-digest-mismatch, is refused by computeDrift digest 1 rather than by the card check, so the card validator contributes no refusal at all. Among the nine accepted are unknown-verb, which is the enforcement the skill body promises for a verb outside the closed enum, and resume-card-too-large, which the shipped gate table names by name: a card over the 8192-byte cap resumes clean. Write-time validation cannot cover isolation-profile-changed, lane-lock-not-rolled-up or lane-entry-not-rolled-up, because each compares the card against the journal and the journal grows after the card is written — a card clean when written trips isolation-profile-changed once a later probe-isolation result records a different profile. Found by an independent committee member who drove the CLI over each violation in turn.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A resume over a card carrying a declared violation refuses with the resume-card-missing-or-invalid gate and reports that violation, rather than proceeding.
- [x] AC-2: A card exceeding the declared byte cap is refused on the resume path, which the gate table names but nothing enforced.
- [x] AC-3: A card whose next_action names a verb outside the closed enum is refused on the resume path.
- [x] AC-4: A card that was clean when written but which the journal has since contradicted is refused, so validation is against the journal as it stands and not as it stood.
- [x] AC-5: A clean card still resumes, so the rule adds a refusal rather than closing the verb.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/cli/orchestrate-resume-card-validation.fr-node-162.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5 | 4/4 pass (npx vitest run --no-file-parallelism, 2026-08-02). Drives main(), so every assertion is about what a resumed session does rather than about a predicate. AC-5 asserts a clean card still resumes exit 0, without which every refusal below would be uninformative. AC-3 supplies a card whose next_action names totally-made-up and asserts exit 2, gate resume-card-missing-or-invalid, violation unknown-verb. AC-2 pads the card past the 8192-byte cap, asserts the fixture really exceeds it, and asserts the refusal carries resume-card-too-large - the violation the shipped gate table names and nothing enforced. AC-1 and AC-4 share one case: a card that was clean when written, resumed against a journal that has since recorded a probe-isolation result with a different profile, is refused with isolation-profile-changed, which is the half write-time validation cannot cover because the journal grows after the card is written. Mutation probe: replacing the refusal with a void of the validation result turns exactly those three red and leaves the clean-card baseline green; bytes restored, rebuilt, re-run 4/4. Regression: test/cli plus test/core/orchestrator plus test/mcp, 148 files / 1457 pass / 0 fail. Found by an independent committee member who drove the CLI over each of the ten declared violations in turn and measured nine accepted. |

### FR-NODE-163 — The phase-1 verb enum is the set the skill declares, and a deferred verb halts a resume

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

The resume card's verb check SHALL be made against the phase-1 verb set the shipped skill declares, not against the union of phase-1 and deferred verbs, so that a card naming a verb the skill states is outside the phase-1 enum is refused on resume. The phase-1 verb set SHALL be asserted equal to the set of verb sections the shipped skill body carries, in every variant, so the constant cannot drift from the document that defines it.

#### Rationale

The shipped skill states that the six lane verbs plus probe-isolation, run-serial-epilogue and replay-deferred-mutations are not in the phase-1 enum and that a verb outside the enum halts a resume immediately. Measured: the skill carries exactly 38 verb sections, VERBS carries 47, and isVerb accepts all nine deferred verbs, so the promised halt fires for none of them. It does fire for a verb outside all 47 — review-partiton and totally-made-up are both refused now that FR-NODE-162 wired the card check onto the resume path — so the check works and its vocabulary is wrong. The consequence is that a phase-1 run can be handed phase-2 machinery and resume clean. Tying the constant to the section set rather than maintaining it by hand is what stops the two drifting: FR-FLOW-074 AC-2 already requires one section per enum member, so the document is the definition. Found by an independent committee member who drove the CLI over each verb class.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A resume over a card naming a deferred verb is refused with the resume-card-missing-or-invalid gate and an unknown-verb violation.
- [x] AC-2: A resume over a card naming a phase-1 verb is not refused on that ground, so the rule narrows the vocabulary rather than closing the verb.
- [x] AC-3: A resume over a card naming a verb outside every enum is still refused, so the existing behaviour is preserved.
- [x] AC-4: The phase-1 verb set is asserted equal to the set of verb sections the skill body declares, in all three shipped variants, so a verb added to one and not the other fails.
- [x] AC-5: The deferred verbs keep their recovery-class mapping, so narrowing the resume vocabulary does not delete the phase-2 vocabulary.

#### Verification Evidence

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

AC-4 parses every section-V heading out of all three shipped variants and asserts PHASE_1_VERBS equals that set, with a non-empty guard so an empty constant could not pass; this is what ties the constant to the document FR-FLOW-074 AC-2 already governs, so the two cannot drift. AC-5 asserts each of the nine deferred verbs is outside the phase-1 set, still a known verb, and still carries a recovery class, so narrowing the resume vocabulary does not delete the phase-2 vocabulary. AC-3 asserts a verb outside every enum is outside phase 1 too. A fourth case asserts the two sets partition the union with no overlap, so no verb is lost or double-counted. Measured before the change: the skill declares 38 verb sections, VERBS carried 47, and isVerb accepted all nine deferred verbs. |
| VE-2 | test | test/cli/orchestrate-resume-card-validation.fr-node-162.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2 | 5/5 pass. Drives main(). AC-1: a card whose next_action names dispatch-lane resumes exit 2 with gate resume-card-missing-or-invalid and violation unknown-verb, which is the halt the shipped body promises and which fired for none of the nine before this change. AC-2: the clean card carrying execute-unit still resumes exit 0, so the vocabulary narrowed rather than the verb closing. Mutation probe: putting isVerb back in place of isPhase1Verb turns exactly the deferred-verb case red and leaves the other four green; bytes restored, rebuilt, re-run 5/5. Regression: test/core/orchestrator, test/cli, test/mcp and test/skills together, 186 files / 3418 pass / 0 fail. |

### FR-NODE-164 — Orchestrator test sources are type-checked at the repository's own strictness

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The repository SHALL type-check its orchestrator test sources under the root compiler options, through a dedicated project file and a named script, so that a fixture whose shape contradicts the declaration it is built from fails a check rather than passing silently.

#### Rationale

FR-NODE-108 AC-2 was unticked on 2026-08-01 because it asserts a property of the type graph and types are erased at runtime; its change note records that the criterion is not to be amended and that "the honest repair is a typechecked test project, which is its own requirement". Nothing type-checks test/**: tsconfig.json includes src only, vitest.config.ts declares no typecheck block, and the eslint config in package.json sets no parserOptions.project, so npm run lint is not type-aware over the files it lints. Measured 2026-08-02, the covered subtree carries 47 diagnostics across 20 files, and two of them are live instances of exactly the class a runtime test cannot catch: test/core/orchestrator/anchors.fr-node-112.test.ts writes a relation property into a trace-link literal declared as {type, reference}, and test/core/orchestrator/resume-merge-witness.fr-node-160.test.ts builds an OrchTrailerCommit with a sha field the type does not declare while omitting the commit field it requires. Both suites are green.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A project file `tsconfig.test.json` extends the root `tsconfig.json`, sets `noEmit`, and includes `src/**/*.ts`, `test/core/orchestrator/**/*.ts` and `test/cli/orchestrate*.ts`. It relaxes no strictness option the root config sets: `strict`, `noUncheckedIndexedAccess` and `exactOptionalPropertyTypes` all remain in force under it.
- [x] AC-2: `package.json` declares a `typecheck:test` script that runs exactly that project file, and the script reports zero diagnostics over the repository.
- [x] AC-3: The check reports both type-graph classes a runtime test cannot carry: an object literal that names a property its declared type does not have, and an index access whose `undefined` case is unhandled.
- [x] AC-4: The check is non-vacuous under a measured mutation: a covered test source is edited to introduce one excess property, the mutation is asserted to have applied, the check is then observed to fail naming that file, and the source is restored and the check observed to pass.
- [x] AC-5: No covered source suppresses a diagnostic with `@ts-ignore`, and the project file relaxes no strictness option. `@ts-expect-error` appears only where the compile error is itself the assertion.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/release/typecheck-test-project.fr-node-164.test.ts | AC-3, AC-4 | Narrowed to AC-3 and AC-4 on 2026-08-02: this row's account of AC-1, AC-2 and AC-5 was measured false by an independent verifier and is superseded by VE-2, and its line ranges went stale in the same repair. What survives, re-measured at 8 passed / 0 failed on this file alone: AC-3 and AC-4 write a probe into the covered glob carrying both type-graph classes (an OrchTrailerCommit literal with an undeclared property, and a bare index access), assert the write applied before running the check by testing existsSync and by reading the marker back out of the file, observe a non-zero exit and at least one diagnostic naming the probe file, assert one of those names the excess property and one mentions undefined, remove the probe in a finally, assert removal, and observe the check pass again - so the failure is attributable to the mutation and not to a standing condition of the repository. Measured over the repository at the same commit: 47 diagnostics across 20 files before the fixes and 0 after. |
| VE-2 | test | test/release/typecheck-test-project.fr-node-164.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-5 | Supersedes VE-1's account of AC-1, AC-2 and AC-5, which an independent verifier measured as uncarried. 8 passed / 0 failed on this file alone after the repair, 2026-08-02, re-confirmed by that verifier. AC-1's first revision derived a key list from the ROOT config and checked key PRESENCE, then compared only keys the PROJECT also declares - and the project declares only noEmit and rootDir, so the comparison loop ran ZERO times; setting exactOptionalPropertyTypes false in the root left the file at 7 passed. The assertion now reads the compiler's RESOLVED options through --showConfig and requires each of the three to be true. Re-measured with the same mutation, application asserted first: 1 failed of 8 naming that assertion, and the restored tree gives 8 passed with tsconfig.json clean. AC-2's first revision asserted the script string by substring and ran an equivalent command rather than the script; it now executes the declared command text with npm's own node_modules/.bin PATH entry AND pins the command to exactly `tsc -p tsconfig.test.json`. The exact pin is what carries the claim: a trailing short-circuit exit is caught by the run, but `--noCheck` appended is NOT - measured, it produces no output and exits 0, so the run alone would pass it. AC-5's first revision enumerated with git ls-files, which returns TRACKED files, while the compiled program is glob-based and pulls in transitive imports; two live divergences were found, orchestrate-journal-outcome.fr-node-165.test.ts and test/support/at.ts. The enumeration now comes from --listFiles and the test asserts test/support/at.ts is among the covered files. AC-5's third sentence is the weakest part of this row and is disclosed rather than dressed up: the covered set carries ZERO @ts-expect-error directives - the repository's only three are string literals inside this test file, which the program does not load - so that clause rests on an empty census. The assertion is the strict form and fails the moment a directive appears in a covered source, which is when a human must judge whether the compile error IS the assertion; a first attempt tested comment length instead and was withdrawn as worse than nothing. The probe is removed in beforeAll as well as afterAll so a rerun of this file self-heals, and its name is in .gitignore so a leak cannot be committed; a leak still fails every typecheck:test between a hard kill and that rerun, which writing the probe outside the repository would close and which was not done. |

- Scope chosen by measurement rather than by preference. Measured 2026-08-02: `test/core/orchestrator/**` alone is 25 diagnostics across 10 files; adding `test/cli/orchestrate*.ts` brings it to 47 across 20, of which 20 are a single repeated idiom (`new PassThrough() as NodeJS.WriteStream`, TS2352). The wider globs were priced and rejected for this requirement: `test/cli/**` plus `test/skills/**` is 301 across 88, and `test/**` is 577 across 153, which is a remediation wave and not this target's work. The orchestrate CLI tests are included because that is where the next fixtures land, and a check that does not cover where the next commits write does not check them.

An independent verifier measured three of five criteria uncarried: AC-1's comparison loop ran zero times, so the file passed 7/7 with exactOptionalPropertyTypes false in the root; AC-2 asserted the script by substring and never ran it; AC-5 enumerated tracked files, not the program. Repaired in bb1c431 and re-verified. The criteria were ticked throughout that window.
