### FR-NODE-165 — A verb that journals its option use reports whether the line landed

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

A verb that records its own option use in the run journal SHALL report the outcome of that write: when the append is refused the verb SHALL refuse rather than return success, and no call site SHALL discard the append's outcome.

#### Rationale

Measured 2026-08-02: `orchestrate schedule plan` at src/cli/commands/orchestrate.ts:1001-1007 and `orchestrate handoff validate` at :1101-1116 both await the journal-append helper without reading its result, while `orchestrate run abort` at :852-859 reads the same helper's result and refuses with run-invariant-drift when the line did not land. Reproduced: `schedule plan --strict-grounding --run-id run-f` against a journal the append would leave invalid returned ok:true, applied:true, violations:[] and wrote zero journal lines. The append helper validates the resulting journal and refuses before the rename, so a refusal is silent at these two sites and the caller is told the option use was recorded when it was not. Two acceptance criteria state that recording unconditionally — IR-CLI-084 AC-6 and FR-NODE-155 AC-3 — and both are therefore false on this path today; amending their wording before this defect is fixed would launder a live defect into settled text, so the fix lands first and the wording is revisited afterwards. Found by a minority committee member whose position the majority had not examined.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `orchestrate schedule plan --strict-grounding --run-id <id>` against a journal whose contents would make the appended line invalid refuses with `run-invariant-drift` carrying the append's diagnostics, instead of returning a plan.
- [x] AC-2: The same command under `--dry-run` does not refuse on a line that was never meant to land: the dry run is reported as such and the journal is untouched.
- [x] AC-3: `orchestrate handoff validate --run-id <id>` against the same journal refuses with `run-invariant-drift` instead of returning counts.
- [x] AC-4: On the success path both verbs report whether a line landed, and the journal read back from disk holds exactly that line.
- [x] AC-5: A refused append leaves the journal byte-identical to what it was before the attempt, at both verbs.
- [x] AC-6: No call site of the journal-append helper discards its outcome: a source census over `src/cli/commands/orchestrate.ts` finds every call bound to a name or tested directly, and the census asserts the number of call sites it found is non-zero and equal to the number of occurrences of the helper's invocation.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/cli/orchestrate-journal-outcome.fr-node-165.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6 | 8 passed / 0 failed when this row was written, 2026-08-02; the file yields 9 at HEAD after VE-2 added its dry-run case. 6 of the 8 were red before the change and the two that passed were success-path controls. Cited by DESCRIBE BLOCK, not by line: this row originally carried line ranges and VE-2's insertion shifted four of them by fifteen, which is the same defect class the FR-NODE-108 repair was opened for. The poison fixture is a journal holding one wave complete line with no preceding passing wave-verify, so complete-without-latest-pass fires at error severity over the resulting journal and the candidate is unlinked before the rename. AC-1's block asserts schedule plan --strict-grounding --run-id over that journal exits 2 with gate run-invariant-drift, that the violations name complete-without-latest-pass, and that no plan is reported, with a control over an accepting journal so the refusal is attributable to the poison and not to the verb. AC-3's block asserts handoff validate over the same journal exits 2 with the same gate and reports no counts. AC-4's block asserts both verbs report journalWritten true on the success path AND that the journal read back from disk contains the line each claims to have written, and that a validate naming no run reports false with an empty journal; the read-back is a distinctive substring rather than full-line equality. ### FR-NODE-166 — The emitted gate vocabulary is closed over GateId at compile time

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md |

#### Requirement

The gate identifier that any `orchestrate` verb reports at exit 2 SHALL be a member of the exported `GateId` union, enforced at compile time rather than only by the four verbs a runtime test happens to drive: `refuse()` SHALL declare its gate parameter as `GateId` with no `| string` widening, and every phase-1 error type whose code reaches `refuse()` SHALL declare that code inside `GATE_IDS`. Closing the direction SHALL NOT be obtained by admitting an identifier that the CLI deliberately collapses to an umbrella gate.

#### Rationale

`FR-NODE-122` asserts the parity in one direction only — every gate id a shipped skill variant declares must be in the union. The emitted direction was checked dynamically and only for the four verbs `orchestrate-exit-codes.fr-node-137.test.ts` covers, and neither path carrying an undeclared identifier is among them. Two live emitters reach exit 2 with an identifier outside `GATE_IDS`: `handoff-pin-untrusted`, thrown by `HandoffPinError` in `pinning.ts` and caught in the `handoff pin` action, and `lane-plan-incomplete`, thrown by `assertLanePlanPartition` in `lane-plan.ts` and caught in the `schedule plan` action. (Named by symbol rather than by line: this paragraph originally carried line numbers in the present tense, and they were stale against the very commit that introduced them — the change it describes moved every one of them.) Both compile because `refuse()` reads `gate: GateId | string`, which makes the union advisory at every call site. Measured repo-wide: neither identifier appears in any bundled variant, in `.agents/`, or in the design document's §13 `critical_gates[]` table, so both were undeclared everywhere at once and no assertion could see it.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `refuse()` in `src/cli/commands/orchestrate.ts` declares its gate parameter as `GateId` alone, with no `| string` widening, so passing an identifier outside the union is a compile error and not a runtime surprise.
- [x] AC-2: `HandoffPinError`'s `gate` and every member of the runtime lane-plan error-code list `LanePlanErrorCode` is derived from are asserted to be members of `GATE_IDS`, so an error type that feeds `refuse()` cannot carry an identifier the union omits.
- [x] AC-3: Driving `orchestrate freeze design` to a handoff-pin failure exits 2 and reports the gate `handoff-pin-untrusted`, which `GATE_IDS` contains. The identifier is pinned and not merely asserted to be some union member, so the criterion cannot be satisfied by an earlier refusal on a different gate.
- [x] AC-4: Driving `orchestrate schedule plan` to a lane-plan partition failure exits 2 and reports the gate `lane-plan-incomplete`, which `GATE_IDS` contains. The identifier is pinned for the same reason as AC-3.
- [x] AC-5: The three `HandoffViolationCode` members that are not gate identifiers — `handoff-schema-invalid`, `handoff-task-field-count` and `handoff-set-inequality` — are asserted absent from `GATE_IDS`, and `orchestrate handoff validate` still reports `handoff-verify-failed` for a document whose first violation is one of them: the compile error at the collapse site is closed by a `GateId` type predicate, never by admitting those three codes.
- [x] AC-6: `handoff-pin-untrusted` and `lane-plan-incomplete` are asserted absent from every bundled `kiwi-orchestrator` SKILL.md variant including the `.agents` mirror, under a guard that at least three variant bodies were read. This records that admitting them to `GATE_IDS` leaves their `--auto` classification unchanged at `business-decision` under `FR-FLOW-073` AC-4, because that classification is keyed on the skill tables and not on the TypeScript union; a later session that declares either identifier takes the test red and has to re-decide deliberately.

### FR-NODE-167 — abort_gate replaces the colliding top-level reason_class on an abort line

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md |

#### Requirement

An `abort-run` journal line SHALL record why the run was abandoned in a field named `abort_gate` whose value is a member of the exported `GateId` union, and SHALL NOT write a top-level `reason_class`. `abort_gate` SHALL be declared as an optional field in `waves-event.md` §2.2 in all four shipped copies and in `WAVES_EVENT_FIELDS.optional`, additively and without raising the document version. A line whose `abort_gate` falls outside `GateId` SHALL produce the diagnostic `abort-gate-outside-vocabulary`, at error severity on the newest line of the run and at warning severity on every earlier line, so that an append carrying a bad value is refused while a run already holding one can still be aborted.

#### Rationale

Measured on the validator: a top-level `reason_class` carrying `utter-garbage` produces no diagnostic, while the identical string inside `verification.residual[]` produces `reason-class-outside-vocabulary` at error severity. `orchestrate run abort` writes the top-level form verbatim from `--reason`, so the field both collides with a name the contract gives to a nested eight-value vocabulary and is enforced nowhere. It is also absent from both halves of `WAVES_EVENT_FIELDS`, so it is undeclared as well as colliding; measured separately, an entirely invented top-level field also produces no diagnostic, which is why declaring the field is not by itself enforcement and the rule has to be written.

The severity split is not a refinement. `appendWavesLine` validates the whole resulting journal and refuses on any error, so a rule at error severity over history would brick every run holding one non-conforming line — including making `run abort` itself impossible, which would leave the run lock held with no way to release it. Scoping the error to the newest line makes the append the thing that is refused: during an append the newest line is the candidate, and a legacy line is always earlier than the line being added.

`gate` cannot be the field name: `waves-event-parity.fr-node-129.test.ts:325-326` asserts `WAVES_EVENT_FIELDS.optional` does not contain it. `abort_reason` re-imports the accident this requirement removes.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `orchestrate run abort --reason <id>` writes a line carrying `abort_gate` equal to the option value and carrying no top-level `reason_class` key at all.
- [x] AC-2: `abort_gate` is a row of the §2.2 optional-field table in all four shipped `waves-event.md` copies — `skills/{claude,codex,etc}/_shared/kiwi/` and the `.agents/` mirror — each with a non-empty type cell and a non-empty purpose cell, and is a member of `WAVES_EVENT_FIELDS.optional`, so the set-equality `FR-NODE-129` AC-1 asserts in both directions still holds.
- [x] AC-3: The document version still reads `v1.4.0` in all four copies and no copy is bumped, because `FR-FLOW-104` AC-1 requires that string; the cost — that `v1.4.0` now names two field sets with no version signal between them — is recorded in this requirement rather than left to be discovered.
- [x] AC-4: A journal line whose `abort_gate` is outside `GateId` produces `abort-gate-outside-vocabulary`; a line whose `abort_gate` is a `GateId` member produces none, and a line carrying no `abort_gate` produces none.
- [x] AC-5: The diagnostic is error severity on the newest line of the run and warning severity on every earlier line. Asserted both ways: an append of a bad value is refused with the journal left byte-identical, and an append of a good value onto a journal whose earlier line already carries a bad value succeeds.
- [x] AC-6: `abort-gate-outside-vocabulary` is registered in `JOURNAL_RULE_CODES` and carries a `JOURNAL_RULES` row naming its source, so the rule tables the parity harness enumerates stay complete.

Across both files of this change the red-first run was 10 failed / 3 passed before any source edit. AC-2 was red on both halves - no copy declared an abort_gate row and WAVES_EVENT_FIELDS.optional did not contain it. AC-4's second case and all three AC-5 cases were red because the rule did not exist, and AC-6 was red because the code was in neither rule table. The one value outside GateId that raises nothing is an explicit JSON null, which the guard treats as absent by design; that decision is recorded in the implementation notes rather than left as a silent gap. 10 passed on this file, re-measured independently. |

#### Research / Analysis

- -

#### Implementation Notes

- The value space is the whole of `GateId` and not a named subset. Measured, the shipped skill names exactly two gate identifiers that end a run through `abort-run` — `route-deescalation-refused` and `design-contradiction-at-wave-boundary` — but §V.abort-run instructs the agent to report whatever gate caused the halt, which is open-ended over the critical gates. A two-member subset would be wrong the first time a third gate ends a run.
- 
- Two consequences of that choice, both stated rather than left to be found. First, `FR-NODE-166` admitted `handoff-pin-untrusted` and `lane-plan-incomplete` to `GATE_IDS`, so both are now legal `abort_gate` values though neither can ever be one; this is accepted imprecision, not an oversight. Second, the two budget abort paths the skill documents — `--run-budget` exceeded and `--subagent-budget` exhausted — name no gate identifier at all, so after this change they cannot call `run abort` honestly. That is a real gap and is reported here rather than closed by widening the type: the fix is a gate identifier for budget exhaustion, which is a §13 decision and not this requirement's.
- 
- Declare with a table row only, never a §2.3 bullet: `FR-NODE-129` AC-1 asserts §2.2 field-table parity as set equality in both directions against `journal-schema.ts`, and a bullet has no counterpart there.
- [2026-08-02] An explicit JSON null in abort_gate reads as ABSENT and raises nothing — deliberate, since an optional field serialised as null is not carried, and raising would refuse legitimate lines. A second verification pass named null as the ONLY value outside GateId producing no diagnostic; every other shape raises, including number, boolean, array, object, empty string, and a string differing only by case or whitespace, because nothing trims or case-folds.

### FR-NODE-168 — A round record is identifiable, and neither run-state reader takes one for a wave status or a final verification

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md |

#### Requirement

A verification-round record SHALL be identifiable from the line alone by a top-level `round` field carrying its 1-based index, declared in `waves-event.md` §2.2 across all four shipped copies and in `WAVES_EVENT_FIELDS.optional`. Neither run-state reader SHALL take a round record for something it is not: a round record SHALL NOT become a wave's latest recorded status, and SHALL NOT become the run's final-verification record, even when it asserts a status and carries a passing verdict. Every other line SHALL read exactly as it does today.

#### Rationale

`FR-NODE-159` and `FR-NODE-161` both discriminate on `assertsStatus`, and both changes notes say in as many words that a round record is excluded *because a round record asserts no status*. Measured, that footing is about to be removed: `status` is the only member of `WAVES_EVENT_FIELDS.required` the validator enforces — dropping each of the eight in turn, seven are accepted silently and only `status` raises `verification-without-status` — so a round record carrying a `verification` object cannot be written status-free. The moment `orchestrate round record` writes a real line, both readers stop discriminating.

No weaker discriminator survives contact with the two requirements. Status cannot serve: `wave-status-invariant.fr-node-159.test.ts:74-84` asserts, on a **verified** row, that a status-bearing line on a completed wave reopens it and is *"taken at its word"*. Nor can a completion-monotonicity guard, for the same reason — that test is exactly the `complete` then `in_progress` case. And shape cannot serve either: the contract's own §5 emit example writes the per-round wave-verify line with `status: "in_progress"`, a full `verification` object and `phase: "wave-verify"`, which is field-for-field what a loop-P round record looks like.

So the line has to say what it is. `round` is that field: `orchestrate.ts` already writes it, it is undeclared in both halves of `WAVES_EVENT_FIELDS`, and declaring it answers the objection that it was undeclared rather than deleting the only thing that can carry the distinction.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `round` is a row of the §2.2 optional-field table in all four shipped `waves-event.md` copies with a non-empty type cell and a non-empty purpose cell, and is a member of `WAVES_EVENT_FIELDS.optional`.
- [x] AC-2: A line carrying a top-level `round` does not become its wave's latest recorded status: appended to a journal whose wave-1 is complete and wave-2 in progress, the wave-status map and the run's first incomplete wave are both unchanged.
- [x] AC-3: A line carrying a top-level `round` does not become the run's final-verification record: a run whose waves are all complete and whose only `final-verify`-phased line is a round record still owes a final verification, even when that record asserts a status and carries verdict `pass`.
- [x] AC-4: `FR-NODE-159` AC-2's status-bearing case is preserved exactly: a line asserting `in_progress` on a completed wave and carrying **no** `round` is still honoured and still reopens the wave. The new rule narrows the round-record case only.
- [x] AC-5: `FR-NODE-161` AC-2's genuine run-scope final-verification line — status-asserting, no `round` — still discharges the run.
- [x] AC-6: The readers use one shared predicate, so they cannot disagree about what a round record is; a source census asserts the predicate has exactly the three call sites named by AC-2, AC-3 and AC-7.
- [x] AC-7: A round record does not enter the program-counter index and so cannot mask an interrupted verb. Every verb a round record projects is a real program-counter verb and the record carries no `stage` and no `lane`, so its `(verb, wave, stage, lane)` key collides with that verb's own bucket by construction: on a journal whose `final-verify` intent is unmatched, appending a loop-F round record leaves that verb still reported as interrupted, and the round record is absent from that bucket.

#### Verification Evidence

### FR-NODE-169 — orchestrate round record writes a conforming round line

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md |

#### Requirement

`orchestrate round record` SHALL write a journal line that describes the round it was given: `scope` SHALL be validated against 05 §5.1's closed six-member vocabulary and an out-of-vocabulary value SHALL be refused with `invalid-run-scope-option`; `phase`, `verb`, `wave`, `order` and `target` SHALL be derived from the loop and its scope rather than hard-coded; the line SHALL carry `status`, `ts`, `summary`, its 1-based `round` index and an externally recomputable `proof`; and `verification` SHALL be an explicit snake_case projection carrying the mapped verdict, the round and cap, the residual list and the open-row counts.

#### Rationale

The verb has never once executed its stated behaviour. Four defects sit on one payload literal — grep `verb: "verify-design"` in `orchestrate.ts`: no `status`, no `phase`, `verb` hard-coded `verify-design` for all six loops, and `payload.wave` read while `Round` declares `scope` and no `wave`, so `wave` is always the `"all"` fallback.

Every derivation below was measured rather than assumed. `waveNumber` is `/^wave-(\d+)$/`, so of the six legal scopes only `wave-{n}` parses: `wave-1-post`, `wave-1-lane-2`, `wave-1-lane-2-handoff`, `design` and `run` all return `null` — five of six, not one. A round line carrying `wave: "wave-1-post"` is refused with `complete-without-latest-pass` where the same round under `wave: "wave-1"` completes, so the reduction is not cosmetic.

`status` cannot be omitted: it is the only member of `WAVES_EVENT_FIELDS.required` the validator enforces, measured by dropping each of the eight in turn. That once made both run-state readers safe by accident; `FR-NODE-168` replaced that accident with `round` as an explicit discriminator, which is why this requirement can give every round line a status without reopening a wave or discharging a run.

A whole-`Round` dump is not a projection: `Round` is camelCase and the validator reads snake_case, and the same dishonest round validated with 5 diagnostics under the correct projection and 0 under the dump.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A `scope` outside 05 §5.1's closed vocabulary — `design`, `wave-{n}`, `wave-{n}-lane-{k}`, `wave-{n}-post`, `wave-{n}-lane-{k}-handoff`, `run` — is refused with `invalid-run-scope-option` at exit 2, and the journal is left byte-identical.
- [x] AC-2: A `scope` whose loop disagrees with the payload's `loop` field is refused the same way, so the line cannot be written with a phase from one loop and a wave from another.
- [x] AC-3: `phase` is derived from the loop: D to `design`, W to `wave-design`, H to `handoff`, L to `lane`, P to `wave-verify`, F to `final-verify`. Asserted for all six, because `verify-design` was hard-coded for all six before.
- [x] AC-4: `verb` is derived from the loop: D to `verify-design`, W to `verify-wave-design`, H to `verify-handoff`, L to `verify-lane`, P to `post-merge-verify`, F to `final-verify`. All six are members of the closed verb enum.
- [x] AC-5: `wave`, `order` and `target` are derived from the scope: the four wave-scoped forms reduce to `wave-{n}`, `order = n` and `target = "wave-{n}"`, and the two run-scoped forms take `wave: "all"`, `order: 0` and `target: "all"`. Asserted per scope form, including that `wave-{n}-post` reduces rather than being written through.
- [x] AC-6: The line carries `ts` as an ISO-8601 UTC instant, a non-empty `summary`, `status`, and its 1-based `round` index — the field `FR-NODE-168` makes both run-state readers depend on. A round record appended to a journal whose wave is complete leaves that wave complete.
- [x] AC-7: The line carries a `proof` whose kind is a member of `EXTERNAL_PROOF_KINDS`, supplied by the caller through a required `--proof` option. A proof of a journal-only kind is refused by the existing `journal-only-verdict` rule rather than by a second check.
- [x] AC-8: `verification` is an explicit snake_case projection and never the `Round` object: it carries `verdict` mapped per AC-9, `rounds` and `cap`, `residual` as `{id, reason_class}` rows, `axis_b.open` counted from the round's open rows by severity, and `frozen_denominator` carrying the round index and the frozen row count.
- [x] AC-9: The verdict map is: `pass` to `pass`; `pass-with-residual` to `pass` with the residual carried in full; `fail-cap` to `fail-cap`; `fail-residual` to `in-progress`; and `invalid` to `fail-cap` when the outcome is unreachable and to `in-progress` otherwise. The `invalid` split is asserted in both directions, because `evaluateRound` short-circuits the denominator mismatch ahead of the cap check, so an arithmetically unpassable round returns `invalid` and would otherwise be journalled as still running.
- [x] AC-10: A recorded round leaves the journal valid: appending a round record for each of the six loops in turn produces zero error diagnostics.

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

Red first and completely: 19 of 19 failed before any source change, because --proof did not exist and commander rejected the invocation - a blanket red rather than a per-criterion one, recorded here as such rather than dressed up. The per-criterion greens after implementation are the informative half. AC-3 and AC-4 are asserted per loop over all six legal scopes AND by a distinctness case that counts six distinct verbs and six distinct phases across the six records, so the hard-coded verify-design the change removes could not satisfy them. AC-5 asserts wave-2-lane-3, wave-3-post and wave-4-lane-1-handoff each reduce to their wave-{n} form rather than being written through, which is what stops five of the six scopes failing waveNumber. AC-9 asserts the invalid split in both directions on the same fixture family: mode max cap 5 roundIndex 4 with a mismatched denominator maps to fail-cap because the outcome is unreachable, while mode normal cap 9 roundIndex 1 maps to in-progress. Three fixtures were repaired rather than the product during the green pass, and the repairs are the requirement working: a pass-with-residual and a fail-cap record are terminal, so truncated-residual demanded the residual list enumerate the open rows, and a seeded wave complete needed its own passing wave-verify record ahead of it. Full suite: 347 files / 4497 passed / 3 skipped / 0 failed against a 345 / 4473 / 3 baseline, exactly +2 files and +24 tests across the two files this change adds. |

AC-4 lacked the enum check its sibling had: phase was compared against WAVE_PHASES and verb against nothing, so the six verbs were pinned only by literals duplicated inside this file. AC-6's completion clause was true of any append-only writer and so could not fail; it now asks a reader, computeRunProgress, whether the wave is still complete. AC-7's requiredness had no case omitting the option, and a bare non-zero exit assertion does not carry it either, because relaxing requiredOption to option still fails further downstream with a different exit; the case now pins the live commander option as mandatory and asserts the usage exit specifically. AC-8's fixture set roundIndex and frozenDenominator both to 2 and left three of four severity buckets at zero, so swapping the two sources of frozen_denominator and cycling the severity labels both survived; every count in the fixture is now distinct. Measured by mutation, all restored byte-identical: swapping the frozen_denominator sources is CAUGHT at 16 failed, cycling the severity labels CAUGHT, relaxing --proof CAUGHT, and removing the round-record exclusion from the wave-status reader CAUGHT. The list above names four; the fifth probe of the set widened the void declaration to fail-cap and belongs to FR-NODE-170 AC-5, where it is recorded. Two of the five still survived the first repair and the assertions were strengthened until they did not. |

#### Research / Analysis

- -

#### Implementation Notes

- `residual` and `axis_b.open` are deliberately projected from two different sources — the caller's `Round.residual` and the round's own open rows — so that `truncated-residual` stays live. Deriving both from one source would make that rule vacuous. The consequence is that a caller whose residual list does not enumerate its open findings is refused on a terminal verdict, which is the rule doing its job; it is off on `in-progress` lines by construction, so it is load-bearing only on `pass` and `fail-cap` records and must not be tested as a universal invariant.
- 
- `--proof` is required rather than optional. `waves-event.md:96` requires a verdict-bearing line to carry at least one externally recomputable proof, and a round record is verdict-bearing; but `checkJournalOnlyProofs` opens `if (proofs.length === 0) continue;`, so a proofless round line validates clean and violates the contract silently. The verb reads no filesystem, so it cannot synthesize a digest itself and the caller must supply one. Requiring it breaks no caller: the verb has never written a line.
- 
- `fail-residual` maps to `in-progress` with no caller-supplied terminality signal. Measured over 55,296 input points, kernel `fail-residual` is terminal in 0 of 13,304 cases and carries an empty residual list in 6,652 of them: it means "did not pass yet". The journal's `fail-residual` is terminal by definition, and its real producer is the oscillation stop, which is unimplemented — do not reach back for this mapping to supply it.

Not verified: no independent pass yet. |

### FR-NODE-170 — A void round is recordable: denominator-mismatch refuses an undeclared mismatch, not a declared one

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md |

#### Requirement

A verification object MAY declare its round void by carrying `verification.invalid_round: true`, declared as a `waves-event.md` §2.3 bullet in all four shipped copies. The `denominator-mismatch` rule SHALL refuse an **undeclared** enumerated-versus-frozen mismatch and SHALL NOT refuse one on a line that declares its round void, so the only honest record of a void round is writable. Every other line SHALL be refused exactly as it is today.

#### Rationale

`evaluateRound` short-circuits on `rows.length !== frozenDenominator` and returns `invalid` before the cap check, so a round whose enumeration disagrees with its frozen denominator is void. Recording that honestly means writing the mismatch into the journal — and `denominator-mismatch` refuses exactly that shape at error severity, so `appendWavesLine` refuses the write. The only honest record of a void round is unwritable.

Scoping the rule to terminal verdicts alone was the earlier proposal and is not enough: `FR-NODE-169`'s map sends both `invalid` and `fail-residual` to `in-progress`, so a terminal-only exemption exempts every non-terminating round line, and the journal can no longer tell a void round from a still-running one — which is the exact fact the exemption exists to preserve. A declaration keeps both: a void round is recordable AND identifiable, and a mismatch nobody declared is still refused.

The cost is one §2.3 bullet in four copies. `waves-event-parity.fr-node-129.test.ts` asserts every §2.3 bullet is claimed by a rule anchor or by the declared non-sources, so the bullet is anchored from `denominator-mismatch`'s own `sourceBullets` — which is honest, because the rule really is drawn from it.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `invalid_round` is a §2.3 bullet in all four shipped `waves-event.md` copies, and `denominator-mismatch`'s `sourceBullets` anchor resolves to exactly one bullet in each, so the parity harness's every-bullet-claimed assertion still holds.
- [x] AC-2: A line whose enumerated counts disagree with `frozen_denominator` and which does **not** declare `invalid_round` still raises `denominator-mismatch` at error severity — the rule is narrowed, not disabled.
- [x] AC-3: The same line with `verification.invalid_round: true` raises no `denominator-mismatch`, so an honest void round can be appended.
- [x] AC-4: The declaration exempts `denominator-mismatch` only: a line declaring `invalid_round` that also violates a different rule is still refused on that other rule.
- [x] AC-5: `orchestrate round record` sets `invalid_round: true` on exactly the rounds `evaluateRound` returned `invalid` for, and omits the key otherwise — asserted in both directions, so the declaration cannot be used to launder a mismatch on a round the kernel judged valid.

Red first, 3 failed / 2 passed: AC-1 failed because no rule claimed an invalid_round bullet, AC-3 with expected [denominator-mismatch] to not include denominator-mismatch, and AC-4 the same. The two that were GREEN from the first run are the narrowing guards - an undeclared mismatch is still refused, and a falsy declaration does not exempt - and they are evidenced by mutation: making the exemption unconditional takes exactly those two red, with the source restored to its pre-probe sha1. AC-4 is the case that keeps the exemption from spreading: a line declaring itself void AND truncating its residual is still refused on truncated-residual while denominator-mismatch stays silent. The §2.3 bullet was reworded once during the green pass after it shadowed an existing lookup: kiwi-event-contract-content.test.ts resolves the row-count rule by the first line matching a phrase the first draft of this bullet also contained, which took 4 tests red across the four copies. ### FR-NODE-171 — The hedge scan matches on an ASCII-letter boundary, and the prose gate carries an admission ticket

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md |

#### Requirement

`scanProse`'s hedge detector SHALL match a token bounded by ASCII letters — `(?<![A-Za-z])token(?![A-Za-z])` — rather than by substring containment, so that every one of the eighteen declared tokens still fires and a token embedded in a longer word does not. The repository SHALL additionally carry a corpus-fitness case asserting that no design document raises an `unmarked-normative-prose` finding; while that assertion is false it SHALL be skipped with its reason recorded, and un-skipping it SHALL be what admits the gate to be wired.

#### Rationale

`scanProse` matched with `includes()`, so `thoroughly` matched the hedge token `roughly` and `Maybelline` matched `maybe`. Neither obvious repair works: `\b` silences all six Hangul tokens, and a `\p{L}\p{N}` lookaround silences their particle forms — measured at 6 and 9 red cases. The shipped matcher bounds a token by ASCII letters only. The corpus case lands skipped because the gate is not fit to refuse anything yet: 7 `unmarked-normative-prose` findings across 4 of the 13 design documents.



#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Every one of the eighteen `HEDGE_TOKENS` still raises a `hedge` finding in a document containing it, asserted per token, so the boundary change narrows matching without silencing any token. This is the assertion the naive `\b` repair would have taken six cases red on.
- [x] AC-2: A token embedded in a longer word raises no finding: `thoroughly` does not match `roughly` and `Maybelline` does not match `maybe`. Both are asserted by name, because both are measured true before the change.
- [x] AC-3: The matcher bounds a token by ASCII letters and by nothing else, asserted through `scanProse` rather than through a regular expression the test builds for itself: an ASCII letter on either side means embedding, while punctuation, a digit or a character of another script is a neighbour and not a longer word.
- [x] AC-4: The corpus-fitness case exists, is registered with `it.skip`, and its skip reason names the three structural false-positive classes and the current finding count — asserted by an unskipped case that reads this test file's own source, so deleting the admission ticket or silently un-skipping it fails rather than passing unnoticed.
- [x] AC-5: The corpus case carries a non-vacuity guard on the file count, so a glob that resolves to nothing would fail it rather than pass it, and the guard runs even though the case is skipped — asserted by a separate, unskipped case over the same corpus loader.
- [x] AC-6: `FR-NODE-121` VE-1's pass count is re-measured after the change and recorded; no criterion of that requirement turns false, so it is not demoted.
- [x] AC-7: Korean agglutination still raises a finding. Each Hangul token followed immediately by each of the particles 은, 는, 이, 가, 로, 도, 라고 and 하다 raises a `hedge` finding, and so does reduplication such as 대충대충. Korean writes no space before a particle, so a word boundary on either side of a Hangul token silences the ordinary written form of the language rather than an embedding.
- [x] AC-8: An ASCII token adjacent to a character of another script, or to a digit, still raises a finding: `maybe라는`, `roughly한` and `approximately3` each fire, while the AC-2 embeddings still do not. A boundary defined over all Unicode letters cannot tell a foreign-script particle from a longer word.

#### Verification Evidence

### FR-NODE-172 — Every top-level field a journal writer emits is a declared field

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

`untested_allowance` SHALL be declared as an optional journal field, as a §2.2 row in all four shipped `waves-event.md` copies and as a member of `WAVES_EVENT_FIELDS.optional`. Every top-level key a typed `appendWavesLine` call site writes SHALL be a declared field, asserted over a line the CLI really produces rather than over a restatement of the payload.

#### Rationale

`FR-NODE-155` AC-3 requires the untested-AC allowance in force to be journalled, and `orchestrate handoff validate` writes it as a top-level `untested_allowance`. Measured across every typed `appendWavesLine` call site, it is the only key written that neither half of `WAVES_EVENT_FIELDS` declares — `abort_gate` and `round`, the two found before it, were both declared as they were found. An undeclared field is enforced nowhere and read by nothing: `reason_class` was undeclared for exactly that reason and collided with a name `verification.residual[]` owns over a different closed vocabulary before anyone noticed. Declaring it also removes the last obstacle to closing the append payload's type over the declared field set, which is the check that would have caught `reason_class` at compile time. The `journal append --payload` leaf is deliberately outside this rule: it takes an arbitrary caller-supplied object and is guarded by validate-then-refuse instead.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `untested_allowance` is a row of the §2.2 optional-field table in all four shipped `waves-event.md` copies with a non-empty type cell and a non-empty purpose cell, and is a member of `WAVES_EVENT_FIELDS.optional` and not of `.required`.
- [x] AC-2: Running `orchestrate handoff validate` over a base declaring an untested-AC allowance and naming a run writes a line whose every top-level key is a member of `WAVES_EVENT_FIELDS` — required or optional — with `writer`, the stamp the append helper adds, among the declared names.
- [x] AC-3: The key census is non-vacuous: the line read back really carries `untested_allowance` and more than one key, so a line that failed to parse or resolved to an empty object cannot pass it.

#### Verification Evidence

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

AC-1 failed with the first copy declaring no untested_allowance row, and AC-2 failed naming exactly one undeclared key, untested_allowance, which is the whole population of the defect and confirms the measurement that it was the last such field at any typed call site. The census reads a line the CLI really wrote rather than a restatement of the payload: it runs orchestrate handoff validate over a base declaring an allowance of 2, reads kiwi/waves.jsonl back off disk and takes the top-level keys of the parsed line. AC-3's floors are asserted before the census rather than after, because a line that failed to parse or came back empty would otherwise satisfy no undeclared key by having no keys at all. The two homes had to land together: FR-NODE-129 AC-1 asserts the field table set-equal to the constant in both directions, so either half alone takes the parity suite red. Re-measured after: waves-event-parity and kiwi-event-contract-content are green at 249 passed across the three files, so the new row shadows no rule locator — the hazard that forced a reword when FR-NODE-171 added a bullet. Deliberately out of scope and stated as such: journal append --payload takes an arbitrary caller-supplied object and is guarded by validate-then-refuse instead, so it is not one of the typed call sites this requirement closes. |

### FR-NODE-173 — Skill install service refuses to destroy unmanaged content and reports what it measured

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | skills, install, safety, metadata |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The skill installation service SHALL refuse to overwrite a destination skill directory it does not own, in every scope rather than only the custom one; SHALL report install metadata that describes the destination it produced rather than restating its source; SHALL report in the plan every file the run would delete; SHALL record the shared resources a skill references whatever spelling the reference uses; and SHALL give a run interrupted mid-install a stated way back.

#### Rationale

An independent audit of `speckiwi skills install` measured eighteen defects, two of them critical. The destructive one: the guard that refuses to update a destination holding no SpecKiwi install metadata is gated on scope equal to custom, so the two scopes that write into a user's real agent directories skip it. A hand-authored skill directory containing SKILL.md plus notes.md was replaced with the bundled body, notes.md was deleted, and the command returned ok with operation update; the staged backup is removed on success, so the loss is unrecoverable. The reporting defects share a shape: a field named for a measurement carries a copy of the input instead. installedChecksum and installedFileCount are assigned the source digest, so a truncated copy would still self-report as matching, and the orphan prune trusts that value as its safety gate. filesRemoved is computed only in the file-count-mismatch branch, so a plan can promise no deletions and then delete. sharedResourceReferences is empty on every claude install while those skills cite contracts by name. A crashed install leaves a directory that fails forever with a message that never says how to recover, and leftover stage and backup directories are never reaped.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A destination skill directory that holds no SpecKiwi install metadata is refused in every scope, not only custom, and the refusal message names the destination and states that removing it is how to proceed, so unmanaged content is never silently replaced.
- [ ] AC-2: `installedChecksum` and `installedFileCount` describe the destination the run produced, and a test computes both from the destination tree and asserts the recorded values match.
- [x] AC-3: The plan's `filesRemoved` counts every file the run would delete, including the case where source and destination hold the same number of files but not the same files, so a plan that promises no deletion never deletes.
- [x] AC-4: `sharedResourceReferences` records the shared contracts a skill cites whatever spelling the citation uses. Writing `_shared/kiwi/<name>.md` in a skill body is therefore a dependency declaration, and a citation whose contract is absent, or whose path climbs out of the shared root, refuses the install and names the offending reference. Scanning covers the skill's own package files only, so a retired name mentioned in a repository-level migration note cannot fail a run.
- [ ] AC-5: A destination that a previous run left without a valid entrypoint is named in the refusal message together with the recovery step, and a later successful install reaps any staging or backup directory its own interrupted run left behind.
- [x] AC-6: The doctor drift check compares every file a bundled skill ships, not only `SKILL.md`, so a stale non-entrypoint file is reported instead of passing as current.
- [ ] AC-7: An automated test drives each of the above through the real service and fails when the guard, the measurement, or the count regresses.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/skills/install-safety.fr-node-173.test.ts | AC-1, AC-3, AC-4, AC-6 | 9 cases, 7 red before the fix. AC-1 runs the unmanaged-destination case in all three scopes and asserts the file the previous behaviour deleted is still there. Changing the guard and the shared-reference collector broke three pre-existing tests that pinned the old message, the old preflight constant and a replicated copy of the collector regex; each was updated to the new contract rather than around it, and the replicated-pattern guard is what surfaced that the claude rendering had been excluded from a prune assertion. |

### FR-NODE-174 — The verified transition refuses evidence references it cannot resolve

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.7.0-verification-integrity |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

A status transition to `verified` SHALL refuse when any of the requirement's verification-evidence references is path-like and does not resolve to an existing file under the project root, and the refusal SHALL name the offending reference; the resolution rule SHALL be the one release readiness already applies, not a second implementation of it.

#### Rationale

`update_status` admits `verified` on three conditions: at least one acceptance criterion, all of them checked, and at least one evidence row whose reference is a non-empty string. A non-empty string is not a reference — measured, 54 requirements in this repository carry evidence pointing at `~/.claude/skills/...`, a directory outside version control, and 44 of them are `verified`. `collectMissingEvidenceReferences` in release-readiness.ts already computes exactly this, already restricts itself to `status === "verified"`, and already distinguishes `outside-project-root` from `missing` — it simply runs at release time, after the rows are locked. Granular edits are refused on a verified requirement, so every defect this check would have caught at the transition becomes unrepairable the moment it is missed. Moving the check earlier costs an existsSync per reference and turns a release-time discovery of a whole class into a per-row refusal at the moment of ticking.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A transition to `verified` is refused when a path-like verification-evidence reference resolves outside the project root, and the requirement's Status row is left unchanged.
- [x] AC-2: A transition to `verified` is refused when a path-like verification-evidence reference resolves under the project root but names no existing file, and the requirement's Status row is left unchanged.
- [x] AC-3: The refusal names the offending reference, states the kind of failure the resolver found, and describes that kind and no other; the four kinds do not share a description.
- [x] AC-4: A reference classified as a command or as a valid http(s) URL by the release-readiness classifier does not cause a refusal, so the two gates agree on what a path-like reference is.
- [x] AC-5: The rule has one implementation: each identifier that carries it appears in exactly one source file, and each exported entry point is defined in exactly one source file. A parallel implementation written under entirely different names is outside what this check can detect and is not claimed to be.
- [x] AC-6: Transitions to statuses other than `verified` are unaffected by evidence-reference resolution, including a transition away from `verified`.
- [x] AC-7: Every route that can write a body `Status` row of `verified` either applies the same resolution — the status transition, direct creation at `verified`, and promotion of a step block that already reads `verified` — or refuses the payload that would produce such a row without one, which is how `add_requirement` and `supersede_requirement` satisfy it through the `title` field. The gate cannot be walked around by choosing a different call.
- [x] AC-8: Every issue kind the shared resolver reports — an unresolvable path, a reference outside the root, an empty reference cell, and a malformed URL — refuses the promotion, and each is exercised by a test rather than left as undeclared behaviour.
- [x] AC-9: No mutation writes caller-supplied text into a requirement block in a form the parser would read as opening a section, whether that text is a prose field or the `reason` of a status, stability or retarget change. One shared guard carries the rule, and it recognises a heading wherever the parser does rather than where CommonMark does: the parser matches its section pattern against the trimmed line, so a guard bounded to three leading spaces left a gap through which an indented heading deleted requirements from the parsed model and turned a blocked release ready with no validation error. A route added later under a different name is outside what this pins, and is not claimed to be.
- [x] AC-10: The guard covers the parser's other record boundary as well as its heading: a code fence. `scanRequirementBlocks` enters fence state on a fence delimiter and returns on every line after it, so a single unclosed fence in caller-supplied text ends the requirement block and hides every block that follows — measured deleting two requirements from the parsed model and turning `ready` false into true with no validation error at all, which is the silent form of this class. Every value the guard admits is measured against the parser rather than against its source text, in one direction: a prefix or a fence spelling the parser treats as a boundary must be refused, while the guard is permitted to be stricter. The guard also covers the single-line values a heading or an index row is rendered from — a requirement's `title`, a scope name, a target goal — where one newline puts the remainder at column zero.

#### Verification Evidence

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/mutation/prose-heading-injection.fr-node-174.test.ts | AC-7, AC-9, AC-10 | 41 passed / 0 failed, 2026-08-04. Red first for every behavioural case added. Mutation-controlled: removing the fence branch takes 12 cases red. CORRECTION, made the same day by an independent audit: this row first claimed that weakening the heading pattern takes the guard/parser property case red, and that removing the fence branch takes 11 red. The first is false and the second was off by one — the property case was measured GREEN under exactly that weakening, because its payload was a record heading, which the parser reads only at column zero, so eight of its nine prefixes were inert. The case now runs the parser's own scanRequirementBlocks over a two-block document and carries a guard refusing to pass when no prefix is a boundary at all. |
| VE-2 | test | test/core/mutation/verified-evidence-resolution.fr-node-174.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6, AC-8 | 21 passed / 0 failed, 2026-08-04. These seven criteria carried no evidence row until an independent audit pointed out that the requirement's only row covered AC-7, AC-9 and AC-10 while the tests for the rest existed and were passing. AC-3 compares the four refusal proses and asserts the set of them has four members, so a shared description fails it. AC-5 was re-measured independently against three identifiers the case's own list omits, and each still resolves to exactly one source file. |

### FR-NODE-175 — Release readiness admits an enumerated residue instead of demanding every requirement verified

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.7.0-verification-integrity |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

Release readiness SHALL treat a requirement left at `implemented` as accepted only when the index declares a residue row for it that names the target, the requirement, and a specific acceptance criterion of that requirement which is currently unticked, together with a reason; an `implemented` requirement without such a row SHALL continue to block, and a row whose named criterion is ticked, absent, or belongs to another requirement SHALL itself block.

#### Rationale

The gate demands every requirement in a target reach `verified`. Measured, that has produced 137 requirements stranded at `implemented` across seven targets, and two targets have now stalled on the identical undischargeable criterion: FR-FLOW-047..066 in v2.4.4 and FR-FLOW-074..081 here, both on a mutation-ledger promise that `05 §10.3` states the ledger cannot supply. A gate that never closes is abandoned in practice while remaining on paper, which is less honest than an argued change. The alternative reached for instead — rewording the blocking criteria until the gate closes on its own terms — erases the evidence that a decision was made, which is why the residue is recorded separately and per requirement rather than absorbed into the criteria it excuses. Requiring the row to name a criterion that is actually unticked is what stops the register becoming a rubber stamp: an excuse that no longer matches the requirement goes stale and the gate blocks again.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: An `implemented` requirement in the release target blocks readiness when the index declares no residue row for it, exactly as before this requirement existed.
- [x] AC-2: An `implemented` requirement does not block readiness when the index declares a residue row naming its target, its id, one of its currently unticked acceptance criteria, and a reason that says something — which is more than "not the placeholder": a reason shorter than eight characters, or carrying fewer than two letters, is refused, because `--`, `—`, `N/A`, `TBD`, `.` and `?` are each neither empty nor the placeholder and each fully excused a criterion. The bar is a threshold and is stated as one in the source.
- [x] AC-3: A residue row naming an acceptance criterion that is ticked on that requirement blocks readiness and is reported as stale, so an excuse cannot outlive the gap it was written for.
- [x] AC-4: A residue row naming an acceptance criterion the requirement does not declare blocks readiness and is reported, so a row cannot be satisfied by an identifier that resolves to nothing.
- [x] AC-5: A residue row for a requirement that is not `implemented` — already verified, discarded, or never present in the target — blocks readiness and is reported, so the register cannot accumulate rows that excuse nothing. Each of those populations is constructed by a test rather than represented by the absent one alone.
- [x] AC-6: The summary reports the accepted residue separately from the requirements that still block, each entry naming the requirement, the criterion and the reason, so a reader can see what the release was accepted without.
- [x] AC-7: A residue row is scoped to the target it names: the same requirement left `implemented` under a different target still blocks there. A row naming a target that neither a requirement nor the index's Target Map declares is reported rather than filtered away, so a typo in that cell cannot put a row beyond the reach of every other check, while a row staged for a registered future target is skipped in silence.
- [x] AC-8: A residue row excuses the acceptance criterion it names and no other. An `implemented` requirement whose remaining unticked criteria are unnamed continues to block, and the report names those criteria, so waiving five criteria cannot cost what waiving one costs.
- [x] AC-9: A row that excuses nothing blocks readiness on its own strength, asserted in a target that is otherwise ready, so the block cannot be attributed to an unrelated blocker that was already false.
- [x] AC-10: The register is the table a renderer draws under the register's own heading, and the reader applies the rules a renderer applies: content indented four spaces is a code block and is not read; a fence is matched by CommonMark rather than counted, so a nested example cannot become the register; an HTML comment renders nothing and is not read; the header row must name the four declared columns and the separator row must immediately follow it, without which no renderer draws a table at all; and the table ends at the first line that is not part of it — a blank line, a paragraph, a heading or a fence. A row a reader sees as four cells is read whether or not it carries its leading and trailing pipes, an escaped pipe is reported as the table renders it, and a row with the wrong number of cells is reported.
- [x] AC-11: Nothing written between the register's heading and the end of its section is both unread and unmentioned. A heading that resembles the register without matching it, a second section carrying the same heading, a code fence left open earlier in the index, a residue row above the header, a row after the table has ended, a row whose cells are all dashes, a row naming a target nothing declares, a row naming an id more than one requirement block claims, and a reason too short to say anything are each reported. Content outside that section — a table under a later subheading, an example in another section — is outside this claim and is not read. The rule cannot judge whether a reason is TRUE, or whether a true reason is a good enough excuse; those limits are stated rather than implied.

#### Verification Evidence

### FR-NODE-176 — supersede mints the successor with the caller's requirement type

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.7.0-verification-integrity |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | supersede, cli, mcp, requirement-type |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The supersede operation shall mint the successor with the requirement type the caller gives it, and shall keep `functional` as the type when the caller gives none, so superseding an `interface`, `data` or `security` requirement yields a successor of that same type and therefore the ID prefix that type implies. The CLI `--type` option, which both the command and the tool registry already declare, shall carry that choice into the operation instead of being accepted and dropped, and the MCP tool shall accept the same argument so the two surfaces do not mint differently typed successors from the same request.

#### Rationale

Superseding IR-CLI-076 — a verified `interface` requirement — minted `FR-CLI-001` on a dry run. The type is hardcoded at `supersede-requirement.ts:183` and absent from `SupersedeRequirementInput`, so the operation can only ever mint `functional` successors, while the CLI advertises a `--type` option that reaches nothing. This is the same defect shape as IR-CLI-087 one command away: a declared input the command decides for the caller. It matters more here because the result is wrong data rather than a refused run — a mistyped requirement carries the wrong ID prefix permanently, and supersede is the only route by which a verified requirement can be discarded at all.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Superseding an `interface` requirement with the type `interface` mints a successor whose Type row reads `interface` and whose ID carries that type's prefix rather than the default `functional` prefix.
- [x] AC-2: A caller that gives no type still mints a `functional` successor, so the prior behaviour is the default rather than a removed capability.
- [x] AC-3: `speckiwi supersede --type interface` produces the AC-1 successor. The option was declared by the command and by the tool registry and never passed to the operation, so every CLI supersede of a non-functional requirement minted a wrongly typed successor.
- [x] AC-4: The MCP `supersede_requirement` tool accepts the same optional type argument, so an agent and an operator superseding the same requirement mint the same successor.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/mutation/supersede-successor-type.fr-node-176.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4 | 4 cases, all green. Red first for AC-1, AC-3 and AC-4: the core minted FR-ARCH-002 for a caller asking for `interface`, the CLI did the same with `--type interface`, and the MCP handler dropped the argument. AC-2 passed before the change because it pins the preserved default; it was measured mutation-sensitive by flipping the fallback to `input.type ?? "interface"`, which failed AC-2 alone. AC-1 and AC-2 supersede the same seeded `interface` requirement with the same arguments and differ only in whether the type is given. AC-4 was VACUOUS as first written and an independent audit proved it: deleting the MCP handler's pass-through — restoring the defect on that surface — left all four cases green, because the case only asserted that the schema declared the argument and never invoked the tool. It now calls `supersede_requirement` through `createMcpServer` and asserts both the minted ID and the written Type row; re-deleting the pass-through fails it with FR-ARCH-002. |

### FR-NODE-177 — Append-only reclassification of targetless workflow corrections

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.7.0-verification-integrity |
| Status | planned |
| Priority | critical |
| Tags | workflow, jsonl, record-reclassification, provenance, feasibility:high |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | Automated JSONL parser, mutation, incident-fixture, idempotency, and reader-convergence tests. |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi shall provide a typed workflow_record_reclassification mutation and a shared JSONL parser pre-pass that recover a parseable workflow record whose status is CORRECTION and whose correction-chain diagnostic is exactly SRS-W054 with reason missing correction target by appending a non-CORRECTION record_reclassification meta overlay that reclassifies the exact source record as audit_note/meta without modifying its bytes, inventing corrects_run_id, adding either record to latest-state, or adding either record to the correction graph.

#### Rationale

A targetless CORRECTION can be parseable and contain valid audit information while being invalid as a correction-chain edge. Existing append mutations halt on its SRS-W054 diagnostic and existing logical-delete requirements forbid deleting CORRECTION records. A typed append-only meta overlay resolves that classification error while retaining the original audit bytes and the existing deletion boundary.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: The typed mutation accepts no arbitrary event body and creates exactly one non-CORRECTION overlay whose schema_version is 1.0.0, skill is speckiwi, event is record_reclassification, run_id and derived eventKey are fixed by the dry-run result, ts is exactly the mutation-defined canonical synthetic instant 1970-01-01T00:00:00.000Z fixed in the dry-run result, recordClass is meta, effectiveRecordClass is audit_note, operation.kind is record_reclassification, owner and non-empty reason equal the typed request, workflow_run_id equals the mutation runId, journal_key equals the canonical FR-NODE-028 journal identity, idempotency_key equals a caller value only after the existing workflow compatibility guard accepts it and otherwise equals journal_key, the repairToken binds that effective idempotency value, and status and corrects_run_id are absent. Parsing rejects any other timestamp, including a different otherwise-valid ISO instant.
- [ ] AC-2: recordType is the closed enum pipeline or worklog. Core resolves the target only through resolveWorkflowArtifacts with that kind and the optional normalized root-relative path/runId selectors; the selected candidate must be inside the workspace, non-legacy, unambiguous, and have kind equal to recordType, and its resolved relativePath must equal the identity path. Unknown, legacy, outside-root, ambiguous, or path-kind-mismatched artifacts fail without writing.
- [ ] AC-3: The request, typed pendingRepair, repairToken, and durable overlay identify the target by normalized root-relative path, recordType, one-based line, zero-based UTF-8 byteOffset, rawSha256, eventKey, targetRunId, and preimagePrefixSha256. rawSha256 covers the exact file-byte slice from byteOffset through the last JSON byte and excludes the complete line terminator (both CR and LF for CRLF, LF for LF; EOF for an unterminated final line), without newline normalization or text re-encoding; preview and parsing accept the overlay only when every identity field matches exactly.
- [ ] AC-4: Dry-run writes no artifact, including no lock, recovery guard, quarantine, or sentinel, and returns written false, exactly one typed pendingRepair of kind record_reclassification, the predicted operation and diagnostic delta, and a repairToken bound to the complete target and journal identities, effective class, expectedSha256 journal preimage, canonical pendingRepair payload, and exact overlay bytes including provenance fields. First apply requires that repairToken and matching expectedSha256 and consumes only the pendingRepair encoded by that token; it never infers a repair from plan checkboxes, deleted/corrected records, or current diagnostics, satisfying FR-NODE-028 AC-5.
- [ ] AC-5: Before examining any durable exact replay, apply reconstructs the canonical pendingRepair and exact overlay bytes from the supplied typed fields and the freshly resolved target identity, recomputes the opaque repairToken, and verifies it in constant time together with expectedSha256 and effective idempotency. Only after that validation, an exact replay whose byte-identical overlay and effective projection already exist returns confirmed with written false, pendingRepair null, and the operation in completedOperations before stale-preimage rejection. When no exact replay exists, a stale expectedSha256, invalid or non-matching token, duplicate or ambiguous target, or non-identical overlay for the target fails without writing.
- [ ] AC-6: A successful first apply preserves the complete journal preimage as a byte-identical prefix, appends exactly the dry-run-bound UTF-8 JSON object plus LF, syncs it, and confirms through authoritative parseWorkflowJsonl read-back before reporting confirmed/written true. Read-back failure returns failed/written false with append completed, confirmation pending, and pendingRepair { kind: record_reclassification_confirmation, artifact: { relativePath, postAppendSha256 }, targetRecord, overlayEventKey, retry: { action: retry_same_record_reclassification, mode: confirm_only } }; retry validates the original repairToken and may only re-read and confirm the same overlay. If read-back succeeded but owner-verified lock cleanup failed, append and confirmation are completed, cleanup alone is pending, and pendingRepair is { kind: record_reclassification_lock_cleanup, artifact: { relativePath, postAppendSha256 }, lock: { relativePath, ownerIdentitySha256 }, cleanupDiagnostic, retry: { action: retry_same_record_reclassification, mode: cleanup_then_replay } }. ownerIdentitySha256 is correlation data, not authority. Core retains the failed owner capability and live fence in a process-local cleanup registry keyed by canonical lock identity; same-process retry atomically consumes it to finish owner-verified cleanup before exact replay without appending. After process exit, recovery uses only the shared run-lock dead-owner and guarded-quarantine protocol. Both failures expose typed diagnostics, never report confirmed, and never roll back or truncate the append. Parser validation binds expectedSha256 to the pre-append journal and preimagePrefixSha256 to the byte prefix immediately before the overlay.
- [ ] AC-7: Dry-run is lock-free. Every apply writer that can mutate the selected workflow JSONL artifact, including generic pipeline, worklog, repair, logical-delete, and record-reclassification writers, serializes through the same canonical artifact exclusive lock. The lock uses the shared Windows-aware run-lock state machine with live-owner fencing, token-bound ownership, synced sentinel publication, torn-sentinel grace, guarded quarantine recovery, and owner-verified release; it never steals a live owner solely because wall-clock time elapsed, never deletes a successor lock through compare-then-unlink, and never falls back to an unlocked append. Under that lock, two concurrent identical first applies using the same token, expectedSha256, and overlay produce exactly one written true result and one confirmed written false exact-replay result, with exactly one durable overlay. Two concurrent non-identical overlays for the same target permit at most one written true result; every loser returns a structured conflict or stale failure with written false, and the journal never contains both overlays.
- [ ] AC-8: The recovery mutation tolerates only the selected target record SRS-W054 diagnostic whose reason is missing correction target. A mismatched identity, another correction-chain reason, invalid JSON syntax, correction cycle or depth error, missing referenced event, conflicting overlay, or any unrelated diagnostic causes first apply to fail without writing; parsing an already valid overlay removes only that exact diagnostic and preserves every unrelated diagnostic code, file, line, and detail.
- [ ] AC-9: Before correction-chain resolution, parseWorkflowJsonl recognizes valid record_reclassification overlays, annotates the original raw entry as audit_note/meta and with its reclassification source, retains both original and overlay in entries/tail raw audit output, and excludes both from latestEntries and correction-graph membership. Every public JSONL consumer preserves that projection, including validateWorkflowArtifacts, workflowNextPlanTask, workflowDoctor, workflowDiff, workflowSchemaCheck, workflowPipelineStatus, workflowPipelineTail, workflowPipelineNext, workflowPipelineCompact, workflowWorklogTail, workflowSessionStatus, workflowResumeHint, and buildNextWorkOrder.
- [ ] AC-10: Generic pipeline-event, worklog-event, and workflow_repair_record append mutations reject status CORRECTION unless corrects_run_id is a string whose trimmed length is greater than zero; rejection occurs before duplicate-key confirmation or file mutation and covers absent, null, number, object, array, empty, and whitespace-only values. This does not apply to the typed non-CORRECTION workflow_record_reclassification mutation.
- [ ] AC-11: Automated tests reproduce the exact targetless-CORRECTION incident shape and cover typed pendingRepair dry-run and consumption, dry-run lock-artifact absence, token validation before replay, resolver and path-kind boundaries, CRLF/LF/final-line hashes, durable provenance and exact canonical timestamp rejection, first apply, authoritative post-append read-back and cleanup failure, byte-prefix preservation, exact replay, stale input, identity mismatch, ambiguity, conflicting and duplicate overlays, identical and conflicting real-process concurrent applies, live-owner non-steal, dead/torn lock recovery, recovery ABA and successor-release safety, lock residue, generic-writer serialization, unrelated diagnostics, parser projection, every named and derived public reader, whitespace-only generic correction targets, generic repair rejection, and unchanged logical-delete behavior.

### FR-NODE-178 — Run-root preflight corroborates the passed git root against the repository that path names

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.7.1 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/15.orchestrator-run-root-multi-module-research.md |

#### Requirement

The run-root preflight SHALL corroborate the `--git-root` value it is given before reporting a match: through an injected git-toplevel probe it SHALL resolve the top level of the repository containing that path, and it SHALL refuse with `run-root-preflight-mismatch` unless the resolved top level equals the passed value under the same normalisation rules, and likewise when the passed path lies in no git repository. When git cannot be consulted at all — absent, refusing, or failing for any reason other than the path naming no repository — the preflight SHALL report an operational error carrying git's own message rather than a gate refusal, because nothing was judged. The corroboration SHALL interrogate the repository named by the passed path and never the process working directory, SHALL NOT be steerable by ambient `GIT_DIR`-family environment variables in any letter case, and `normaliseRoot` SHALL remain free of filesystem and subprocess access, the git answer arriving through an injected probe exactly as the realpath answer already does.

#### Rationale

The gate compares two strings supplied by one caller, so a caller who needs it to pass can pass the same value twice — and `test/cli/orchestrate-surface.ir-cli-082.test.ts:216-234` pins that as a success (exit 0, ok true). A layout the gate refuses permanently maximises the incentive to do exactly that, and the run then carries a passed gate as a false attestation, which is worse than the split-brain the gate exists to prevent. Corroborating one side against the repository its own path names removes the forgery without making the comparison vacuous: the two sides still originate independently, which is the property SKILL.md:145 requires and the reason neither root may be defaulted by the tool.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Given `--git-root` names a directory that lies inside a git repository but is not that repository's top level, preflight refuses with gate `run-root-preflight-mismatch` and a non-zero exit, and it does so even when `--mcp-root` is passed the identical value.
- [x] AC-2: Given `--git-root` names a path that lies in no git repository, preflight refuses rather than reporting a match on string equality alone.
- [x] AC-3: Given `--git-root` is a corroborated git top level, every pass the four normalisation rules already granted is still granted — including one decided by `separators` rather than by byte equality. The corroboration withholds passes from uncorroborated roots, which is its entire purpose; it takes none away from corroborated ones.
- [x] AC-4: The toplevel probe is consulted with the passed `--git-root` path. A fixture whose process working directory is a different git repository produces the same outcome as one run from outside any repository, so the corroboration cannot be satisfied by the directory the tool happens to run in.
- [x] AC-5: `normaliseRoot` keeps the no-filesystem-access property FR-NODE-153 AC-4 asserts: the corroboration lives in a separate function, and a fixture injecting a toplevel probe that answers for paths existing on no disk still decides the corroboration.
- [x] AC-6: When git cannot be consulted at all — absent from the host, refusing to read the directory, or failing for any reason other than the path naming no repository — preflight reports an operational error carrying git's own message, rather than reporting the path as naming no repository. A host without git must not be told its argument is wrong.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/orchestrator/run-root-corroboration.fr-node-178.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-5 | 8/8 pass. AC-1 drives the forgery directly — the module path passed as both roots, with a probe naming the repository above it — and asserts the verdict refuses while the two-root comparison still matches, which is why the comparison alone cannot decide it. AC-2 asserts an unlisted path yields git-root-not-a-repository with a null toplevel. AC-3 asserts an identical corroborated root and a separators-decided one both still pass. AC-5 asserts the corroboration decides with probes answering for paths on no disk, and scans preflight.ts for any import or filesystem token, which is FR-NODE-153 AC-4's guard restated where this change could break it. A further case pins the reason ordering when both conditions fail at once. |
| VE-2 | test | test/cli/preflight-corroboration.ir-cli-090.test.ts | AC-4, AC-6 | 8/8 pass. AC-4 runs the same bare-directory case twice — once from inside this repository, once with the process chdir'd outside any repository — and asserts identical outcomes, which is what shows the working directory was never consulted; the earlier single-fixture form could not distinguish that. AC-6 passes a directory that does not exist and asserts exit 1 with no gate and git's own message, rather than the exit-2 gate refusal an earlier version produced for every git failure alike. |
| VE-3 | test | test/core/root-facts.test.ts | AC-6 | 7/7 pass. Pins the resolver the corroboration depends on: an ambient GIT_WORK_TREE or GIT_DIR in either letter case must not become the answer (a review reproduced the Windows bypass this closes — lower-case git_work_tree survived an upper-case-only scrub and made the module corroborate itself); a dangling gitdir pointer and a missing directory throw GitUnavailableError rather than reporting no repository; git's own words survive into the error; and the locale is pinned where git's sentence is matched. |
