### FR-NODE-179 — Doctor reports a project root that is not the git top level

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.7.1 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/15.orchestrator-run-root-multi-module-research.md |

#### Requirement

The doctor SHALL report whether the resolved project root is the top level of the repository containing it, comparing the two with the same normalisation the run-root preflight applies, symlink resolution included. When the root lies inside a git repository but is not its top level the check SHALL warn and its remediation SHALL name the surfaces that silently key off the git top level rather than the project root — the pre-commit hook, the pipeline journal, and the agent skill destinations. When the root is the top level, when it lies in no git repository at all, or when git cannot be consulted, the check SHALL report ok; in the last two cases its message SHALL say which of them happened, a workspace under no version control being a supported configuration and an unconsultable git being an absence of evidence rather than evidence of a defect.

#### Rationale

`SRS root == git top level` is an unwritten invariant of the whole tool: README states the root is the Git repository, `upgrade` calls sub-directory scaffolding a mistake, and the index excludes multi-root hosting from scope — yet the only place that ever checks it is the orchestrator preflight, which most projects never run. Everything else keeps working well enough to look correct while the pre-commit hook, the pipeline journal the skills pin, and the skill destinations quietly follow a different directory. Doctor is where a project learns what is wrong with it before something breaks, and it currently performs no git check of any kind.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A workspace whose root is a git top level yields state `ok` for this check.
- [x] AC-2: A workspace whose root is a sub-directory of a git repository yields state `warn`, and the message names the resolved root and, separately from it, the top level it differs from.
- [x] AC-3: A workspace in no git repository yields state `ok` rather than `warn`, and its message says no repository was found rather than implying the layout is correct.
- [x] AC-4: The warn remediation names the pre-commit hook, the pipeline journal path, and the skill install destinations as the things that follow the git top level rather than the project root.
- [x] AC-5: The check is reported by `diagnoseHealth` alongside the existing checks, so every surface that renders a doctor report carries it without a further change.
- [x] AC-6: The comparison applies the same normalisation the run-root preflight applies, symlink resolution included, so a root reached through a symlink or a junction is not reported as a different directory from the top level it actually is.
- [x] AC-7: When git cannot be consulted at all — absent from the host, or refusing to read the directory — the check yields state `ok` and its message carries the reason git gave, so a host without git is never told its layout is wrong.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/health/doctor-project-root.fr-node-179.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6, AC-7 | 7/7 pass. AC-2 asserts the top level is named in its own right: the root's own occurrences are removed from the message before the containment check, because the root is nested inside the repository and the naive assertion passed on a message that never named the top level. AC-6 builds a junction to a real repository root and asserts ok — the earlier path.resolve().toLowerCase() comparison failed this on the host it was written on, so the criterion is discriminating rather than theoretical; where the host withholds the privilege the case skips instead of returning, which would have recorded a pass with no coverage. AC-7 drives the cannot-look branch through diagnoseHealth's injected resolver and asserts ok plus the reason. |

### FR-NODE-180 — Resume refuses when the repository it resumes in is not the one the run was pinned to

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.7.1 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/15.orchestrator-run-root-multi-module-research.md |

#### Requirement

`orchestrate resume` SHALL compare the git top level recorded in the resume card's `frozen.run_root.git_toplevel` against the top level of the repository containing the root it is resuming, and SHALL refuse with `run-invariant-drift` when they differ, when the resumed root lies in no repository, or when the card carries no pin at all. When git cannot be consulted, resume SHALL report an operational error rather than refusing on a gate or resuming unchecked, because nothing was judged. The comparison SHALL use the same normalisation rules the run-root preflight uses, and the refusal SHALL name both the pinned value and the observed one. The check SHALL run after the card parses and validates and before any resume state is computed, so a refused resume dispatches nothing.

#### Rationale

The card has carried `run_root: { git_toplevel, mcp_workspace_root }` since it was designed, and `computeInvariantDigest` covers both fields — but the pair appears nowhere else in the source. Nothing ever compares them to the world, so the digest only proves the card did not change; it says nothing about whether the session reading it is in the repository the run was pinned to. The preflight gate protects the start of a run and there is no equivalent at resume, which is where the exposure actually is: a resumed session has no conversation to contradict it, and the orchestrator's own measured incident was exactly a session whose root moved out from under it.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A resume whose card pins the repository it is running in proceeds exactly as before, reporting resume state and rung.
- [x] AC-2: A resume whose card pins a different repository refuses with gate `run-invariant-drift` and reports both the pinned top level and the observed one.
- [x] AC-3: A resume from a root that lies in no git repository refuses rather than proceeding on the ground that nothing contradicted the card.
- [x] AC-4: A pinned value differing only by separators, a trailing separator, or letter case is a match, so a card written on one spelling of a Windows path does not refuse every later resume.
- [x] AC-5: The check runs after card validation and before `computeResumeState`: a card that is invalid AND pinned elsewhere refuses as invalid, and a card that is valid but pinned elsewhere refuses on the run root even when the journal and drift inputs would have produced a clean resume.
- [x] AC-6: The comparison is not vacuous: the pinned side comes from the card written in an earlier session and the observed side is measured from the resumed root, so neither side is derived from the other.
- [x] AC-7: A card carrying no pinned git top level, or an empty one, is refused rather than resumed unchecked. The check cannot be skipped by deleting the field and recomputing the invariant digest, which is otherwise a self-consistent card.
- [x] AC-8: When git cannot be consulted — a broken gitdir pointer, an absent git, a refusal to read the directory — resume reports an operational error carrying git's own message and claims no gate, because refusing on a gate would assert the run root moved when nothing was judged, and resuming would be the unchecked resume this requirement exists to prevent.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/cli/resume-run-root-pin.fr-node-180.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6, AC-7, AC-8 | 9/9 pass. AC-5 is pinned in both directions: a card that is invalid and pinned elsewhere refuses as invalid, and a valid card pinned elsewhere refuses on the run root while the journal and drift inputs are the ones AC-1 resumes cleanly on. AC-7 deletes frozen.run_root and recomputes the invariant digest, producing a self-consistent card that passes validation — the state that previously skipped the check entirely, which two independent reviews named as the same forgery FR-NODE-178 was written against. AC-8 points a .git file at a gitdir that is not there and asserts exit 1 with no gate. |

| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Code | src/cli/commands/orchestrate.ts | implements | trace_intent: verifies; the run-root pin check in the `resume` action, placed after card validation and before `computeResumeState`. |

### FR-NODE-181 — Tracked .gitignore coverage of the agent-runtime worktree directory

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase2-parallel-lanes |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | orchestrator, isolation, worktree, gitignore |
| Risk | low |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md |

#### Requirement

The tracked `.gitignore` SHALL cover `.claude/worktrees/` as its own entry, so that the agent-runtime worktree directory a Profile A lane executes in is machine state `git clean -fd` may remove and `git add -A` can never stage. The coverage SHALL come from the tracked file rather than from `.git/info/exclude`, which is local and unshared and therefore absent on a fresh clone or a CI runner.

#### Rationale

Today the exclusion lives only in `.git/info/exclude:11`, a local unshared file, so the isolation Profile A that phase 2 introduces is broken on any fresh clone or CI runner. Phase 1 registered two entries — `kiwi/waves.jsonl` and `kiwi/orchestrator/` — under FR-NODE-106 and deferred this third one, because phase 1 creates no worktree to ignore (`frozen.isolation_profile` is the constant `none-serial`). This registration fixes this repository only; writing the entry into a consumer's own tracked `.gitignore` at Preflight P.6, inside the isolation probe, is separate phase-2 work.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `.gitignore` is a tracked file, and carries an entry covering `.claude/worktrees/` as its own non-comment, non-blank line rather than as text inside a comment.
- [x] AC-2: The entry covers the directory at any depth, matching the reach of the `**/.claude/worktrees/` line in `.git/info/exclude` it exists to replace; a nested `sub/.claude/worktrees/` is covered by the tracked file and not only by the local exclude.
- [x] AC-3: In a repository seeded with the tracked `.gitignore` alone and with no exclusion of its own — neither `.git/info/exclude` nor a global excludes file — a `.claude/worktrees/<id>/` checkout is reported by neither `git status --porcelain --untracked-files=all` nor `git add -A`, while a control file at the repository root is reported by both.
- [x] AC-4: The same repository with only this entry stripped from the copied `.gitignore` does report and does stage that checkout, so AC-3 cannot pass without the entry; the strip is asserted to have removed bytes.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/orchestrator/worktree-gitignore.fr-node-181.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4 | Four cases. AC-1 asserts `git ls-files -- .gitignore` returns the file — the tracked-versus-local-exclude distinction the requirement rests on — and separately filters comments and blanks before asserting the entry, so text inside a comment cannot satisfy it. AC-2/AC-3 seed a throwaway repository with the tracked file and one seed file, pin `core.excludesFile` to nothing so no global ignore on the running machine can stand in, assert the seeded `.git/info/exclude` carries no worktree rule, then create a lane checkout both at the root and nested one level down and assert `git status --porcelain --untracked-files=all` and `git add -A` report neither while a root control file is reported by both. AC-4 re-seeds with only this entry stripped (the strip throws if it removed no bytes) and asserts both checkouts ARE reported and ARE staged, so AC-3 cannot pass without the entry. Mutation-checked: narrowing the entry from `**/.claude/worktrees/` to a root-anchored one turns the depth case red with the nested checkout leaking into status, so the `**/` prefix is load-bearing rather than decorative. |

Measured at authoring time: `grep -an worktrees .gitignore` returns nothing; `.git/info/exclude:11` carries `**/.claude/worktrees/`. ### FR-NODE-182 — Deferred-mutation replay planner deduped on (tool, argsHash)

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase2-parallel-lanes |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

The orchestrator SHALL provide a pure planner `replayDeferredMutations(queue, dedupeIndex)` in `src/core/orchestrator/replay.ts` that plans the host-root replay of a lane's deferred SRS mutations. It SHALL compute `argsHash = sha1(canonicalJson(args))` with the tool name outside the hashed input, dedupe on the pair `(tool, argsHash)` against both the supplied index and the queue's own earlier entries, and return a `ReplayPlan` carrying every queue entry in input order marked `apply` or `skip-duplicate`, together with the index that results.

#### Rationale

05 §10.1 pins the output shape at `:4140-4142` and the signature at `:4069-4070`; §15.1 E18 (`:5473`) states the dedupe rule; RW-48 (`:7910`) persists the index at `replay-index.json` so `replay-deferred-mutations`'s `idempotent-by-key` recovery class (`journal-schema.ts:100`) has something to key against after a compaction. FR-FLOW-121 shipped the producer — `kiwi-coder --defer-srs-mutation` — with no consumer, and its shipped skill text says so; this is that consumer. The key is the PAIR and not the hash alone because `update_status` and `add_completed_work` share an `{id, target}` args shape (05 `:6526`), so hashing args alone would let one silently skip the other. The tool name stays OUTSIDE the hashed input because `kiwi-coder SKILL.md:569` already writes `args_hash: sha1(canonicalJson(args))`; including it would make every lookup miss and re-apply every lane's evidence and completed-work rows on resume (05 `:6518-6522`). A `dry_run: true` entry needs no semantics here and is deliberately given none: FR-FLOW-121 AC-7 refuses `--defer-srs-mutation` together with `--dry-run` at the producer, for exactly the reason that replaying such an entry is undefined, so the state is unreachable by construction rather than unhandled.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `replayDeferredMutations` is exported from `src/core/orchestrator/replay.ts`, takes exactly two arguments each annotated with a named exported type, and returns `ReplayPlan`, so a fixture is constructible from the declarations alone without reading the implementation.
- [x] AC-2: `argsHash` equals `sha1(canonicalJson(args))` and the tool name is not part of the hashed input: two entries carrying different `tool` values and identical `args` receive the same `argsHash`.
- [x] AC-3: The dedupe key is the pair `(tool, argsHash)`: an entry whose pair is already present in `dedupeIndex` is marked `skip-duplicate`, while an entry whose `argsHash` appears in the index only under a different tool is marked `apply`.
- [x] AC-4: Duplicates inside the queue itself are deduped as well: the first occurrence of a `(tool, argsHash)` pair is `apply` and every later occurrence of that same pair is `skip-duplicate`.
- [x] AC-5: `calls` is one entry per queue entry in input order with nothing dropped, and `indexAfter` holds, per tool, the sorted duplicate-free union of the incoming index with every `argsHash` marked `apply`; the `dedupeIndex` argument is not mutated.
- [x] AC-6: The planner is pure: identical inputs produce byte-identical output, and the module's static import closure reaches `node:crypto` and `./canonical-json.js` only, so it performs no filesystem, process, network or clock access.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/orchestrator/replay.fr-node-182.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6 | 24/24 pass, measured 2026-08-10 (npx vitest run --no-file-parallelism --testTimeout 30000). Cited by DESCRIBE BLOCK rather than by line, because line citations in this repository have gone stale on every edit that added an import. AC-1 asserts arity 2 and, through a TypeScript AST oracle, that both parameters and the return type are named types the module itself exports — an inline structural annotation is refused because a fixture author cannot import one; a floor assertion stops the check running over an empty exported-name set. AC-2 asserts the formula against an independently computed hash, asserts two different tools with identical args receive the SAME argsHash (the case that fails if the tool name is folded into the digest), asserts member-order independence, and pins one digest as a LITERAL — the other cases route through this repository's own canonicalJson and would stay green if its serialisation changed, while the queue is written by a skill, so the literal is what actually pins the bytes both sides must agree on. AC-3 covers all three quadrants of the pair key, including the decisive one where a hash present in the index under a DIFFERENT tool must still apply. AC-4 asserts the exact action sequence over a repeated pair and that a within-queue skip does not suppress a different tool sharing the args. AC-5 asserts one call per entry in order with nothing dropped, the sorted duplicate-free union, carry-forward of an untouched tool, that a skip adds no hash, that a duplicate in the INCOMING index is also collapsed, and that dedupeIndex is neither mutated nor aliased into the result. AC-6 asserts byte-identical output for identical input, an exact two-file import closure (replay.ts plus canonical-json.ts, the latter asserted to import nothing, so this is the whole closure and not a one-file approximation), and the absence of clock/env/process tokens. MUTATION-VERIFIED, seven mutations applied one at a time with the source restored byte-identically after each (sha1 checked): folding the tool name into the hashed input at the call site, keying on the hash alone, not recording an applied hash, dropping skipped entries from calls, aliasing the incoming arrays into the result, leaving the per-tool list unsorted, and emitting a constant hash — all seven turned the file red. Two defects were found this way rather than by inspection: the first sorted-union fixture was VACUOUS because its two hashes happened to arrive already ordered, so an implementation that never sorted passed it, and the case now carries an explicit precondition that insertion order differs from sorted order; and an independent audit found that a tool name colliding with Object.prototype silently vanished from indexAfter while its call was still reported apply, which broke the module's idempotent-by-key contract and is now fixed with a null-prototype result and asserted in both the write and read-back directions. |

#### Trace Links

| Type | Reference | Relation | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| requirement | FR-FLOW-121 | depends_on | FR-FLOW-121 is the producer: `kiwi-coder --defer-srs-mutation` records the four §0.12 mutations into the queue this planner reads, and its AC-7 (mutual exclusion with `--dry-run`) is what makes a `dry_run: true` entry unreachable here, so this planner needs no semantics for one. |

### FR-NODE-183 — Every package-relative runtime asset ships in the published tarball

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.7.0-verification-integrity |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [release readiness research](../research/17.release-readiness-and-verification-reproducibility.md) |

#### Requirement

Every file the runtime resolves relative to its own installed location — the `new URL("../../../<path>", import.meta.url)` idiom — SHALL be tracked in git and covered by a `package.json` `files` entry, so that the path exists on a consumer's machine. In particular `docs/.kiwi/hooks/pre-commit.mjs` and `docs/.kiwi/hooks/trace.mjs`, which `speckiwi init` copies into a target project, SHALL be present in the published package.

#### Rationale

Measured 2026-08-10: `src/core/bootstrap/init-project.ts:167` reads `docs/.kiwi/hooks/<name>` from the installed package, and `:186` falls back to `bundled ?? "#!/usr/bin/env node\nprocess.exit(0);\n"` when the read fails. `npm pack --dry-run --json` yields 796 files with zero `.kiwi` entries, and both runners are untracked because `.gitignore` line 28 (`.kiwi/`) matches `docs/.kiwi/` as well as the intended runtime `.kiwi/` session directory. So on every consumer machine the fallback fires and `init` installs a no-op hook. `IR-CLI-047` requires `init` to install the governance hooks and `FR-NODE-054` requires an installed script AT that exact path; both are contradicted for every user. The trace hook has no alternative path in `src/`; the pre-commit gate is partly mitigated by the shipped CI command `vibe-gate check` (`src/cli/commands/read.ts:806-811`), whose own comment says it exists because local hooks can be bypassed. Three of the four package-relative reads — `docs/rule/*` twice and `skills/*` in `doctor.ts:290-294` — ARE covered by `files`, which is what makes the fourth an oversight rather than a decision. The failure is silent by construction: a `??` fallback substitutes a working-looking stub, so nothing fails and nothing warns. That is the same failure mode this target names — prose asserts a fact, reality moves, nothing re-reads the assertion.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A test derives the package-relative asset paths from `src/` by scanning for the `new URL("../../../<path>", import.meta.url)` idiom rather than hard-coding a list, so a newly added bundled asset is covered automatically.
- [x] AC-2: For each derived path that resolves outside `dist/`, the test asserts the file exists on disk, is tracked by git, and is covered by a `package.json` `files` entry.
- [x] AC-3: The hook runners are tracked and covered by `files` at the repository root, AND the fixture copies under `test/fixtures/workspaces/*/docs/.kiwi/hooks/` are tracked too, because the hook tests copy that fixture into a temp project and execute the runner from it. The ignore rule releases `docs/.kiwi/hooks/` at any depth while still excluding the runtime `.kiwi/` session directory and `docs/.kiwi/trace/` at every depth.
- [x] AC-4: Reverting any one of the three changes — the `.gitignore` narrowing, the git tracking, or the `files` entry — turns the test red.
- [x] AC-5: `test/core/mutation/fr-node-039-trace-hook.test.ts` and `fr-node-040-pre-commit-hook.test.ts` pass on a clean checkout of the recorded commit — measured in an isolated `core.autocrlf=false` worktree with `npm run build` run first, which is what CI does — not only in a working tree that happens to hold the ignored files.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/release/bundled-asset-parity.fr-node-183.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4 | 6/6 pass, measured 2026-08-10 in the main working tree at commit-in-progress (npx vitest run --no-file-parallelism --testTimeout 30000). AC-1: the asset list is derived by scanning `src/` for the `new URL("../../../<path>", import.meta.url)` idiom, with a floor assertion so a regex that stopped matching cannot leave the file vacuously green, plus a self-check that the scan finds the three paths that were already correct (`docs/rule`, `skills/codex`, `skills/claude`) — that self-check caught a real bug in the scanner during authoring, which stripped a trailing path segment unconditionally and turned `skills/codex` into `skills`. AC-2 asserts, for every derived directory, that EVERY file under it is covered by a `package.json` `files` entry; per-file rather than per-directory because `files` legitimately names a directory (`skills/codex`) or its members one by one (`docs/rule/SRS-…md`), and the per-file rule accepts both while still catching a directory that ships only some members. AC-3 asserts both runners exist, are tracked, are packaged, that `docs/.kiwi/hooks/` is not ignored so a future runner can still be added, that the runtime `.kiwi/` session directory is still ignored, and that the runner content is the real gate rather than the two-line stub. DECISIVE ARTIFACT MEASUREMENT, not just a green test: `npm pack --dry-run --json` went from 796 files with zero `.kiwi` entries to 798 files containing `docs/.kiwi/hooks/pre-commit.mjs` and `docs/.kiwi/hooks/trace.mjs`. AC-4 MUTATION-VERIFIED, three mutations one at a time with the source restored after each: removing the `files` entry → 2 failed; `git rm --cached` on both runners → 1 failed; reverting the `.gitignore` narrowing → 1 failed. The third was GREEN until a dedicated case was added, because once a file is tracked `.gitignore` no longer affects it, so reverting the rule left the two existing runners tracked and every other assertion satisfied; what it actually breaks is the NEXT runner, which `git add` would silently skip, and `git check-ignore --no-index` is the only way to observe the rule rather than the index. AC-4 was false as written until that case existed. |
| VE-2 | test | test/core/mutation/fr-node-040-pre-commit-hook.test.ts | AC-3, AC-5 | 17/17 pass together with test/core/mutation/fr-node-039-trace-hook.test.ts, measured 2026-08-10 IN A CLEAN CHECKOUT of commit a8cd318 — an isolated worktree created with `git -c core.autocrlf=false worktree add --detach`, `npm run build` run first, then vitest. The environment is stated because it is the whole point: these two files pass in any working tree that happens to hold the ignored runners, and passed there throughout, while failing on every clean checkout. Measured before the fix at the same commit family: `cannot find module …\\docs\\.kiwi\\hooks\\pre-commit.mjs`. The first fix attempt did NOT move this number — a full CI-equivalent run after tracking the ROOT runners still showed the same 17 red, because `.gitignore` patterns containing a slash are anchored to the file's own directory, so `docs/.kiwi/` released only the repository root while `test/fixtures/workspaces/valid-basic/docs/.kiwi/hooks/` stayed ignored, and `copyFixtureWorkspace("valid-basic")` copies that fixture into a temp project and executes the runner from it. Rewriting the rule with `**/` released both depths; the four-path check confirms the runtime `.kiwi/` session directory and `docs/.kiwi/trace/` remain ignored at every depth. LIMITATION: this row's measurement used a junctioned `node_modules` rather than `npm ci`, so it verifies file presence and hook execution, not dependency resolution from a cold install. |

### FR-NODE-184 — Hermeticity guard classifies sentinels against a pre-suite baseline

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.7.0-verification-integrity |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | hermeticity, test-harness, data-loss |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/17.release-readiness-and-verification-reproducibility.md |

#### Requirement

The test hermeticity guard SHALL classify each sentinel path by comparing the working tree against a baseline captured before the suite starts, and SHALL NOT treat presence alone as proof that the current test created the path. It SHALL delete only paths absent from the baseline, SHALL report but never delete paths the baseline already contained, and SHALL delete nothing when a baseline cannot be obtained. A sentinel naming a directory SHALL be compared as a set of entries, so a file created inside a directory the baseline already contained is still classified as added.

#### Rationale

Presence-only detection is false in the one checkout that matters most: this repository dogfoods its own product, so .mcp.json, docs/spec/steps, .claude/skills and .codex/hooks.json are legitimate `speckiwi init` output that is always present and never tracked. Measured 2026-08-11: a routine `vitest run test/skills` deleted all four (49 untracked files, unrecoverable by git) and named kiwi-wave-continuity-r3-content.test.ts as the offender; running kiwi-tdd-content.test.ts first moved the accusation to that file instead. Both are pure content tests that only read SKILL.md and cannot write .mcp.json, so the accusation is false and the deletion is real. The guard both destroys data and corrupts its own diagnosis. Deleting a path the baseline contained can never be correct, because the guard holds no copy to restore.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Given a baseline captured while a sentinel path already exists, a test that creates nothing is classified as clean: no added and no modified entries.
- [x] AC-2: A sentinel path absent from the baseline and present afterwards is classified as added.
- [x] AC-3: A file created inside a sentinel directory that the baseline already contained is classified as added, identified by its path within that directory.
- [x] AC-4: Cleanup removes only added paths; every baseline path survives byte-identical, verified by digest.
- [x] AC-5: A baseline path whose content changed is classified as modified, is reported, and is NOT deleted.
- [x] AC-6: When no baseline is available the guard reports what it found and deletes nothing.
- [x] AC-7: The acceptance criteria above are exercised by driving the audit function directly rather than by scanning source text for the behaviour.

### FR-NODE-185 — Deferred-mutation replay admission and checkpoint reduction

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase2-parallel-lanes |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | orchestrator, lane, replay, mutation, security |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md |

#### Requirement

Before any planned replay call is applied at the host root, the orchestrator SHALL admit it only when its tool is one of the four mutations `kiwi-coder` may defer — `add_trace_link`, `add_verification_evidence`, `update_status`, `add_completed_work`. Every other tool SHALL be refused with the reason `tool-not-deferrable`, and admission SHALL NOT be disableable by an argument. A call carrying a `target` that differs from the run's frozen target SHALL be refused as `target-not-frozen`. Applying SHALL be reduced from an append-only record of one line per attempted call, so that a run interrupted after N applications resumes with exactly the remaining calls and re-applies none of the N.

#### Rationale

The deferred-mutation queue is written by the lane, and the planner deliberately reads only `tool` and `args` from it. Nothing between the lane and the host root compares that tool against anything, so a lane that records `set_active_target` or `supersede_requirement` has them planned as `apply` exactly like the four legitimate ones — and replay executes at the host root with full authority. Charter C1 keeps SRS mutation out of the lane precisely so the lane cannot reach the host's `docs/spec/`; replaying an arbitrary tool name hands that reach back. The prior design (05 R20) describes the queue-and-replay decomposition without an admission step, so the gap is in the design, not only in the code. Checkpointing is the second half: `add_completed_work` and `add_verification_evidence` append, so re-applying them after a crash duplicates rows, and the index alone cannot say which of a plan's calls had already run when the process died.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A planned call whose tool is one of the four deferrable mutations is admitted.
- [x] AC-2: A planned call whose tool is anything else — including `set_active_target`, `supersede_requirement` and `__proto__` — is refused with reason `tool-not-deferrable`, and refusal carries the call's index in the plan.
- [x] AC-3: Admission takes no argument that widens the allowed set; the set is a module constant.
- [x] AC-4: A call whose args carry a `target` different from the run's frozen target is refused with reason `target-not-frozen`; a call carrying no target is admitted on that axis.
- [x] AC-5: A `skip-duplicate` call is never admitted for application, because the plan already accounts for it.
- [x] AC-6: Given a plan and an append-only record of the calls already attempted, reduction returns the calls still to apply, and that set excludes every call the record shows succeeded.
- [x] AC-7: A call the record shows failed is reported as failed and is not silently retried by the same reduction.
- [x] AC-8: Reduction of an empty record returns every admitted call, and reduction of a complete record returns none.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/orchestrator/replay-admission.fr-node-185.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6, AC-7, AC-8 | red: module absent, 0 tests collected; green 9 passed. Full suite 382 files / 5136 passed / 4 skipped / 0 failed; typecheck, typecheck:test and lint exit 0. AC-3 is asserted behaviourally — a third argument naming set_active_target is passed and the call is still refused. |

#### Trace Links

| Type | Reference | Relation | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Code | src/core/orchestrator/replay-admission.ts | implements | DEFERRABLE_MUTATION_TOOLS module constant, admitReplayCalls, reduceReplayOutcomes |
| Requirement | FR-NODE-182 | extends | The planner decides what replay WOULD do; this decides what it MAY do. FR-NODE-182 is unchanged — it reads only tool and args by design, and admission is the step that was missing between it and the host root. |

### FR-NODE-186 — Worktree topology classification and the lane-role run-root gate

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase2-parallel-lanes |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | orchestrator, lane, worktree, run-root, isolation |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/16.wave-master-per-wave-worktree-subagent-research.md |

#### Requirement

The orchestrator SHALL classify the relation between the MCP workspace root and a candidate git root into exactly one of `same-root`, `linked-worktree`, `unregistered-worktree`, `foreign-repo` and `not-a-repo`, deciding it from the git common directory the two roots resolve to and from the worktrees that common directory registers, both obtained through injected probes. A run root declared to hold the `lane` role SHALL be accepted only when it classifies as `linked-worktree`, its lane identifier appears in the run's frozen lane plan, and that lane's write set names no path under `docs/spec/`; each failure SHALL be refused with its own reason. A run root declared to hold the `host` role SHALL additionally be refused as `host-root-is-a-linked-worktree` when it is itself a linked worktree, because a host session rooted in a worktree writes SRS into a checkout that must still be merged. The role SHALL be declared by the caller and SHALL NOT be inferred from the roots, because a gate that infers it cannot tell a planned lane from a session that wandered.

#### Rationale

The existing run-root gate compares two roots for equality, so it refuses every worktree — including the ones the run itself created and planned. That is why worktree isolation is unreachable today: the only mechanism that could grant it is indistinguishable, to the gate, from the accident the gate exists to catch. Measured: every linked worktree of one repository reports the same `--git-common-dir` while reporting a different `--show-toplevel`, so the common directory is a discriminator the caller does not choose. Membership in the frozen lane plan is what separates a planned lane from a merely well-formed worktree; without it, any worktree of the repository would pass, and the gate would be back to trusting the caller. The role is declared rather than inferred for the reason FR-NODE-178 already established: a value the tool derives from the same caller it is checking cannot corroborate that caller.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Two roots that normalise equal classify as `same-root` whatever the probes report.
- [x] AC-2: A git root whose common directory equals the MCP root's, whose top level differs, and which the common directory registers, classifies as `linked-worktree`.
- [x] AC-3: A git root whose common directory equals the MCP root's but which that common directory does not register classifies as `unregistered-worktree`.
- [x] AC-4: A git root in a different repository classifies as `foreign-repo`, and a path in no repository classifies as `not-a-repo`.
- [x] AC-5: A lane-role root that classifies as anything other than `linked-worktree` is refused, and the reason names which of the four non-lane classifications it was.
- [x] AC-6: A lane-role root whose lane identifier is absent from the frozen lane plan is refused as `lane-id-not-in-plan`, even when the root classifies as `linked-worktree`.
- [x] AC-7: A lane-role root whose lane plan entry has a write set naming any path under `docs/spec/` is refused as `lane-write-set-touches-srs`.
- [x] AC-8: A host-role root that is itself a linked worktree is refused as `host-root-is-a-linked-worktree`; a host-role root that is a repository top level and not a linked worktree passes.
- [x] AC-9: The role is a parameter; passing lane roots under the host role and host roots under the lane role are both refused, so the gate cannot be satisfied by relabelling.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

Full suite 383 files / 5149 passed / 4 skipped / 0 failed; typecheck, typecheck:test and lint exit 0. The AC-8 order-independence case is load-bearing: the first implementation read `git worktree list` positionally and that case turns it red. |

| Requirement | FR-NODE-178 | extends | FR-NODE-178 corroborates the git root against the repository that path names; this adds the common directory as a second corroborator so a planned lane worktree is distinguishable from a wandered session. FR-NODE-178's own conditions are unchanged and its module is untouched. |

### FR-NODE-187 — MCP registration points a self-hosting checkout at its own build

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.7.0-verification-integrity |
| Status | planned |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | mcp, bootstrap, self-host, version-drift |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/17.release-readiness-and-verification-reproducibility.md |

#### Requirement

When the project being registered is the SpecKiwi package itself — its `package.json` names `speckiwi` and it carries `bin/speckiwi` — `speckiwi init` SHALL write an `.mcp.json` launcher that runs that checkout's own entrypoint rather than one resolved from a registry or a global install. Every other project SHALL keep the existing launcher. The detection SHALL require both signals, so a project that merely names itself `speckiwi` is not pointed at an entrypoint it does not have.

#### Rationale

The launcher this repository carries was written by the product itself and reads `npx -y speckiwi mcp`, which resolves to whatever is installed globally. Measured 2026-08-11: the session's MCP server reported package version 2.7.1 while the checkout was 2.7.2, and the tool list was missing exactly the one tool the newer build adds. A repository that dogfoods its own product is the one place where the registry copy is guaranteed to be the wrong one — the whole point of working in the checkout is to run what the checkout builds. Every agent-facing claim measured through that server describes a build that is not the one under change, which is a verification-integrity defect rather than a convenience gap. Both signals are required because the name alone is satisfied by a fork or a scratch directory that has no built entrypoint to launch.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A root whose package.json names `speckiwi` and which has `bin/speckiwi` is registered with a launcher that runs that file from the checkout.
- [x] AC-2: A root that names `speckiwi` but has no `bin/speckiwi` keeps the ordinary launcher, because the detected entrypoint does not exist.
- [x] AC-3: A root whose package.json names anything else keeps the ordinary launcher, even when `bin/speckiwi` happens to exist.
- [x] AC-4: A root with no readable package.json keeps the ordinary launcher rather than failing registration.
- [x] AC-5: The existing idempotence is unchanged: an `.mcp.json` that already carries a `speckiwi` server key is still skipped, and other servers and top-level keys are still preserved.
- [x] AC-6: The warning text that names the entry for manual registration names the same launcher the detection selected.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/bootstrap/mcp-registration-self-host.fr-node-187.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6 | red 3 failed / 7 (the three self-host paths), then green 7 passed; test/core/bootstrap 18 files / 171 passed, so the pre-existing FR-NODE-067 registration cases are unchanged. Both detection signals were mutated and each turns the suite red on its own: dropping the bin/speckiwi check fails AC-2, dropping the package-name check fails AC-3. A first attempt at the bin mutation reported green because the replacement had not applied; the count of `access` call sites is now asserted before the run. |
